All publicly owned and maintained works used to supply the city and users with water
under the metered rates shall be known as the "water utility."
(Code 1981, § 8-1-1; Code 1993, § 14.04.010; Code 2009, § 14.04.010; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 2, 1-27-2015)
The water utility and all things pertaining thereto shall be operated and administered
by the department of public works and natural resources.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-2; Code 1993, § 14.04.020; Code 2009, § 14.04.020; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 2; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 3, 1-27-2015)
The water utility shall be headed by the director of public works and natural resources
under the general direction of the city manager, who may make such rules and regulations
as necessary to carry out the intent of this chapter. The director of public works
and natural resources shall be responsible for the management, operation and maintenance
of the water utility and for such other duties as may be delegated to the director
by the city manager. The director of public works and natural resources shall have
charge of all reservoirs, sedimentation basins, filters, pumps, intakes, pipelines
and all appliances connected therewith, as well as all water mains, taps, the public
portion of service pipes in the public right-of-way or easements, water meters, fire
hydrants and all connections with water mains, and all machinery and appurtenances
of the water utility, and the director shall carefully maintain and operate the same.
The director shall have direction of the laying of all water mains, tapping of mains,
installing of service pipes or other connections with the water mains and the regulation
of the water supply to all fire hydrants and to all users of water. The director shall
have charge and be responsible for all pipes, fixtures, plumbing material and other
apparatus and appliances purchased by the city and kept and furnished to water consumers
or used in making connection with the water mains, and shall keep account of all such
material and the manner in which the same is used or disposed of.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-3; Code 1993, § 14.04.030; Code 2009, § 14.04.030; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 3; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 4, 1-27-2015)
The director of public works and natural resources shall report to the city manager
as required, and the city manager shall keep the city council advised of the operating
and financial condition and future needs of the water department and shall prepare
and submit each month to the city council a report covering the activities of the
water utility, including a statement of revenues and expenditures for the preceding
month.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-4; Code 1993, § 14.04.040; Code 2009, § 14.04.040; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 4; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 5, 1-27-2015)
The rates to be charged for water service shall be as provided at sections 14.04.070 through 14.04.150 , and are effective as of the dates shown in each section.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-16; Code 1993, § 14.04.050; Code 2009, § 14.04.050; Ord. No. O-80-94,
§ 3; Ord. No. O-82-94, § 1; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 6, 1-27-2015)
There shall be assessed and charged for the use of water measured through a meter,
per month, from any of the water mains of the city, rates and rentals as set forth
at sections 14.04.070 through 14.04.150 .
(Code 1981, § 8-1-16(B); Code 1993, § 14.04.070; Code 2009, § 14.04.070; Ord. No.
O-80-94, § 1; Ord. No. O-82-94, § 1; Ord. No. O-2000-52, § 1)
_____

Large commercial customers are defined by meter size and water usage characteristics.
They must have water demands that require a four-inch or larger water meter, continuous
flow greater than 160 gallons per minute, and nominal peaking. For large commercial
metered service, there shall be charged for consumption, as measured in 100-gallon
increments, a rate to be negotiated to properly recover the cost of accommodating
the specific usage of the large commercial customer.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.110; Code 2009, § 14.04.110; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 5; Ord. No. O-2000-52,
§ 5; Ord. No. O-2008-78, § 4; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 11, 1-27-2015)

A.
The city council may enter into a special water service contract with customers who
do not fall within the customer user classifications of sections 14.04.070 through 14.04.150 . Rates for water contracts are calculated to recover the cost of service.
B.
The city entered into an intergovernmental agreement (treated water service) with
the Town of Lyons, dated July 21, 2003, which was supplemented by an agreement dated
December 14, 2004 and an agreement dated December 6, 2010, by which the city provides
treated water to the Town of Lyons (collectively, the water service agreement). The
water service agreement provides that the city may revise the rates. The following
rate schedule shall apply to the Town of Lyons, for water by volume, measured in 100-gallon
increments:

| 2025 (per 1,000 gallons) | 2026 (per 1,000 gallons) | 2027 (per 1,000 gallons)
Lyons Contract Rate | $3.03 | $3.31 | $3.61

O-80-94, § 1; Ord. No. O-81-66, § 5; Ord. No. O-82-94, § 1; Ord. No. O-2000-52, §
9; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 14, 1-27-2015; Ord. No. O-2019-64 , § 7, 12-3-2019; Ord. No. O-2024-76 , § 1, 10-22-2024)
_____
The rate for bulk loading stations shall be the same as small commercial metered service
as set forth in section 14.04.100 .
(Code 1993, § 14.04.140; Code 2009, § 14.04.140; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 7; Ord. No. O-2000-52,
§ 10)
The rate for construction water shall be the same as small commercial metered service
as set forth in section 14.04.100 .
(Code 1993, § 14.04.150; Code 2009, § 14.04.150; Ord. No. O-2000-52, § 11; Ord. No.
O-2009-34, § 2, 7-14-2009)
The city shall issue no permit nor enter any agreement to provide water under sections 14.04.130 , 14.04.140 , or 14.04.150 that would provide water for use in oil and gas operations. Any permit or agreement
issued for service under any of these sections shall contain provisions prohibiting
the use of water from the city in oil and gas operations, and provisions requiring
accounting of such water to prevent such use. This section shall not apply to existing
permits and agreements but shall apply to any extension thereof.
(Code 2009, § 14.04.155; Ord. No. O-2016-41, § 2, 6-14-2016)
A.
Unless otherwise noted, the following charges shall be made for services inside and
outside the city limits by the city, relative to providing new or larger size water
service or changing customer user classification:
1.
Residential.
a.
Inside city. All new single-family and duplex residential services shall pay a base fee according
to meter size plus charges per square foot of gross lot size, based on the following
schedule:

3.
Large commercial. Large commercial customers are defined by meter size and water usage characteristics.
They must have water demands that require a four-inch or larger water meter, continuous
flow greater than 160 gallons per minute, and nominal peaking. The system development
fee is negotiated based on individual usage characteristics.
4.
Multifamily. All multifamily services shall pay a per unit fee, which is cumulative, based on
the following schedule:

Fee Block | 2024
First 4 units | $3,380.00
Next 8 units | $2,215.00
Next 22 units | $1,421.00
Next 29 units | $1,293.00
Beginning with 64th unit | $679.00

5.
Irrigation. All water services installed for outdoor irrigation purposes and providing no domestic
water or connection to the wastewater collection system shall pay system development
fees as follows:

Water Meter Size (inches) | 2024
⅝ × ¾ | $18,170.00
¾ | $27,260.00

1½ | $90,850.00
2 | $145,360.00

6.
Vance Brand Municipal Airport master meter. The Vance Brand Municipal Airport master meter system development fee shall be the
same as for a five-eighths-inch by three-fourths-inch small commercial meter. The
rated capacity of a meter will be set according to the most recent version of the
meter standards published by American Water Works Association and the corresponding
fixture units as determined in the latest edition of the International Plumbing Code.
Each customer on the Vance Brand Municipal Airport shall pay a proportionate share
of the system development fee according to the customer's number of fixture units.
7.
Small-lot affordable residential.
a.
Applicability. This subsection A.7 applies as may be requested for the provision of new water service
to single-family and duplex residential services meeting the following criteria:
i.
The average area of the lots for which such request is made does not exceed 3,500
square feet;
ii.
Separate irrigation services to be operated by a homeowners' association or similar
body are also purchased under subsection A.5 of this section, for all outdoor water
service for the applicable lots and any other outside areas included in the final
plat of the development;
iii.
The lots are part of a development including affordable housing receiving city incentives
under a written agreement with the city pursuant to chapter 4.79 ; or the final plat or final PUD plan for the development requires some of the lots
to be either affordable rental housing or affordable owner housing as defined in section 15.10.020 ; and
iv.
Such an instrument requires at least 20 percent of the total units in the development
to qualify as either affordable rental housing or affordable owner housing as defined
in section 15.10.020 .
b.
Regulations.
i.
No water provided via the single-family services may be used for outside irrigation.
ii.
Upon a determination by the general manager of public works and natural resources
that a single-family service has been put to an outside use, or that the homeowners'
association or similar body responsible for outdoor irrigation has dissolved or is
neglectful of any responsibility to irrigate, the owner of the service shall pay the
per-square-foot component of the pertinent fee described in subsection A.1 of this
section. Failure to make this payment immediately may result in the discontinuance
of service under the procedures of section 14.20.150 , and the city may place a lien upon the owner's premises to ensure payment. The city
may collect such liens and liabilities by an action at law or equity to enforce the
lien. In addition to an action at law or equity to enforce the lien, all fees may
be certified by the clerk to the treasurer of Boulder or Weld County to be collected
and paid over by the treasurer of Boulder or Weld County in the same manner as taxes.
iii.
The final plat for the development must include easement rights for the homeowners'
association or similar body over the applicable lots for purposes of installing, maintaining,
replacing, and repairing the irrigation system.
c.
Fees.
i.
The fees for these services shall be the same as in subsection A.1 of this section,
except that the per-square-foot component of the fee shall not be required, provided
that separate irrigation services are purchased contemporaneously.
ii.
All other applicable fees and charges, including the surcharge described in section 14.04.210 , remain in effect.
iii.
No adjustment for sewer use charges shall be available under section 14.08.645 .
8.
Small multifamily.
a.
Applicability. This subsection A.8 applies as may be requested for the redevelopment of single-family
residential services to multifamily services serving up to five units, meeting the
following criteria:
i.
The redevelopment shall not require a replat of the single-family residential lot,
other than corrections to any recorded plat; and
ii.
The existing single-family residential lot size shall not be increased or subdivided
into additional lots.
b.
Fees.
i.
The fees per multifamily unit outlined in subsections A.4 of this section and sections
14.04.210.A.4, and 14.08.610.A.4 apply to each unit to be constructed in excess of
existing dwelling units.
ii.
As of the date of the certificate of occupancy for the first redeveloped unit, the
service will be subject to a multifamily rate for both water and sewer.
9.
Effective date of fees. The fees set forth above for 2024 shall become effective as of the effective date
of the ordinance from which this section is derived. The city manager or designee
shall adjust the fees set forth in 2024 annually in the same proportion as changes
in construction costs, according to the "Engineering News Record 20-City Construction
Cost Index."
B.
That portion of the system development fee required for debt service attributable
to expansion of the system and new regulatory requirements shall be deposited to the
water fund. The remaining portion, including any interest earned, shall be deposited
in the water construction fund and restricted solely to expenditures related to improvements
and expansion of the water system.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.160; Code 2009, § 14.04.160; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 8; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 7; Ord. No. O-95-109, § 1; Ord. No. O-96-43, § 2; Ord. No. O-99-10, § 6; Ord. No.
O-2000-54, § 1; Ord. No. O-2006-76, § 2; Ord. No. O-2008-78, § 7; Ord. No. O-2009-34,
§ 3, 7-14-2009; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 15, 1-27-2015; Ord. No. O-2017-79, § 2, 12-12-2017; Ord. No. O-2021-37 , § 2, 7-27-2021; Ord. No. O-2023-65B , § 2, 11-28-2023)
A.
The size of water service shall be determined by the chief building official based
upon water main size, connected fixtures, equipment devices, area irrigated, and available
water pressure. The city shall base the meter size upon the maximum safe operating
capacity of the meter, according to the most recent version of the meter standards
published by American Water Works Association, and the peak usage as determined from
the adopted plumbing code.
B.
For small commercial and mixed-use service, the city shall base the system development
fee upon the size of one meter necessary to provide the anticipated peak usage, less
fire protection, downstream of the meter. The city shall not allow the payment of
fees for smaller multiple meters in lieu of the one single meter fee.
C.
For irrigation service, the city shall base the system development fee upon the size
of one meter necessary to provide the anticipated peak usage downstream of the meter.
The city shall not allow the payment of fees for smaller multiple meters in lieu of
the one single meter fee.
D.
For multifamily service:
i.
The city shall base the system development fee upon the total number of units proposed
for the entire project if the entire fee for all units in the project is paid with
the initial certificate of occupancy for the construction of the first unit of the
project. If the developer does not choose to pay the system development fee for the
entire project initially, then the fee will be based upon the number of units for
which the certificate of occupancy is being issued.
ii.
The chief building official shall confer with the director of public works and natural
resources, or the director's designee, concerning the sizing of all meters and service
lines for multifamily development.
iii.
At least one meter shall be set per building for all multifamily projects. If a separate
service line and meter are set for each individual townhome unit, each service shall
pay the single-family system development fee.
iv.
Irrigation service for common areas shall be metered separately and shall be subject
to the irrigation system development fee, except for facilities redeveloped under
section 14.04.160.A.8.
v.
All facilities associated with a multifamily complex that are not residential units
or irrigation systems shall have a separate meter set and be charged the small commercial
fees as set forth in sections 14.04.160.A.2, 14.04.210.A.2 and 14.08.610.A.2.
E.
The chief building official shall confer with the director of public works and natural
resources, or the director's designee, concerning the sizing of meters and service
lines for all small commercial, mixed use and irrigation user classifications. Where
the meter size is smaller or larger than the service line size, the water service
size shall be considered equal to the water meter size, and there shall be installed
adjacent to and upstream of such meter a pipe of the same diameter and 20 times that
diameter in length. If, for any reason, the water service size is inadequate to satisfy
the water demand, the service line, including tap and/or meter, shall be increased
at the expense of the owner to the size as determined above, and the owner shall pay
the difference in cost entailed on the new basis, including meter costs and system
development fees as applicable.
F.
The city will not refund any previously paid system development fees on existing services
should the owner, for any reason, decide to reduce the size or number of meters or
taps, or change the customer user classification for which the system development
fee was originally paid.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-16(C); Code 1993, § 14.04.170; Code 2009, § 14.04.170; Ord. No.
O-79-83, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-6, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-94, § 1; Ord. No. O-82-94, § 2;
Ord. No. O-93-33, § 9; Ord. No. O-96-43, § 3; Ord. No. O-99-10, § 6; Ord. No. O-2000-54,
§ 3; Ord. No. O-2006-76, § 3; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 16, 1-27-2015; Ord. No. O-2019-18 , § 3, 3-19-2019; Ord. No. O-2021-37 , § 3, 7-27-2021)
Each and every water service shall be separately metered, and the system development
fee shall be charged according to schedules by customer user classification as set
forth in section 14.04.160 . At Vance Brand Municipal Airport, notwithstanding that system development fees are
charged by master meter under section 14.04.160.A.6, charges for consumption under section 14.04.100 and for monthly service under section 14.04.120 shall be assessed based on separate meters for each and every water service.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-16(C); Code 1993, § 14.04.180; Code 2009, § 14.04.180; Ord. No.
O-79-83, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-6, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-94, § 1; Ord. No. O-82-94, § 2;
Ord. No. O-93-33, § 10; Ord. No. O-96-43, § 4; Ord. No. O-2000-54, § 4; Ord. No. O-2015-48,
§ 2, 10-13-2015)
Any change in the customer user classification will require payment of the difference
in the system development fees at time of approval of such change. The city will not
refund any previously paid system development fees should the owner, for any reason,
decide to change the customer user classification for any property.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.185; Code 2009, § 14.04.185; Ord. No. O-93-33, § 11)
The owner or developer shall pay the system development fee and surcharge on water
fees for each water tap in accordance with Code sections 14.04.160 and 14.04.210 respectively and also in accordance with Code chapters 4.72 and 4.78 .
(Code 1981, § 8-1-16(C)(2); Code 1993, § 14.04.190; Code 2009, § 14.04.190; Ord. No.
O-79-83, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-6, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-94, § 1; Ord. No. O-82-94, § 2;
Ord. No. O-93-33, § 12; Ord. No. O-2019-18 , § 4, 3-19-2019)
In the determination of the system development fee to be charged, there shall be taken
into consideration the use for which the proposed tap is to be made and, in such cases
as where oversized taps and service lines are required for fire-prevention devices
only, the system development fee shall be based on the tap size required to adequately
serve the nonfire-prevention uses for the building for which the tap is purchased.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-16(C)(1); Code 1993, § 14.04.200; Code 2009, § 14.04.200; Ord. No.
O-79-83, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-6, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-94, § 1; Ord. No. O-82-94, § 2;
Ord. No. O-93-33, § 13)
A.
There is established a surcharge, as set forth below, which shall be an additional
charge applicable to all system development fees as set forth in this chapter, such
surcharge to be due and payable in conjunction with the underlying fees. The proceeds
of any such surcharge collected shall be restricted to and designated for the satisfaction
of costs associated with the Windy Gap project as they become due and payable by the
city.

Residential.

3.
Large commercial. Large commercial customers are defined by meter size and water usage characteristics.
They must have water demands that require a four-inch or larger water meter, continuous
flow greater than 160 gallons per minute, and nominal peaking. The surcharge for large
commercial customers is negotiated based on individual water usage characteristics.
4.
Multifamily. All multifamily services shall pay a per unit fee, which is cumulative, based on
the following schedule:

6.
Vance Brand Municipal Airport surcharge. The surcharge shall also apply to the system development fees at the Vance Brand
Municipal Airport. The city shall use the following criteria in determining the surcharge
at the Vance Brand Municipal Airport:
a.
The Vance Brand Municipal Airport master meter system development fee shall be the
same as for a five-eighths-inch by three-fourths-inch small commercial meter;
b.
The rated capacity of a five-eighths-inch by three-fourths-inch meter will be set
according to the meter standards published by American Water Works Association and
the corresponding fixture units as determined in the latest edition of the International
Plumbing Code; and
c.
Each customer on the Vance Brand Municipal Airport shall pay a proportionate share
of the system development fee according to the customer's number of fixture units.
7.
Effective date of surcharges. The surcharges set forth above shall become effective on January 1, 2022. The surcharges
set forth in this section shall expire on December 31, 2027.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.210; Code 2009, § 14.04.210; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 9; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 14; Ord. No. O-95-14, § 1; Ord. No. O-95-109, § 3; Ord. No. O-96-43, § 5; Ord. No.
O-99-10, § 6; Ord. No. O-2000-54, § 5; Ord. No. O-2006-76, § 4; Ord. No. O-2008-78,
§ 8; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 17, 1-27-2015; Ord. No. O-2017-83, § 2, 12-19-2017; Ord.
No. O-2018-44 , § 2, 10-23-2018; Ord. No. O-2021-48 , § 2, 9-28-2021; Ord. No. O-2022-52 , § 2, 11-29-2022)
A.
Water meters. Water meters are required on all water services. All installations must have the
meter placed so that water used by that property is measured.
1.
Installation and costs. Water meters shall be installed at the expense of the property owner for all new
water users served by the city water utility, including all new structures. The applicant
for a new building permit shall purchase from the city the required water meter at
the time of the issuance of such permit.
2.
Ownership. Although initially paid for by the building permit applicant, all meters shall be
furnished by and be the property of the city.
3.
Maintenance. Meters shall be maintained by the city except in the event of neglect or willful
abuse by the user. The cost of any repair required because of neglect or willful abuse
shall be billed to the user, as set forth in section 14.04.530 .
4.
Location.
a.
Single-family and duplex residences. Water meters shall be installed in pits or vaults, as described in section A.5 below.
For any development activity requiring a major, minor, or administrative development
application as defined in section 15.02.020 , meters located inside the existing building shall be abandoned or removed and a
new meter pit shall be installed by the property owner per the requirements of section
A.5 below.
b.
Commercial, industrial, multifamily, mixed use, and special customer classifications. Water meters may be installed inside the building or outside in a pit or vault. If
the location of an inside meter impedes the city's ability to perform routine maintenance
activities, the property owner shall relocate the meter to an outside meter pit or
vault upon notification by the city.
5.
Water meter pits, vaults, and covers.
a.
Installation and costs. All meter pits and vaults must be constructed pursuant to the city design standards
and construction specifications. Water meter pits and vaults shall be purchased by
the property owner from third-party vendors. At the discretion of the city manager
or designee, the city may install meter pits and vaults in accordance with section 14.04.300 . If the city performs the installation, the city shall restore damage to private
property, if any, as near as feasible to the condition pre-existing the installation,
except as necessary to accommodate the installation.
b.
Ownership. Meter pits and vaults shall be the property of the property owner. Meter pits installed
by the city as described in this section A.5 will become the property of the property
owner after the work has been completed.
c.
Maintenance. Meter pits and vaults shall be owned and maintained by the property owner. Access
to meter pits and vaults shall not be impaired by landscaping, fences, or other obstructions.
d.
Location.
i.
Single-family and duplex residences. Water meter pits or vaults shall be installed outside the residence, as close to
the street as practical.
ii.
Commercial, industrial, multifamily, mixed use, and special customer user classifications. Water meter pits or vaults shall be installed outside the building, as close to the
street as practical.
6.
Service lines and curb stops.
a.
Installation and costs. Water service lines and curb stops shall be installed by and at the expense of the
owner for all new water users served by the city water utility. Service lines and
curb stops may be installed by the city as directed by the city manager or designee
and in accordance with section 14.04.300 . If the city performs the installation, the city shall restore damage to private
property, if any, as near as feasible to the condition pre-existing the installation,
except as necessary to accommodate the installation.
b.
Ownership and maintenance. The city shall own and maintain the water service line from the mainline to the curb
stop, excluding the curb stop valve and box, where a curb stop exists. In instances
where no curb stop or outside meter exists, the city will maintain the service line
from the main to the right-of-way line. When an outside meter exists without a separate
curb stop, the city will maintain the service line from the main to the meter, provided
the meter is within 15 feet of the right-of-way line. All remaining portions of the
service line are to be owned and maintained by the property owner.
7.
Water facilities at Vance Brand Airport. At the Vance Brand Municipal Airport, the city shall maintain the water service line
from the mainline to the curb stop set with the master meter. Each lessee shall maintain
all remaining portions of the service line. The city may use master meters to determine
whether such remaining portions of the service line have been adequately maintained
to efficiently convey water. If lessees do not adequately maintain the service line
from the master meter to the endpoint of the water service, the city may perform this
work subject to reimbursement of the cost of the work by the lessees served by the
master meter, plus a 25 percent surcharge. In these circumstances, each such lessee
shall pay a proportionate share of such cost and surcharge according to the lessee's
number of fixture units. Upon failure of a responsible party to comply with these
requirements, the city may discontinue service under the procedures described in section 14.20.150 and may take any other action permissible under the lease.
8.
Water services installed under section 15.05.040.O to exclusively irrigate arterial
rights-of-way shall be maintained by the landowner, his or her successors, heirs,
and assigns, or lessees of any real property abutting the arterial right-of-way and
shall be subject to the requirements described herein. Further, all arterial right-of-way
irrigation systems shall be subject to the requirements described in chapter 14.06 , pertaining to backflow and cross-connections.
9.
Water service, facilities, and meters remain with the property. Water service facilities installed to a property, including water taps, meters and
necessary appurtenances, shall remain with the property, except as allowed in chapter 14.07 , and are not transferrable in whole or in part.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.290; Code 2009, § 14.04.290; Ord. No. O-2004-41, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2015-09, § 18, 1-27-2015; Ord. No. O-2015-48, § 3, 10-13-2015; Ord. No. O-2021-23 , § 4, 4-27-2021; Ord. No. O-2025-36 , § 1, 6-3-2025)
A.
Right of entry.
1.
The city manager or designee shall have the right to enter any property during reasonable
hours for any legitimate purpose related to water service provided by the city, including
inspection, installation, construction, or maintenance of utility infrastructure or
related services. If the city is not able to obtain access as required herein, the
city shall notify the property owner of the failure of the required access, and the
property owner shall promptly take such steps as may be necessary to provide the required
access. Any refusal to allow such access will be cause for suspension of water service
in accordance with section 14.20.150 until access is provided.
2.
If inspection, installation, construction, or maintenance of utility infrastructure
or related services requires entry into a residence by the city or its agent, the
city or its agent shall obtain the consent of the property owner or lawful resident.
If the city is not able to obtain such consent, this refusal to allow access will
be cause for suspension of water service in accordance with section 14.20.150 until access is provided.
B.
City right to inspect, install, construct, maintain.
1.
If the responsible party, as indicated in this chapter, does not install, construct,
perform maintenance, or take other required action to keep meter pits, vaults and
covers, water service lines, and the curb stop valve and box, or any other water facilities
necessary for water service, in good condition and accessible to city personnel, the
city shall be entitled to enter the property and perform such installation, construction,
maintenance, or similar service.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-12; Code 1993, § 14.04.300; Code 2009, § 14.04.300; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 19; Ord. No. O-96-43, § 8; Ord. No. O-2000-52, § 12; Ord. No. O-2021-23 , § 5, 4-27-2021; Ord. No. O-2025-36 , § 1, 6-3-2025)
Editor's note— Ord. No. O-2025-36 , amended the title of § 14.04.290 to read as herein set out. The former § 14.04.290
was entitled "Service lines and appurtenances; maintenance by owner."
It is unlawful for any person to drill any well without first obtaining the necessary
permits from the state engineers office and the city. It is unlawful for any person
to tap or make any connection with a pipeline or water mains forming a part of the
waterworks, or to take or use any water from the waterworks, without having first
obtained a permit and paid the required fees. Any person desiring to drill a well
or tap the mains of the water system, or make any connection therewith or use water
therefrom, shall make written application to the chief building official for a permit
to do so.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-10(A); Code 1993, § 14.04.310; Code 2009, § 14.04.310; Ord. No.
O-93-33, § 20)
It is unlawful to attach or tap, in any manner, any well to the city water supply,
and it is unlawful to use well water for irrigation unless signs are prominently displayed
informing the public of such irrigation usage. Signs shall conform to regulations
set forth by the city manager.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-10(B); Code 1993, § 14.04.320; Code 2009, § 14.04.320; Ord. No.
O-79-3, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-65, § 2)
It is unlawful for any person to take water for temporary use for construction purposes
without a permit from the director of water and waste services, or the director's
designee. The city may require a deposit for a permit based on estimated water use,
and city costs involved in providing meters or other equipment used for measuring
temporary water use. At the end of the temporary water use period, the permittee will
return any city provided meters or equipment and pay the city for the actual amount
of water used based on current city rates. Once the permittee returns any city provided
meters or equipment, the city will refund any deposit for such meter or equipment,
as well as any deposit for water that is in excess of actual water used. To the extent
the actual water used is in excess of the deposit for estimated water usage, the permittee
will be required to pay for the remaining amount.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-10(B); Code 1993, § 14.04.330; Code 2009, § 14.04.330; Ord. No.
O-79-3, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-65, § 2; Ord. No. O-93-33, § 21; Ord. No. O-2015-09, §
19, 1-27-2015; Ord. No. O-2024-41 , § 1, 7-23-2024)
The term "compound tap" means more than one property connected to a single water or
fire-service line. No compound usage taps, except as provided for residential condominium
or residential townhome ownership in section 14.04.350 , or as provided for master metering at Vance Brand Municipal Airport in section 14.04.290.C.5,
shall be permitted, and it is unlawful for any person to use water from the water
mains in any building for other than that for which permit was issued. The term "residential
townhome" means, for purposes of this chapter, three or more separately owned dwelling
units within one building structure sharing common walls, with each unit of the building
structure on a separate legal lot, and all common areas in homeowner's association
ownership. The term "residential condominium" means, for the purposes of this chapter,
three or more separately owned dwelling units, with the units often stacked one above
the other in a vertical configuration, sharing common vertical walls and/or horizontal
floors and ceilings with the entire structure, or combination of multiple structures,
situated on one legal lot. Each separate building structure shall have a separate
service line and meter. Services for a residential townhome unit on a compound tap
must extend out from the building structure to a common service line which is located
at least five feet horizontally from the outside foundation of the building structure.
Service lines shall not be connected underneath or through one building structure
to the next.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-10(B); Code 1993, § 14.04.340; Code 2009, § 14.04.340; Ord. No.
O-79-3, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-65, § 2; Ord. No. O-85-40, § 1; Ord. No. O-93-33, § 22;
Ord. No. O-94-40, § 1; Ord. No. O-2000-53, § 1; Ord. No. O-2015-48, § 4, 10-13-2015)
A.
Compound usage taps shall be permitted for residential condominiums or townhomes in
the city if the owner of the realty projected for residential condominium or residential
townhome development submits to the city, prior to site plan approval, a declaration
containing a covenant running with the land and binding upon the owner's heirs, successors
and assigns, that a homeowners' association consisting of the owners of each residential
condominium or residential townhome unit shall be jointly and severally responsible
for the water rents and rates attributable to the development and any subsequent repairs
or maintenance of the lines for the entire subdivision plat or site plan. All such
charges for residential condominium or residential townhome developments containing
more than two residential condominium or residential townhome units shall be based
upon such rates and development fees applicable to multifamily residential service
or as otherwise provided by ordinance. Discontinuance of service for nonpayment of
such water rents and rates by the association shall be imposed upon the entirety of
the residential condominium or residential townhome development sharing the compound
tap, and any lien for such rents and rates as set forth in section 14.04.270 shall
attach to the individually owned residential condominium or residential townhome units,
as well as the general and limited common elements of the development in common use.
B.
If the declaration containing the covenants described in subsection A of this section
is not submitted to the city by the owner of the realty to be developed as residential
condominium or residential townhome property, then each residential condominium or
residential townhome unit shall have a separate water tap, service line and meter,
and no compound tap shall be permitted for such development.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-10(B); Code 1993, § 14.04.350; Code 2009, § 14.04.350; Ord. No.
O-79-3, § 1; Ord. No. O-80-65, § 2; Ord. No. O-85-40, § 2; Ord. No. O-93-33, § 23;
Ord. No. O-94-40, § 2)
Water services classified as mixed use and any associated irrigation service will
be billed to the building owner. It is the building owner's responsibility to distribute
costs among the tenants.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.355; Code 2009, § 14.04.355; Ord. No. O-2006-76, § 5)
All water installations shall be made in accordance with the city public improvements
design standards and construction specifications.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-11(A); Code 1993, § 14.04.360; Code 2009, § 14.04.360; Ord. No.
O-93-33, § 24; Ord. No. O-2001-78, § 3; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018)
A.
Unless otherwise noted, there shall be a permit and inspection fee for all permits
issued for building water service lines and meter pits for all customers. All customer
classifications shall be charged a fee of $95.00 for water service line and meter
pit inspections.
B.
Fees shall be paid at the time the application is filed.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.365; Code 2009, § 14.04.365; Ord. No. O-2000-67, § 1; Ord. No.
O-2009-34, § 4, 7-14-2009; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 20, 1-27-2015)
All excavations in the street shall be in conformity to the provisions of this Code,
and such suitable barricades and guards shall be placed around such excavations as
shall be sufficient to protect all persons, and in addition, from twilight until sunrise,
sufficient warning lights shall be kept in operation near such excavation in order
to protect all persons from injury or damage thereby. The persons making such excavations
shall be liable for all injuries or damages resulting from their failure to so guard
such excavation, give proper warning and to protect persons and property on account
of such excavation.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-11(B); Code 1993, § 14.04.370; Code 2009, § 14.04.370)
All new single-unit or multiple-unit residential structures shall be equipped with
a meter registering the flow of water to and for each structure. The applicant for
a building permit for such structures shall purchase, from the city and at the cost
to the city, water-measuring equipment for each residential structure at the time
of issuance of such permit. The measuring equipment shall be installed by the applicant,
at the expense of the applicant, and in accordance with the specifications of and
at locations designated by the city for such installation, to facilitate uniformity
of operation and readings.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-11(C); Code 1993, § 14.04.380; Code 2009, § 14.04.380; Ord. No.
O-80-65, § 1; Ord. No. O-93-33, § 25; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 21, 1-27-2015; Ord. No. O-2021-73 , § 2, 12-14-2021)
Plumbers or any other persons are prohibited from connecting pipes whereby a steam
boiler may be supplied with water direct with city pressure. All such boilers must
be provided with a tank or other receptacle of sufficient capacity to hold at least
six hours' supply, in case of a pipe district being shut off for repairs or making
connections or extensions. In such cases, the city will not be responsible for lack
of water for steam boilers or for any other purpose.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-13; Code 1993, § 14.04.390; Code 2009, § 14.04.390)
It is unlawful for any person not in the employ of or contracted by the city to make
any tap less than or equal to two inches in diameter to any water line of the water
utility. Approval must be granted by the director of public works and natural resources,
or the director's designee, prior to making any tap greater than two inches to any
water line of the water utility.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-14; Code 1993, § 14.04.400; Code 2009, § 14.04.400; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 26; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 22, 1-27-2015)
When tap or connection is made and work completed, as provided in this chapter, the
same shall be tested and, when found sufficient, the water shall be shut off at the
stopcock until the owner of the premises produces to the director of public works
and natural resources a receipt for the owner's water rates for the current period.
No water shall be turned on for any premises, house, building or lot until the water
rate for the current period has been paid according to the uses called for in the
permit.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-15; Code 1993, § 14.04.410; Code 2009, § 14.04.410; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 27; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 23, 1-27-2015)
It is unlawful for any person to injure or in any way damage any property, equipment
or appliance constituting or being a part of the water utility, or any fences, guard
rails, boxes, covers or buildings constructed and used to protect such utility or
any part thereof.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-5; Code 1993, § 14.04.420; Code 2009, § 14.04.420)
It is unlawful for any person, save those charged with the responsibility of the management
of the water utility or employees of the water utility or the grounds upon which the
same is constructed, to tap any mains or to make connections therewith, or in any
manner to interfere with the utility or the property, equipment or appliances constituting
the utility or any part thereof, or to meddle or interfere with any pipe, valve or
appliance used to regulate the flow in the utility or any part thereof, or to change
or alter the position of any valve or appliance regulating the flow of water in any
pipeline.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-6; Code 1993, § 14.04.430; Code 2009, § 14.04.430)
It is unlawful for any person to cast, place, dump or deposit in the water utility
any substance or material which will in any manner injure or obstruct the same, or
any material or substance which will contaminate or pollute the water in the water
utility or in any pipe, reservoir, filter, sedimentation basin, or any equipment or
appliance forming a part of the water utility, or in any manner to obstruct the water
utility or pollute the water therein. It is unlawful for any person, in any manner,
to pollute or contaminate the stream or source of water from which the city obtains
its water for a distance of five miles around or above the intake of the water utility,
or to have, keep or maintain, at, along or near the banks of said stream or source
for a distance of five miles around or above the intake of the water utility, any
building, privy, pen, yard or corral for stock, or to have, keep or conduct any business
near such creek, as aforesaid, which will contaminate or pollute the waters of the
creek or render the same unfit for domestic use.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-7; Code 1993, § 14.04.440; Code 2009, § 14.04.440)
A.
Nonemergency watering restrictions. The director of public works and natural resources may implement nonemergency watering
restrictions of domestic water used for garden, lawn or other exterior watering or
sprinkling applications as the director finds necessary for the conservation, preservation
and efficient management of the city's water resources and to maximize, to the extent
practicable, the beneficial use of the city's water resources for its citizens. The
watering frequency and schedule shall be determined by the director at time of implementation.
Such restrictions shall apply to all water users on the city water system. Such restrictions,
unless otherwise contemplated by emergency conditions, shall not apply to such water
usage applied primarily in the course and conduct of a business activity or domestic
watering applied by hand. The nonemergency watering restrictions may be implemented
or suspended upon adoption and publication in a newspaper having general circulation
in the city, of a resolution by the city council stating the effective date and duration
of the restrictions.
1.
A water customer may make a personal application for a special permit for daily watering
of new seed, new sod or xeriscape landscaping, notwithstanding applicable nonemergency
restrictions, as follows:
a.
If the application is for daily watering of one-half acre or less for residential
property, the director or director's designee should issue the permit. The director
or designee may decide to withhold the permit as inconsistent with the purposes of
the restrictions after considering the impact on the city's water resources, including,
but not limited to, the size of the area to be watered, the amount of water to be
used, the type and severity of conditions prompting the watering restrictions and
the public benefit. If drought conditions prompted the watering restrictions, the
director or designee should also consider the then-current drought response plan,
including the current and expected drought response levels.
b.
If the application is for more than one-half acre or other than residential property,
the director or designee should not issue the permit. The director or designee may
issue the permit if he determines that the permit is consistent with the purposes
of the restrictions.
c.
If a special permit for daily watering is issued, it shall be for 35 days for new
seed, or 21 days for new sod or xeriscape landscaping daily watering.
d.
For the purposes of this section, landscaping shall be considered xeriscape landscaping
if it meets the standards set forth in section 15.05.040.C.3.
e.
The director or designee may revoke permits prior to their expiration date if the
permit terms are not complied with or if wasting of water occurs.
B.
Emergency watering restrictions. In the event of emergency conditions affecting the availability of treated water
at any time, due to climatic conditions, plant breakdown, capacity of the water treatment
plant, or otherwise, the city manager, or acting city manager in the city manager's
absence, may declare a watering emergency and establish further restrictions or prohibitions
for any exterior watering application. Such shall become effective upon publication
in a newspaper of general circulation in the city and shall remain in effect for 14
days unless sooner modified or rescinded earlier by supplementary orders, or modified
or extended by resolution of the city council, which rescission, modification or extension
shall become effective upon such publication.
C.
Special assessments for violation of mandatory watering restrictions. When a city representative observes violation of watering restrictions, the representative
shall serve a written warning or notice of special assessment, specifically stating
violations alleged, by any of the following methods: (1) personally on the water customer
of record at that address; or (2) posting the warning or notice on the premises and
mailing a copy by first class mail to the water customer of record at that address.
Each day that a violation occurs shall constitute a separate violation. The city representative
shall issue one written warning per calendar year to a customer for the first observed
violation. The written warning shall include information about wise water practices.
For violations after the written warning, the assessment for a single family, duplex
or multifamily water tap for the first violation in any calendar year is $100.00;
for a second violation in any calendar year, $300.00; and for third or additional
violation in any calendar year, $500.00. After written warning, the assessment for
a small commercial, large commercial or irrigation water tap for the first violation
in any calendar year is $200.00; for a second violation in any calendar year, $600.00;
and for a third or additional violations in any calendar year, $1,000.00. Unless timely
appealed, the city shall include special assessments as additional charges on the
customer's next water bill. For the purposes of this section, the term "customer"
shall include homeowners' associations or other entities obligated to maintain irrigation
systems along city streets and the special assessment may be assessed against any
property within the city owned by those entities. Nothing in this section shall restrict
the city from otherwise seeking legal or equitable relief to secure compliance with
watering restrictions.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-8; Code 1993, § 14.04.450; Code 2009, § 14.04.450; Ord. No. O-82-23,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-84-30, § 1; Ord. No. O-93-33, § 28; Ord. No. O-94-61, § 9; Ord. No.
O-96-43, § 9; Ord. No. O-2002-51, § 2; Ord. No. O-2003-20, § 2; Ord. No. O-2005-61,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 24, 1-27-2015; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018)
No water shall be used from fire hydrants except for the extinguishment of fires,
provided that water may be used therefrom by members of regularly constituted fire
companies for the purpose of cleaning, washing or testing fire engines or other fire
apparatus, and employees of the water/wastewater department for testing the water
pressure in the water mains, or for testing the condition of the water utility or
flushing sewers.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-9(A); Code 1993, § 14.04.460; Code 2009, § 14.04.460; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 29)
No water shall be issued from fire hydrants except as indicated in section 14.04.460 or as approved by a temporary water use permit prepared and issued by the director
of public works and natural resources or the director's designee.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.465; Code 2009, § 14.04.465; Ord. No. O-93-33, § 30; Ord. No.
O-2015-09, § 25, 1-27-2015)
It is unlawful for any person to permit water to flow from any private hydrant or
fountain during a fire, except on or near the premises where the fire exists and for
the purpose of putting out or protecting the person's property from such fire.
(Code 1981, § 8-1-9(B); Code 1993, § 14.04.470; Code 2009, § 14.04.470; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 31)
No water shall be provided for internal or external use to any residential, commercial,
industrial, agricultural, recreational, governmental or public building or structure
of any kind which is constructed or remodeled, and in which plumbing, water piping
or water fixtures are to be installed, extended or altered in any way, and for which
construction a building permit is required to be obtained from the city (or would
be required but for an exemption from a permit requirement for public or governmental
agencies) unless the new or altered plumbing fixtures therein conform to the requirements
and standards of section 14.04.500 .
(Code 1993, § 14.04.480; Code 2009, § 14.04.480; Ord. No. O-84-77, § 1)
A.
Waste of water prohibited. All waste of water from the city water system is prohibited. Customers shall not
cause or permit water furnished by the city to run to waste in any gutter or other
impervious surface, or other application. For the purposes of this section, in addition
to individual utility users, the term "customer" shall include homeowners' associations
or other entities obligated to maintain irrigation systems along city streets.
B.
Definition. Waste of water constitutes the use of water serving no beneficial use and not constituting
an unavoidable consequence of the beneficial usage of water. Waste of water does not
include incidental and occasional over spraying. An apparent waste of water will be
based on a comparative usage by persons or entities in reasonably similar situations.
Waste of water shall include, but is not limited to, any of the following:
1.
The application of water to any of the following in a manner that results in pooling
of water or a steady flow of water into storm drainage facilities, or onto streets,
sidewalks, driveways or other impervious surfaces:
a.
Any lawn, turf, sodded or landscaped area;
b.
An impervious surface, such as a patio, driveway, garage;
c.
A vehicle or other structure or object; or
d.
Any similar property or area.
2.
The release of water from a leak, broken component, or malfunction of a valve or controller,
a sprinkler or other irrigation system, or a broken or malfunctioning plumbing fixture;
3.
The application of water to an impervious surface, vehicle or other structure or object
with a hose without the use of a hose nozzle designed to stay in the closed position,
except when activated by hand pressure to permit the flow of water; or
4.
Other scenarios of similar nature that fall within the definition of waste of water
in this section.
C.
Notice of violation and enforcement. When a city representative observes a violation of this section, the representative
shall serve a written notice of violation. The notice shall:
1.
State the nature of the violation;
2.
State the timeframe for correction of the violation;
3.
Be served by the methods identified in section 14.04.530.B;
4.
Include information about wise water practices;
5.
State that the owner or occupant may submit, in writing, a request for a hearing in
the matter to the city clerk.
D.
Compliance and fines. A customer that receives a notice of violation shall have ten days from the date
of the notice to correct the violation unless the city manager or designee determines
in his or her discretion that public health, safety and welfare requires that the
customer correct the violation in a shorter time. If the violation is not timely corrected
after the written warning, fines shall be assessed as follows:
1.
No mandatory watering restrictions. If mandatory watering restrictions pursuant to section 14.04.450 are not in effect, fines will be as follows:
a.
Single-family, duplex or multifamily water tap: the first violation in any calendar year is $20.00; the second violation in any calendar
year is $40.00; the third and any additional violation in any calendar year is $100.00
per violation.
b.
Small commercial, large commercial, or irrigation water tap: the first violation in any calendar year is $40.00; the second violation in any calendar
year is $120.00; the third and any additional violation in any calendar year is $200.00
per violation.
2.
During mandatory watering restrictions. If mandatory watering restrictions pursuant to section 14.04.450 are in effect, fines will be as follows:
a.
Single-family, duplex or multifamily water tap: the first violation in any calendar year is $100.00; the second violation in any
calendar year is $300.00; the third and any additional violation in any calendar year
is $500.00 per violation.
b.
Small commercial, large commercial, or irrigation water tap: the first violation in any calendar year is $200.00; the second violation in any
calendar year is $600.00; the third and any additional violation in any calendar year
is $1,000.00 per violation.
3.
Each day that a violation occurs shall constitute a separate violation. In the event of a continuing violation that has not been corrected within ten days
of the initial notice of violation, one written notice of violation will be issued
per month, detailing each violation within that month.
4.
During no mandatory water restrictions, fines will not exceed $1,000.00 per month
for single-family, duplex or multifamily water taps, and $2,000.00 per month for small
commercial, large commercial, or irrigation water taps.
5.
Charge on bill; lien. The city shall include fines pursuant to this section as additional charges on the
customer's next water bill. In the event the customer is a homeowners' association
or other entity obligated to maintain irrigation systems along city streets, the fines
may be assessed against any property within the city owned by those entities. If such
waste of water is not remedied within the given period of time, the city may also
terminate the customer's service pursuant to the process delineated in section 14.20.150 . The city may collect unpaid fees as identified in section 14.04.530 . Nothing in this section shall restrict the city from otherwise seeking legal or
equitable relief to secure compliance with this section.
6.
Appeals. Any fee imposed pursuant to this section may be appealed pursuant to section 14.04.540 .
(Code 1993, § 14.04.490; Code 2009, § 14.04.490; Ord. No. O-84-77, § 1; Ord. No. O-2005-61,
§ 2; Ord. No. O-2026-15 , § 1, 4-14-2026)
An owner or utility billing customer who seeks reimbursement for the fees or charges
assessed under this chapter from any tenant or lessee of the customer, or any other
person, shall charge no more than the actual fee or charge attributable to such person's
actual proportionate use of the water, plus any actual, reasonable, demonstrable,
administrative costs associated with collecting the reimbursement, which shall not
exceed five percent of such attributed amount.
(Ord. No. O-2021-73 , § 3, 12-14-2021)
A.
Water closets operated by flush tanks. The water consumption of water closets operated by flush tanks shall not exceed an
average of 1.6 gallons per flush cycle over a range of test pressures from 20 to 80
psig. The fixture shall perform in accordance with the flushing test requirements
cited in the ANSI (American National Standards Institute) Vitreous China Plumbing
Fixtures Standard.
B.
Water closets and urinals operated by flushometers.
1.
Water closet water consumption shall not exceed an average of 1.6 gallons per flush
cycle over a range of test pressures from 20 to 80 psig. The flushometer shall be
adjusted according to manufacturer's specifications. The fixture shall perform in
accordance with the flushing test requirements cited in the ANSI Vitreous China Plumbing
Fixtures Standard.
2.
Urinal water consumption shall not exceed an average of one gallon per flush cycle
over a range of test pressures from 20 to 80 psig. The flushometer shall be adjusted
according to manufacturer's specifications. The fixture shall perform in accordance
with the flushing test requirements cited in the ANSI Vitreous China Plumbing Fixtures
Standard.
C.
Showerheads. Showerhead discharge rates shall not exceed 2.5 gallons of water per minute over
a range of test pressures from 20 to 80 psig. The fixture shall perform in accordance
with the test requirements cited in the ANSI plumbing fixture fittings standard.
D.
Sink faucets.
1.
Nonresidential lavatory faucets shall be either self-closing or metering faucets,
as described below:
a.
Self-closing faucets shall not exceed an average discharge rate of 0.5 GPM between
the pressures of 20 and 80 psig when tested in accordance with the discharge test
procedure cited in ANSI plumbing fixture fittings standard.
b.
Metering faucets shall be field adjustable and set so that the discharge quantity
shall not exceed 0.5 gallons of water per cycle.
2.
Residential interior faucets discharge rates shall not exceed 2.5 gallons per minute
over a range of test pressures from 20 to 80 psig. The faucets shall perform according
to the test requirements cited in the ANSI plumbing fixture fittings standard.
E.
Blowout toilets and urinals. Replacement of blowout toilet and urinal fixtures with like type fixtures may be
granted by the director of public works and natural resources upon written request
where adequate justification of special need is provided.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.500; Code 2009, § 14.04.500; Ord. No. O-84-77, § 1; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 32; Ord. No. O-96-43, § 10; Ord. No. O-2000-67, § 3; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 26, 1-27-2015)
Where the service water pressure to a building is expected to exceed 90 PSI, a water
pressure reducing valve with strainer shall be installed at owner/builder expense
just downstream of the building's main valve, so as to be accessible. The valve shall
provide for pressure adjustment within the range of 55 to 90 PSI. The valve shall
conform to the requirement of product standard ASSE (2) 1003 (American Society of
Sanitary Engineering). Exemptions to this chapter are service lines to sill cocks,
outside hydrants, and main supply risers to buildings where pressure from the main
does not exceed 60 psi at the fixture branches or at individual fixtures.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.510; Code 2009, § 14.04.510; Ord. No. O-84-77, § 1; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 33)
Any person may petition the director of public works and natural resources in writing
for an exception to the standards contained in this chapter, which request shall be
granted upon establishment of proof that some other fixture, system or procedure proposed
will conserve as much or more water than would compliance with the standards set forth
in section 14.04.500 , or that compliance would result in undue hardship in the modification or replacement
of existing plumbing fixtures.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.520; Code 2009, § 14.04.520; Ord. No. O-84-77, § 1; Ord. No. O-93-33,
§ 34; Ord. No. O-2015-09, § 27, 1-27-2015)
A.
It is unlawful for any person to violate any of the provisions of this chapter. Offenses
shall be punishable according to chapter 1.12 , and as otherwise indicated herein. Each day that a violation occurs shall constitute
a separate offense and violation.
B.
Notice of violations under this chapter shall be served by any of the following methods:
(1) personally on the water customer of record; or (2) by posting the notice on the
premises and mailing a copy by first class mail to the water customer of record. The
notice shall also be mailed by first class mail to the owner of the property if the
owner is not the customer of record.
C.
Bypassing meter. The city may serve a written notice to any customer who fails to pay for water service
by bypassing the meter, which will require the customer to move the meter to a location
determined by the city outside the structure. The relocation of the meter shall be
at the customer's sole cost and expense. Unless timely appealed, the customer must
move the meter within seven days of receipt of the notice. If the customer does not
timely move the meter, the city may do so at the customer's sole cost and expense
and charge the cost of moving the meter as a special assessment on the customer's
next water bill. Any refusal by the customer or owner to allow the city access to
move the meter shall be cause for suspension of water service in accordance with sections 14.20.150 and 14.20.160 until access is allowed.
D.
Installation, construction, maintenance. If the responsible party under this chapter does not perform the installation, construction,
maintenance, or other action required under this chapter, except for violations of
bypassing the meter, which are provided for above, the responsible party shall have
30 days from the date of the notice of violation to comply with the identified requirements
and certify compliance to the city manager or designee within such time. Unless timely
appealed, if the responsible party does not come into compliance within 30 days from
the date of the notice of violation, the city or its agent may enter the property
to perform the installation, construction, maintenance, or other action identified
subject to reimbursement of costs, plus a ten percent administrative service charge.
Failure to reasonably respond to the city or permit entry to the city for such purpose
may result in suspension of water service in accordance with section 14.20.150 until access is provided.
1.
All charges, penalties, fees, or expenses according to this section shall be a lien
upon the premises, from the date the same becomes due until paid. The city may collect
such liens and liabilities by an action at law or equity to enforce the lien.
2.
In addition to an action at law or equity to enforce the lien, the city finance director,
or designee, may certify such charges, penalties, fees, or expenses as an assessment
to the office of the treasurer of the county that includes the premises, for collection
in the same manner as the collection of general property taxes. Upon receipt of the
assessment roll certified by the director of finance, or designee, the county treasurer
shall collect the amounts so assessed in the same manner as the collection of general
property taxes. The city finance director, or designee, may accept payment of the
amount of assessment before certification to the county treasurer. After certification,
only the county treasurer may accept payment.
E.
Hazards. Further, when, as a result of an inspection, a condition involving violation of this
chapter resulting in a health or sanitary hazard is determined to exist, the city
manager or designee shall have the option of immediate discontinuance of water service
to the premises until the condition has been corrected, or the city manager or designee
may specify a date for compliance after which time the city manager or designee may
discontinue service until the responsible party has corrected the condition or defect
or allowed the city to do so. Any such discontinuance of service is governed by section 14.20.150 .
F.
In addition to the penalties authorized in this section, the city attorney may bring
a civil action, including, but not limited to, enjoining the waste of water, before
any court of competent jurisdiction.
G.
All of the remedies provided for violations of this chapter, whether civil or criminal,
shall be cumulative and in addition to any other remedy provided by law.
(Code 1993, § 14.04.530; Code 2009, § 14.04.530; Ord. No. O-84-77, § 1; Ord. No. O-94-61,
§ 9; Ord. No. O-2005-61, § 3; Ord. No. O-2025-36 , § 2, 6-3-2025)
A.
Within seven days of receipt of any notice of special assessment pursuant to this
chapter, the customer may file a written appeal with the city clerk, who shall schedule
a hearing before the city's hearing officer. The appeal shall state the grounds upon
which the customer contends the notice is unjustified or should be suspended. The
hearing officer shall conduct a hearing within ten days of receipt of appeal. The
city shall have the burden to substantiate the violation alleged in the notice. The
hearing officer's decision shall be final, subject to further appeal under rule 106(a)(4),
Colorado Rules of Civil Procedure.
1.
Related to failure to pay water service by bypassing the meter, if the proof shows
a first-time violation, the hearing officer may suspend the special assessment or
requirement to move the meter upon adequate assurance of future compliance. The city
shall include any special assessment sustained on appeal in the next water bill. If
the notice to move the meter is upheld, the customer must obey the relocation notice
within seven days of receipt of the hearing officer's final decision.
B.
All special assessments shall be collected as set forth in section 14.20.080 .
(Code 1993, § 14.04.540; Code 2009, § 14.04.540; Ord. No. O-2005-61, § 4; Ord. No. O-2025-36 , § 2, 6-3-2025)
A.
It is necessary to adopt, revise and continually update a raw water master plan to
maintain an adequate water supply.
B.
It is the intent of this chapter to encourage the retention of historical water rights
on the agricultural properties within the St. Vrain Basin by requiring the transfer
of such water rights to city ownership as a condition of annexation and by requiring
the payment of an equivalent cash amount or the transfer of equivalent water rights
in the absence of such historical water rights.
C.
This chapter provides the method whereby the city will acquire water rights in such
quantity and of such quality as to provide a safe and reliable water supply for present
and future water users under a wide range of hydrological conditions by requiring
the transfer to city ownership of not less than three acre-feet of water per acre
of land, which quantity has been determined by engineering analysis to be sufficient.
D.
This chapter also provides the method whereby the owners of properties with historical
water rights insufficient to provide three acre-feet of water per acre of land may
satisfy the city's raw water requirements by allowing the transfer of selected nonhistorical
water rights and/or the payment of cash in lieu of water rights transfers.
E.
It is in the interest of the health, safety and welfare of the citizens to dedicate
a specified amount of water supply to be used as an economic incentive to encourage
the construction of affordable housing and spur certain types of economic development.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.010; Code 2009, § 14.05.010; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1; Ord. No. O-2012-73, § 2, 10-23-2012; Ord. No. O-2020-02 , § 2, 1-28-2020)
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter shall have the meanings
ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates a different
meaning:
Acre-foot means 43,560 cubic feet and any volume of water based on the assumption that a flow
rate of one cubic foot of water per second of time (cfs) will equal two acre-feet
in 24 hours. An acre-foot equals approximately 325,850 gallons.
Annexation means the bringing of land into the territorial jurisdiction of the city.
Beneficial use means the application of water by an individual user for either direct or indirect
human benefit.
Cash in lieu of water rights means cash that is paid to the city in lieu of dedicating nonhistorical water rights
to the city. Cash in lieu is not acceptable for dedication in lieu of historical water
rights.
Colorado-Big Thompson water or C-BT water means water diverted from the Colorado River drainage by the USBR Colorado-Big Thompson
diversion project and allocated as a supplemental supply by the Northern Colorado
Water Conservancy District.
Development activity means any activity requiring a major, minor or administrative development application,
as defined in section 15.02.020 .
Direct flow right or direct flow water means the right to use water which is diverted for use without interruption between
diversion and use except for purposes incidental to the use and the water diverted
under such a right.
Dry-up agreement means a restrictive covenant running with a particular parcel of land whereby upon
removal of the historical water rights from that parcel (for transfer to the city),
agricultural irrigation and any consumptive use of irrigation water will cease.
Economic development opportunity means a sole proprietorship or corporation that locates a new business within the
city limits at a location that has not previously been final platted, and has provided
information satisfactory to the city's chief financial officer that the business will
generate annual sales and use tax revenue of at least $2,500,000.00.
Historical water rights mean those water rights associated with a particular parcel of land at the time of
annexation and which have been applied to beneficial use on that land.
Nonhistorical water rights means those water rights which have not been regularly applied to beneficial use
on a parcel of land proposed for development activity.
Statement of beneficial use means a signed affidavit which provides historical information concerning the beneficial
use of a water right proposed for transfer to city ownership.
Storage right or storage water means the right to use water which is interrupted in its natural flow and detained
for later beneficial use and the water diverted under such a right.
Unit of C-BT or acre-foot unit means, as defined by the Northern Colorado Water Conservancy District, 1/310,000
of the annual supply of Colorado-Big Thompson water apportioned for distribution to
holders of allotment contracts.
Water right means a property right to use in accordance with its priority a certain portion of
the waters of the state by reason of the appropriation of the same.
Windy Gap water means water diverted from the Colorado River drainage by the Windy Gap (six cities)
project and delivered through the C-BT delivery system for use by participants within
the boundaries of the Municipal Subdistrict-Northern Colorado Water Conservancy District.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.020; Code 2009, § 14.05.020; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1; Ord. No. O-2012-73, § 2, 10-23-2012; Ord. No. O-2015-17, § 4, 3-17-2015;
Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018; Ord. No. O-2020-02 , § 3, 1-28-2020)
A.
For all annexations, the water board shall review the adequacy of water rights proposed
for transfer to ensure satisfaction of the requirements of this policy.
B.
For all other development activity, water resources staff shall review the adequacy
of water rights transferred to ensure satisfaction of the requirements of this policy.
In advance of transfer of water rights for satisfaction of this policy, the landowner
may request that the water board assess the adequacy of water rights proposed for
transfer.
C.
All documentation and information necessary for the water board to determine the adequacy
of water rights shall be submitted to water resources staff a minimum of ten working
days prior to the water board meeting at which such determination is to be made.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.030; Code 2009, § 14.05.030; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1; Ord. No. O-2012-41, § 4, 7-10-2012; Ord. No. O-2012-73, § 2, 10-23-2012)
A.
Annexation.
1.
As a condition of annexation of land to the city, there will be transferred to the
city ownership all historical water rights associated with the land. Land annexed
as enclaves must meet this requirement as set forth in subsection B.1 of this section.
All historical water rights will be credited on a pro-rata basis over the entire land
being annexed, less those areas exempted in subsection C of this section.
2.
Historical water rights acceptable for satisfaction of the requirements of this chapter
shall be transferred to city ownership upon meeting all other conditions necessary
for finalization of annexation.
B.
Other development activity.
1.
As a condition of development activity (other than annexation) on lands annexed to
the city, including land annexed as enclaves, there shall be transferred to the city
all historical water rights not previously dedicated at time of annexation. If the
historical water rights are insufficient to provide at least three acre-feet of water
for each acre of such lands, then, except as otherwise provided for in subsection
D of this section, sufficient nonhistorical water rights, or cash in lieu of water
rights, to yield at least three acre-feet of water must be transferred to the city.
Not less than one acre-foot of water per acre of land shall be supplied by storage
water rights. The remaining requirement will be supplied by the transfer of direct
flow or storage water rights. Credit for water rights transferred at annexation pursuant
to this policy will be applied toward satisfaction of this requirement.
2.
Satisfaction of the full raw water requirement applicable to a particular land development
activity may be accomplished at any time subsequent to finalization of the associated
annexation ordinance; however, said satisfaction shall be due and payable prior to
any development activity and prior to the provision of any water service from the
city.
C.
Exceptions.
1.
For land annexed prior to creation of the water board on June 25, 1963, raw water
requirements will not be imposed in connection with development activity on said land
unless raw water requirements have been previously specified in a written agreement
relative to the original annexation.
2.
For the purposes of calculating the full raw water requirement applicable to any development
activity, the areas occupied by railroad, state or county rights-of-way in existence
at the time of annexation will be exempt from such requirements. For those areas which
are owned and used for municipal purposes by the city, the transfer of historical
water rights shall satisfy the raw water requirements. All public rights-of-way created
by platting shall require full satisfaction of this policy.
3.
For any parcel of property meeting the requirements of this subsection that involves
annexation of a body of water larger than an aggregate of ten surface acres in a single
annexation, with a minimum of one surface acre per waterbody, then for the property
below the high-water line of the body of water no additional water beyond the proportional
historical water need be dedicated to the city. In order to meet the requirements
of this subsection, the property must meet the following requirements:
a.
The owner must grant to the city an irrevocable conservation easement, an undivided
interest in the property or other land use agreement that would perpetually guarantee
the continued use of the property for a waterbody and maintenance of any court decree
or augmentation requirements for the waterbody on the property so inundated by the
water;
b.
All augmentation requirements for the waterbody must be previously met by a final
decree with the water court or an augmentation agreement with the Saint Vrain and
Left Hand Water Conservancy District.
4.
Conveyance plats under title 15 do not require satisfaction of the full raw water requirement applicable to the lots
created by the conveyance plat. However, final city approval authorizing other development
activity on such land requires prior satisfaction of the requirements. Transfer of
all associated historical water rights to the city is required before final approval
of a conveyance plat on any portion of annexed property.
D.
Additional water requirement. The city reserves the right to require the transfer of additional water, in excess
of three acre-feet per acre, for users whose projected water demands exceed three
acre-feet per acre. The amount of additional water, timing of transfer to the city,
and method of satisfaction, shall be established by the city. The intent of this subsection
is to provide the method whereby the city will acquire water rights in such quantity
and of such quality as to provide a safe and reliable water supply. The city further
reserves the right to monitor the user's water demand and require the transfer of
additional water should the user's water demand exceed the original projected level.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.040; Code 2009, § 14.05.040; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1; Ord. No. O-2007-25, § 2; Ord. No. O-2012-41, § 5, 7-10-2012; Ord.
No. O-2012-73, § 2, 10-23-2012)
A.
For any parcels of land proposed for annexation, or land annexed as enclaves, which
are served by an existing city water tap and have no change in customer classification
or size of the water tap or additional development activity, then the requirements
of this chapter will be deemed to have been met by transfer of all existing historical
water rights at time of annexation for the lot so served by that tap. A raw water
credit, to be established and applied at time of annexation, will be given for the
existing water tap. The raw water credit will be equal to the amount of water transferred
to the city at the time the water tap was originally approved by the city. In the
event it is not possible for the city to accurately establish the water dedication
at the time the water tap was approved by the city, the following shall apply:
1.
For a three-quarter inch or a one-inch residential water tap, the existing tap shall
receive a raw water credit equal to the average annual water use based upon a minimum
of the most recent five years of prior water service history as recorded by the city's
utility billing records, up to a maximum credit of two acre-feet of water. The credit
will be applied to the lot being served by the existing water tap. Any change in either
the size of the tap or the customer classification of the existing tap will require
full satisfaction of the requirements of this chapter for the lot.
2.
For any nonresidential tap or residential tap larger than a one-inch tap, a raw water
credit will be given equal to the average annual water use based upon a minimum of
the most recent five years of prior water service history as recorded by the city's
utility billing records.
B.
Full satisfaction of the requirements of this chapter will be required for any new
parcel subsequently created through development activity of any kind on the property.
Full satisfaction of the requirements of this chapter will be required for any parcel
which changes the customer classification of the water tap, any parcel, which on a
rolling five-year average, utilizes water in excess of the five-year averages listed
