in subsection A.1 or A.2 of this section, or any parcel which increases its water
tap size.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.050; Code 2009, § 14.05.050; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1; Ord. No. O-2012-73, § 2, 10-23-2012)
A.
Storage water deficits.
1.
If the historical storage water rights are insufficient to supply one acre-foot of
water per acre of land being subdivided, resubdivided, platted or otherwise developed,
the requirement may be satisfied by the payment of cash in lieu of water rights transfers,
or the transfer of other acceptable nonhistorical water rights.
2.
A tabulation of nonhistorical water rights acceptable for credit against the storage
requirement follows, subject to the provisions of subsection C of this section.
Table.14.05.060.A. Nonhistorical Water Rights Acceptable for Storage Water Credit

Irrigation Organization | Storage Water Credit (in acre-feet per share or unit)
Lake McIntosh Reservoir Company | 0.53
The Longmont Supply Ditch Company | 16.74
The Oligarchy Irrigation Company | 17.11
The Pleasant Valley Reservoir, Fish and Ditch Company | 25.4
Rough and Ready Irrigating Company | 38.43
Union Reservoir Company | 15.3
Colorado-Big Thompson Project Units | 0.76

B.
Direct flow deficits.
1.
If the historical direct flow water rights are insufficient to supply at least two
acre-feet of water per acre of land undergoing development activity, the direct flow
water requirement may be satisfied by: Application of credit for historical storage
water rights in excess of one acre-foot per acre of land; payment of cash in lieu
of water rights transfers; or transfer of other acceptable nonhistorical water rights.
2.
A tabulation of nonhistorical water rights acceptable for credit against the direct
flow water requirement follows, subject to the provisions of subsection C of this
section.
Table.14.05.060.B. Nonhistorical Water Rights Acceptable for Storage Water Credit

Irrigation Organization | Direct Flow Credit (in acre-feet per share or unit)
Lake McIntosh Reservoir Company | 0.53
The Longmont Supply Ditch Company | 27.89
The Oligarchy Irrigation Company | 26.48
Rough and Ready Irrigating Ditch Company | 59.96
Union Reservoir Company | 15.3
The Pleasant Valley Reservoir, Fish and Ditch Company | 25.4
Colorado-Big Thompson Project Units | 0.76

C.
Acceptance of nonhistorical water rights.
1.
In order for a nonhistorical water right to be considered for acceptance, the following
must be supplied to the water resources division:
a.
A properly completed statement of beneficial use for each water right proposed for
transfer.
b.
Stock certificates, transfer orders and deeds conveying ownership to the city. For
ownership interest in unincorporated ditches, transfer of ownership is accomplished
by warranty deed only; however, additional documentation may be required to confirm
unencumbered ownership.
c.
A dry-up agreement, including a revegetation agreement, applicable to the land which
had been irrigated by the water rights proposed for transfer.
d.
A map showing location of the land to be removed from irrigation.
e.
For any unincorporated entity, including, but not limited to, the Smead Ditch, a notarized
statement signed by the secretary of the company attesting to the ownership of the
interest proposed for transfer to the city as reflected on the books of the company.
f.
A copy of the current ditch company bylaws.
g.
A copy of the current articles of incorporation, if applicable.
h.
At the city's discretion, any other relevant data or documentation, including, but
not limited to, a current list of stockholders indicating the total number of shares
in the company.
2.
Subsections C.1.f, g and h of this section are required only for the proposed transfer
of water rights in which the city has no ownership interest at the time of proposed
transfer.
3.
Requests for acceptance of nonhistorical water rights, with all necessary supporting
documentation, shall be submitted to water resources staff a minimum of ten working
days prior to any action of the water board, or of the development review committee
under title 15 , involving their assessment of the satisfaction of this chapter.
4.
When utilizing nonhistorical city supply, oligarchy, or rough and ready ditch water
rights and a request to apply the water fractionally to both storage and direct flow
deficits is made, the full credit necessary for application to satisfy direct flow
deficits shall be made first, then application to the storage deficit will be made.
All requests for both direct flow and storage credit will be reviewed by water resources
staff which will determine the practicality of allowing fractional or divisional crediting.
5.
The city will not accept any ditch or reservoir shares as nonhistorical water rights
that were used, as evidenced by a statement of beneficial use or other documentation,
on lands located within the city planning area that are not currently annexed to the
city.
6.
Water resources staff shall periodically review the list of acceptable nonhistorical
water rights and will identify those water rights that may or may not be desirable
for the city to receive and forward its recommendation to the water board for review.
The water board will then forward its recommendation to city council for action. This
evaluation shall include, but not be limited to, such issues as: the city's past acceptance
of a particular water right; the feasibility of successfully and economically adding
municipal uses to the water right through a water court change case; the impact of
removing the water right from the land; and the ability of the city to integrate the
water right into its overall water supply portfolio.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.060; Code 2009, § 14.05.060; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1; Ord. No. O-2012-73, § 2, 10-23-2012)
A.
Per acre-foot fee. The per acre-foot fee for cash in lieu of water rights transfers shall be as set
from time to time by resolution of the city council based on water board recommendation.
The per acre-foot fee for cash in lieu of water rights transfers will be reviewed
by the water board at least quarterly at its meetings in March, June, September and
December. The basis for the water board recommendation shall include the current cost
of new water supply projects identified in the city's raw water master plan, the current
market value associated with the purchase of units of Colorado-Big Thompson water,
and the current market value of nonhistorical native basin water rights identified
in section 14.05.060 . Recommendations to adjust the fee will be forwarded to city council if the need
for such adjustment is indicated.
B.
Payment of fee.
1.
The actual cash amount to be paid in lieu of water rights transfers will be calculated
by water resources staff. The amount so determined shall be due and payable prior
to final city approval of the development activity requiring such transfers or payment.
Said cash amount, which will be calculated as the product of the acre-foot raw water
deficit multiplied by the per acre-foot fee in effect at that time, will be fixed
for a period of 30 days after notification to the landowner. After expiration of the
30-day period, the amount due will be adjusted by water resources staff to reflect
the per acre-foot fee in effect on the day that payment is made.
2.
For any raw water deficits to be satisfied by cash payments there will be no deferral
of payment nor will installment payments be allowed.
3.
Notwithstanding the above, the city council may, by ordinance, in its sole discretion
agree to rebate some or all of the cash in lieu of nonhistorical water rights transfer
fee for an economic development opportunity if it finds in its sole discretion, that
such rebate is in the interest of the health, safety and welfare of the citizens of
Longmont and serves vital and important public purposes. The rebate provided above
may, if granted by the city council, be part of an economic incentive/development
agreement with the economic development opportunity approved by city council and shall
be subject to such terms and conditions as will in the judgement of the city council
ensure that the eligibility criteria and other pertinent development conditions will
be performed and satisfied. If any such terms or conditions are not performed or satisfied,
the economic incentive/development agreement shall terminate and the parties to that
agreement shall not be eligible for any further incentive payments.
4.
Notwithstanding subsections B.1 and B.2 above, any person, sole proprietorship, corporation,
or nonprofit who wishes to construct for-sale owner occupied attainable housing units
within the city limits, may apply for a partial rebate of this fee using the forms
supplied by the city. Commercial structures, garages, and outbuildings are not eligible
for a rebate under this subsection. Approved applicants shall receive a rebate of
the proportionate share of the total amount calculated by water resources staff as
in subsection B.1 above for each for-sale owner occupied attainable housing unit upon
presentation to the city of sufficient proof, to the satisfaction of the city, that
the unit will sell at an attainable price. The proportionate amount, based on the
square feet of lot size for each attainable unit as a share of the total lot sizes
in the subject development, will be rebated to the seller based on the contracted
sales price for the unit according to a schedule set forth in the application provided
by the city. Promptly upon sale of the attainable unit, the applicant shall provide
proof to the satisfaction of the city that the unit did actually sell for a price
within the applicable range. Any approved application granted by this subsection shall
be reduced to a written agreement between all the owners of the property and the city.
The written agreement will, at a minimum, provide for the following:
a.
The written agreement shall be subject to such terms and conditions of this subsection
and shall guarantee performance or satisfaction of the eligibility criteria and other
pertinent development conditions. The city manager or designee shall include any additional
terms or conditions that in their sole discretion are necessary or appropriate to
effect the intent of this subsection.
b.
The written agreement shall run with the land and be binding upon the heirs and successors
of the owners until such time that the agreement expires.
c.
The written agreement shall require that all owners and developers of the property
be responsible for the performance and satisfaction of its terms and conditions.
d.
If the owners or developers do not perform or satisfy any term or condition of the
agreement, the city may revoke the grant of any rebate, and the property and the owners
and developers of the property shall be required to repay any rebate that was improperly
granted.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.070; Code 2009, § 14.05.070; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1; Ord. No. O-2012-73, § 2, 10-23-2012; Ord. No. O-2013-31, § 2, 7-23-2013; Ord. No. O-2020-02 , § 4, 1-28-2020; Ord. No. Ord. No. O-2023-27 , § 2, 7-11-2023)
A.
Statement of program.
1.
Should the owners of land annexed to the city desire to continue its historical agricultural
use of the land, the city may lease to the owners a portion of the historical Saint
Vrain Creek Basin water rights proportional to the area of the land which will continue
to be used for agricultural purposes, for use on that agricultural area only. The
fee for the lease of such water will be determined by water resources staff subject
to water board concurrence. The fees will be based on then current agricultural market
conditions.
2.
The lease of such water shall be temporary and only for the year in which the payment
is made; it shall not initiate or give rise to any vested right to a continuation
of the same lease or use of water in any other year.
3.
Delivery of water under this program will not be authorized by the city prior to receipt
of the required lease fees.
4.
The city assumes no obligation for the delivery of such water beyond the payment of
the fee as set in subsection A.1 of this section and the notification of the water
commissioner and irrigation company superintendent of the lease arrangement.
5.
In any year that city water resources staff determines, at the city's sole discretion,
that the city's need to use water rights transferred to its ownership supersedes the
agricultural need, the option to lease water rights pursuant to this section may be
suspended by the city.
B.
Termination of program. At any time the city determines, in its sole discretion, it is in its best interests
to obtain water court approval of changed or alternate points of diversion or storage,
changes of use, exchange or augmentation plans or other such plans as may be devised
to optimize the beneficial use of its water rights, the annual lease program pursuant
to this section may be terminated.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.080; Code 2009, § 14.05.080; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1; Ord. No. O-2012-73, § 2, 10-23-2012)
A.
Statement required. The owners of land proposed for annexation shall submit a separate statement of beneficial
use for each water right proposed for transfer to the city. Forms for this purpose
may be obtained from water resources staff.
B.
Time of submittal. Statements of beneficial use must be submitted to water resources staff no less than
ten working days prior to the meeting of the water board at which the acceptability
of the water rights will be discussed.
C.
Nonsubmittal. Water rights, the use of which is not accounted for by statements of beneficial use,
will not be accepted for credit against raw water requirements unless specifically
reviewed by the water board and accepted by city council. All supporting information
concerning such water rights must be submitted to water resources staff no less than
ten working days prior to the meeting of the water board at which the acceptability
of the water rights will be discussed.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.010; Code 2009, § 14.05.090; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1)
A.
Calculation of allowance. The number of acre-feet per share of stock, undivided interest or percent ownership
in irrigation companies proposed or contemplated for transfer to the city which will
be allowed for application against the raw water requirements associated with any
land development activity will be as calculated by water resources staff based upon
historical data and pertinent decrees of the division 1 water court.
B.
Colorado-Big Thompson water allowance.
1.
The acre-foot allowance per unit of Colorado-Big Thompson water is 0.76 acre-feet
per unit.
2.
Colorado-Big Thompson water shall be transferred to city ownership in whole acre-foot
units only.
3.
Acceptance of Colorado-Big Thompson water shall only occur after approval of the transfer
of the allotment unit to the city by the board of directors of the Northern Colorado
Water Conservancy District as evidenced by the board's approval of a temporary use
permit for the allotment contract to the city.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.100; Code 2009, § 14.05.100; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1)
A.
Submittal of application. Applications for individual water service outside city limits will be processed and
reviewed pursuant to section 14.09.020 .
B.
Requirements upon approval.
1.
For those applications which are approved by city council, the following requirements
shall apply:
a.
For each three-quarter inch or one-inch water tap, in addition to the payment of the
regular outside tap fees, there shall be transferred to the city the equivalent of
two acre-feet of water. Water will be supplied by providing water rights from the
tabulation of water rights in section 14.05.060.A; cash in lieu of water rights; or
any combination thereof.
b.
For each water tap larger than one inch, in addition to the payment of the regular
outside tap fees, there shall be transferred to the city an amount of water equivalent
to the estimated annual use as confirmed by city staff and approved by the water board.
Water will be supplied by providing water rights from the tabulation of water rights
in section 14.05.060.A; cash in lieu of water rights; or any combination thereof.
In no case will the raw water requirements be less than three acre-feet of water.
c.
In the case of a request to increase tap size with no change in tap location, the
raw water requirements will be the difference between current requirements for the
existing tap and the requirements for the requested tap. Other fees will be based
on the differences in current fees between the existing and requested tap. The applicant
will also be responsible for any other extraordinary costs associated with the change
in tap size.
d.
The actual cash amounts to be paid in lieu of water rights transfers will be determined
by the water board and approved by city council coincidental with approval of the
application.
C.
Additional requirements.
1.
All applicants for water service outside city limits will be required to execute an
application for inclusion of the property to be served by the water tap within the
municipal subdistrict, Northern Colorado Water Conservancy District.
2.
The landowners will be required to enter into a contractual agreement with the city
to ensure adherence to the provisions of this chapter.
3.
The city does not guarantee or warranty that the water service provided to extraterritorial
water service taps will have sufficient pressure or volume for the intended purpose
of the tap.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.110; Code 2009, § 14.05.110; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1)
A.
Landowner responsibility.
1.
It is the responsibility of the landowner to provide all documentation required by
this chapter and/or requested by the city council, water board, or water resources
staff.
2.
It is the responsibility of the landowner or an authorized representative to attend
any and all meetings of the water board at which the land development activity or
request for water service is to be reviewed. Failure to attend may result in postponement
of consideration of the activity or request.
3.
All matters requiring review or consideration by the water board will be first submitted
to water resources staff for scheduling and determination of adequacy of information.
B.
Unconditional transfer. Water rights transferred to the city shall be free of conditional or contractual
restrictions, liens or encumbrances of any kind whatsoever.
C.
Pre-existing agreements. In those cases where the provisions of this chapter are not in concurrence with the
terms and conditions of written agreements executed by the city prior to adoption
or revision of this chapter, the terms and conditions of such agreements will control.
D.
City authority. The city water board shall be the sole authority in determining the acre-foot credits
which staff will allow for any and all water rights proposed for transfer in satisfaction
of the requirements of this chapter and shall have the right to accept or reject any
such water right. The city shall be the final authority determining either acceptance
or refusal of any water right proposed to be transferred to the city.
E.
Right of review. Landowners seeking approval of development activity in the city may request that
the water board review any staff action or determination pursuant to this chapter.
Any such landowner must submit the request for water board review, and any supporting
backup material, to water resources staff no less than ten working days prior to the
meeting of the water board at which such review will occur.
(Code 1993, § 14.05.120; Code 2009, § 14.05.120; Ord. No. O-2004-15, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2005-99, § 1; Ord. No. O-2012-73, § 2, 10-23-2012)
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter shall have the meanings
ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates a different
meaning:
Annual date means the date established by the city of when required tests and inspections must
be completed and proof submitted to the city. If the annual date falls on a Saturday,
Sunday or legal holiday, the annual date will be the next regular business day.
Approved backflow prevention assembly means a backflow prevention assembly described in Foundation for Cross-Connection
Control and Hydraulic Research's (FCCC & HR) most current list of approved backflow
prevention assemblies or American Society of Sanitary Engineers (ASSE).
Backflow means the undesirable reversal of the direction of flow of water or mixtures of water
and other liquids, gases, or other substances into the distribution pipes of the potable
water supply from any source caused by backpressure and/or backsiphonage.
Backflow preventer means a device designed to prevent backflow created by backpressure or backsiphonage.
Backpressure means backflow caused by a pump, elevated tank, boiler or means that could create
an elevated pressure within the nonpotable system greater than the supply pressure.
Backsiphonage means the flow of water or other liquids, mixtures or substances into the distribution
pipes of a potable water supply system from any source other than its intended source
caused by negative or sub-atmospheric pressure in the potable water supply system.
Certified cross-connection control technician means a person who has proven his or her competency to the satisfaction of the state
department of health and possesses a valid backflow prevention assembly tester certification
from one of the following approved organizations: American Society of Sanitary Engineers
(ASSE) or the American Backflow Prevention Association (ABPA).
Containment means the installation of an approved backflow prevention device, or method, on the
water service line, so that water delivered to the service line cannot return to the
city potable water distribution system due to any backpressure and/or backsiphonage
condition which might arise.
Contamination means an impairment of the quality of the potable water by sewage, industrial fluids
or waste liquids, compounds or other materials to a degree which creates an actual
hazard to the public health through poisoning or through the spread of disease.
Cross-connection means any physical arrangement whereby a public water supply is connected, directly
or indirectly, with any other water supply system, sewer, drain, conduit, pool, storage
reservoir, plumbing fixture or other device which contains, or may contain, contaminated
water, sewage or other waste or liquid of unknown or unsafe quality which may be capable
of imparting contamination to the public water supply as a result of backflow. Bypass
arrangements, jumper connections, removable sections, swivel or changeover devices,
four-way valve connections, and other temporary or permanent devices through which,
or because of which, backflow could occur are considered to be cross-connections.
Director means the city manager or the city manager's duly authorized representative.
Hazard, degree of, means an evaluation of the potential risk to public health and the adverse effect
of the hazard upon the potable water system.
Isolation means a backflow device installed on a branch of the internal plumbing to protect
the customers water system.
Nonpotable water means water that is not safe for human consumption or that is of questionable quality.
Pollution means the presence of any foreign substance (organic, inorganic, radiological or
biological) in the water that may degrade the water quality so as to constitute a
hazard or impair its usefulness.
Potable water means water free from impurities in amounts sufficient to cause disease or harmful
physiological effects. The bacteriological, chemical and radiological quality shall
conform with state department of health and environment primary drinking water regulations.
Responsible party means the individual or entity billed for water service provided by the city, or
the owner of the property being served by city water. If the individual or entity
being billed for water service and the owner of the property being served are different,
the owner of the property shall be solely responsible for all requirements in this
chapter.
State of Colorado Department of Public Health and Environment Regulation 11.39 , Backflow and Cross-Connection Control Guidance (or "guidance document") means a manual, as amended, that has been published by the state addressing cross-connection
control practices which will be used by the utility in implementing a cross-connection
control program.
Utility means the city water/wastewater utility.
Water service connection means the terminal end of the city's service connection from the public potable water
system; i.e., where the city loses jurisdiction and sanitary control over the water
at its point of delivery to the customer's stop box or shut-off valve or meter, whichever
comes first from the utility water main. If a meter is installed at the end of the
service connection, then the service connection shall mean the downstream end of the
meter. There shall be no unprotected takeoffs from the service line ahead of any meter
or backflow prevention device located at the point of delivery to the customer's water
system. Service connection shall also include water service connection from a fire
hydrant and all other temporary or emergency water service connections from the public
potable water system. For customers outside the city limits, the term "water service
connection" means the terminal end of the city's service connection from the public
potable water system to the customer's corporation stop.
(Code 1993, § 14.06.010; Code 2009, § 14.06.010; Ord. No. O-2003-88, § 1; Ord. No. O-2023-43 , § 2, 9-26-2023)
A.
Establishment of cross-connection control program.
1.
The director is vested with the authority and responsibility for implementing and
enforcing an effective cross-connection control program in accordance with this chapter
and in accordance with Colorado Department of Public Health and Environment Regulation 11.39 , as may be amended from time to time, and other applicable laws and regulations.
2.
If, in the director's judgment, an approved backflow prevention device is required
at connection of the city's water service to any responsible party's premises for
the safety of the water system, the director shall give notice in writing to the responsible
party to install such an approved backflow prevention device at each service connection
to the responsible party's premises.
3.
No water service connection to any premises shall be installed or maintained unless
the water supply is protected as required by applicable laws, the regulations of the
Colorado Department of Public Health and Environment, and by this chapter.
B.
Inspections and testing procedures.
1.
The responsible party, at any premises where containment backflow preventers are installed,
shall have operational tests conducted upon installation and at least annually thereafter.
The responsible party shall ensure that such test is completed and proof provided
to the city no later than the annual date. These operational tests, including any
applicable fees to submit such information to the city, shall be made at the expense
of the responsible party and must be performed by a certified cross-connection control
technician. Records of such tests, repairs, and replacement, including materials and
parts changed, shall be kept by the responsible party and reported within ten days
of such activities to the city.
2.
The director reserves the right to require certified tests at more frequent intervals
if the director deems the potential hazard to be great enough. These certified inspections
and operational tests shall be made at the expense of the responsible party and will
be performed by a certified cross-connection control technician.
3.
The director reserves the right, at any time, to test or inspect the installation,
operation, repair, replacement, or relocation of any containment or isolation assembly,
device, or method. Further, the director reserves the right to inspect installed backflow
preventers, cross-connections, or other structural or sanitary hazards at any time
to ensure the devices are in proper working order and to ensure compliance with this
chapter and applicable regulations. Such tests and inspections may be made at the
expense of the responsible party.
4.
A required backflow prevention assembly or method shall be repaired, replaced, or
tested at the expense of the responsible party, if the assembly or method is found
to be defective, malfunctioning, or not approved by the director for the degree of
hazard.
C.
Installations of backflow prevention devices.
1.
Backflow preventers shall be installed at or near the property line or immediately
inside the structure being served; but in all cases before the first branch line leading
off the service line.
2.
With the director's approval, a backflow preventer may be installed to isolate a hazard
and lower the degree of hazard for containment.
3.
All backflow prevention devices shall be located and installed according to the manufacturer's
specifications and in accordance with this chapter and the city design standards and
construction specifications or the state cross-connection guidance document. The director
shall review and approve all plans as to the location and installation of backflow
prevention devices.
4.
If the location of the backflow preventer results in the meter becoming contaminated,
the responsible party shall bear the cost of decontamination.
D.
Standards and testing for backflow prevention devices.
1.
Any approved backflow prevention assembly required in this chapter shall be of a model
and size approved by the director and approved by either the Foundation for Cross
Connection Control and Hydraulic Research's List of Approved Backflow Prevention Assemblies,
as amended and/or the American Society of Sanitary Engineers (ASSE).
(Code 1993, § 14.06.020; Code 2009, § 14.06.020; Ord. No. O-2003-88, § 1; Ord. No. O-2023-43 , § 3, 9-26-2023)
A.
Installation of required devices.
1.
All installations of backflow prevention devices, or any other devices as required
under this chapter, including types and models of such devices, and installation configurations
shall comply with any city guidance or policies concerning water distribution, or
as may be determined by the director.
2.
The responsible party shall, at its expense, purchase and install the device and any
necessary plumbing or construction. Such plumbing and construction shall meet all
provisions of the state cross-connection guidance document and the city design standards
and construction specifications and must be inspected and approved by the utility.
The device, including any device installed prior to the effective date of the ordinance
from which this section is derived, shall be the property of the responsible party.
B.
Compliance required.
1.
The utility will take necessary action to ensure compliance with the state department
of public health & environment, primary drinking water regulations, regulation 11,
and C.R.S. § 25-1.5-202, as amended.
2.
The responsible party of the premises is required to permit entry to the premises
for inspection, testing and maintenance purposes at reasonable times. Failure to permit
entry to the premises will result in the premises being regarded as a high hazard,
and a reduced-pressure-principle device will be required to protect the city's water
system, and service may be discontinued in accordance with chapter 14.20 .
3.
The responsible party is required to provide all necessary plumbing and construction
needed for the installation of the device. Failure to provide such required plumbing
and construction will result in action being taken as provided for in section 14.06.040 .
(Code 1993, § 14.06.030; Code 2009, § 14.06.030; Ord. No. O-2003-88, § 1; Ord. No. O-2023-43 , § 4, 9-26-2023)
A.
Right of entry.
1.
The director reserves the right and has the authority, at any reasonable time, to
enter a premises to inspect or test any backflow prevention assembly or method owned
and maintained by a responsible party to determine whether the connection requires
a backflow prevention device and to determine the extent and degree of hazard.
B.
Annual testing and inspection compliance procedures.
1.
A notice of reminder will be issued to the responsible party at least 45 days in advance
of the annual date to ensure that any required testing or inspection is completed,
and proof submitted to the city no later than the annual date.
2.
Upon failure of a responsible party to comply with the requirements as set forth in
this chapter, the responsible party may be subject to the following actions:
a.
In the event that the responsible party does not complete and submit proof of any
required testing or inspection to the city by the annual date, a written notice of
violation will be mailed or personally served to the last known address of the responsible
party. The notice shall describe the nature of the violation and clearly state the
amount of any fee along with the due date. The responsible party shall have 15 days
from the date of the notice of violation to comply with the requirements herein. If
the responsible party does not comply with the requirements herein within 15 days
from the date of the notice of violation, the city shall perform the testing or inspection
subject to reimbursement of costs plus a 25 percent surcharge, which will be added
to the water bill for the property as a service fee. The responsible party will be
required to schedule and permit entry to the premises to the city to perform the required
testing or inspection. Failure to reasonably respond to the city or permit entry to
the city for any required testing or inspection may result in civil penalties as provided
in this chapter.
b.
In the event that the responsible party does not comply with the requirements of this
chapter within 90 days of the annual date, service may be discontinued in accordance
with chapter 14.20 .
3.
A responsible party receiving a notice of violation that does not contain civil penalties,
as provided in subsection D below, may appeal in writing to the director within ten
days following the date of the notice. Upon receipt of the appeal, the director may
modify the notice of violation to include a revised schedule to correct the cause
of the violation, if the director finds that just cause exists for such modification.
C.
Discontinuance of service by the city.
1.
Further, when, as a result of an inspection, a condition involving violation of this
chapter resulting in a health or sanitary hazard is determined to exist, or a responsible
party fails to properly test, repair, or otherwise maintain a backflow preventer as
required by this chapter, the director shall have the option of immediate discontinuance
of water service to the premises until the condition has been corrected, or the director
may specify a date for compliance after which time the director may discontinue service
until the responsible party has corrected the condition or defects. Any such discontinuance
of service is governed by chapter 14.20 .
D.
Civil penalties.
1.
It is unlawful and an offense for any person to violate this chapter, or to cause
or allow such a violation. Violations of this chapter may be subject to civil penalties
of up to $1,000.00 for each violation. In addition, any costs incurred by the city,
including reasonable attorneys' fees, due to violations subject to civil penalty,
may be added to the total amount of the civil penalty assessment. Nothing in this
section shall prevent the city from acting or seeking relief under any other sections
of this chapter for any violations of this chapter.
a.
Should a violation of this chapter be subject to civil penalty, the director shall
send a notice of violation to the responsible party via regular mail to the last known
address of the responsible party or via personal service. The notice of violation
shall describe the nature of the violation and clearly state the amount of the civil
penalty along with the due date and possible consequences of nonpayment in accordance
with this chapter. The notice may provide for additional time for correction of the
cause of the violation.
b.
Any person who is the recipient of a notice of violation with a civil penalty assessment
pursuant to this chapter may appeal the notice in writing to the director on or before
the expiration of ten days following the date of the notice. Upon receipt of the appeal,
the director will meet with the person and allow the person to present evidence or
reasons why the person believes that the violations are not subject to civil penalty
or present reasons for reductions in the civil penalty amount. Based on the evidence
and reasons, the director may reduce the amount of civil penalty, require additional
corrective actions of the party, or take other administrative actions as deemed necessary.
c.
Should the person not agree with the director's determination, the party may appeal
in writing within ten days of such determination. Any notice of appeal shall be submitted
to the director and a copy thereof to the office of the city attorney. The director
shall appoint a hearing officer to hear and determine the matters subject to appeal
and determination by this chapter. The hearing officer shall not be an employee of
the department of public works and utilities. The hearing shall be conducted in compliance
with chapter 2.98 .
d.
Civil penalties not paid and received by the city within 30 days of the latter of
the issuance of the notice of civil penalty, the director's determination pursuant
to subsection b of this section, or the hearing officer's determination pursuant to
subsection C of this section, shall be deemed delinquent. Delinquent civil penalties
are subject to delinquency charges of 1½ percent per month, compounded quarterly.
e.
The city has the right to recover on all charges, penalties, fees, or expenses owed
according to this chapter in any manner provided by law in any court of competent
jurisdiction.
E.
Fees, charges, and expenses to be lien upon premises.
1.
All charges, penalties, fees, or expenses according to this chapter shall be a lien
upon the premises, from the date the same becomes due until paid. The city may collect
such liens and liabilities by an action at law or equity to enforce the lien.
2.
In addition to an action at law or equity to enforce the lien, the city finance director,
or designee, may certify such charges, penalties, fees, or expenses as an assessment
to the office of the treasurer of the county that includes the premises, for collection
in the same manner as the collection of general property taxes. Upon receipt of the
assessment roll certified by the director of finance, or designee, the county treasurer
shall collect the amounts so assessed in the same manner as the collection of general
property taxes. The city finance director, or designee, may accept payment of the
amount of assessment before certification to the county treasurer. After certification,
only the county treasurer may accept payment.
(Code 1993, § 14.06.040; Code 2009, § 14.06.040; Ord. No. O-2003-88, § 1; Ord. No. O-2023-43 , § 4, 9-26-2023; Ord. No. O-2024-36 , § 2, 5-28-2024)
The purpose of this chapter is to foster redevelopment in areas which the city council
has identified as redevelopment or revitalization areas, to recognize when existing
water and sewer infrastructure is sufficient to support redevelopment, and to acknowledge
that fees or improvements paid previously to service an original development may,
in some circumstances, not need to be repaid to service a redevelopment in the same
location.
(Code 2009, § 14.07.010; Ord. No. O-2014-25, § 1, 6-10-2014)
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter shall have the meanings
ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates a different
meaning. All other terms shall be defined as in chapter 15.10 :
Associated lot means a lot:
(1)
Any portion of which is within 500 feet of any portion of the primary redevelopment
lot;
(2)
Which is under the same ownership as the primary redevelopment lot at the time of
approval of credit; and
(3)
Which has been held in the same ownership as the primary redevelopment lot for at
least five consecutive years within ten years prior to approval of credit under this
chapter.
Capacity means the average annual water use on a primary redevelopment lot from an eligible
tap over a five-year period which, in the determination of the city manager or his
designee, is representative of the primary redevelopment lot's water use when the
pre-redevelopment use of the land was ongoing, provided that such five-year period
not extend more than ten years prior to approval of a credit under this chapter.
Credit means part or all of a subject fee which is considered received.
Director means the public works and natural resources director, or other designee of the city
manager.
Eligible tap is a water tap at least three inches in diameter and does not include water service
provided pursuant to a water service contract or terminable water use agreement.
Primary redevelopment lot means a lot on which a redevelopment is proposed to occur, which may have more capacity
than necessary to service the redevelopment.
Redevelopment means site development which, in the determination of the city manager or his designee:
(1)
Is within an urban renewal area or downtown development authority area;
(2)
Is consistent with a revitalization or urban renewal plan approved by the city council;
and
(3)
Is replacing pre-existing structures or site uses (other than vacant or agricultural
uses) that existed on the same lot before the development.
Subject fees means water system development fees as described in section 14.04.160 , the surcharge on water fees as described in section 14.04.210 , and sewer development fees as described in section 14.08.605 .
Tap credit agreement means an agreement setting forth rights and responsibilities of the owner and the
applicant under this chapter, consistent with the terms and purpose of this chapter.
(Code 2009, § 14.07.020; Ord. No. O-2014-25, § 1, 6-10-2014; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018)
Where a primary redevelopment lot has more capacity than necessary to service a redevelopment
on such lot, credit shall be assigned toward subject fees for redevelopment on associated
lots to the extent of the excess capacity, if, in the determination of the city manager
or his designee:
(1)
Existing infrastructure is adequate to serve such redevelopment;
(2)
Such credit will not negatively impact the city's ability to provide water or sewer
service; and
(3)
Such redevelopment will not result in greater demands on the water or sewer system
in the general area of the redevelopment.
(Code 2009, § 14.07.030; Ord. No. O-2014-25, § 1, 6-10-2014)
A.
Approval of credit. The owner of a redevelopment lot may request a credit under this chapter at any time
prior to payment of any subject fee, by submitting the request with a concept plan
and any other relevant materials city staff determines to be necessary. The director
may approve the credit in writing if it meets the requirements of this chapter. A
building permit may be issued without payment of credited subject fees for redevelopment
which, in the determination of the city manager or designee, is consistent with the
concept plan.
B.
Tap credit agreement. As a prerequisite to approving a request for credit, the director may require a tap
credit agreement as necessary to describe in greater detail the implementation of
this chapter. The director shall prepare the tap credit agreement in conjunction with
other applicable city departments and then present it to the applicant. The applicant
and the director shall execute the tap credit agreement.
(Code 2009, § 14.07.040; Ord. No. O-2014-25, § 1, 6-10-2014)
A credit is not refundable. Even if the credit exceeds the subject fee itself, that
shall not result in the return of money to the owner. The city will not refund any
money for available, potential, unused, partially unused, or any other credits of
any description, for any reason, to any person.
(Code 2009, § 14.07.050; Ord. No. O-2014-25, § 1, 6-10-2014)
A credit approved for a particular redevelopment shall lapse automatically if, after
one year of approval of the credit, there has been no application for a building permit
which, in the determination of the city manager or designee, is consistent with the
concept plan. A credit which has lapsed shall be null and void. The one-year period
may be extended, before its expiration, according to the procedures described in section
15.02.040.H, or as described in a tap credit agreement executed pursuant to section
14.07.040.B.
Notwithstanding any provision to the contrary in this Code, if a credit in a tap credit
agreement has lapsed for good cause in the determination of the city manager or designee,
the director may approve a one-time amendment to such tap credit agreement extending
the time by which the lapsed credit may be applied to qualifying fees at the time
of issuance of a building permit. Such one-time amendment to a tap credit agreement
must be executed no later than December 31, 2024.
(Code 2009, § 14.07.060; Ord. No. O-2014-25, § 1, 6-10-2014; Ord. No. O-2015-17, §
4, 3-17-2015; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018; Ord. No. O-2024-25 , § 2, 4-9-2024)
A.
This chapter has as its purpose the protection of public health, safety and the environment
by providing for the collection and treatment of domestic and nondomestic wastewater
and abating and preventing pollution. Pollution control shall be implemented through
regulation and control of the quantity and quality of industrial, commercial and other
wastes admitted or discharged into the sewage collection system and wastewater treatment
plant under the jurisdiction of the city to enable the city to comply with all applicable
state and federal laws required by the federal Clean Water Act of 1977, the General
Pretreatment Regulations (40 CFR 403) and any subsequent amendments thereto.
B.
The objectives of this chapter are:
1.
To protect the health, safety and welfare of the POTW personnel and the general public;
2.
To make provisions for the collection and treatment of all wastewater generated by
domestic and nondomestic sources under the jurisdiction of the city;
3.
To protect the city's wastewater collection and treatment system from pollutants which
may interfere with the operation of the system or contaminate the biosolids or other
waste products generated as a result of operating the system;
4.
To prevent pollutants from entering the wastewater treatment and collection system
which could pass through the system, inadequately treated, into the receiving waters;
5.
To improve the opportunity to recycle and reclaim wastewater and biosolids from the
system;
6.
To meet federal and state standards, regulations, and requirements for collection,
treatment, and reuse of wastewater and waste products such as biosolids;
7.
To generate sufficient revenues to operate, maintain and construct the wastewater
collection and treatment system.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.010; Code 2009, § 14.08.010; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 1(14.08.010))
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter shall have the meanings
ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates a different
meaning:
Act (also Clean Water Act) means the federal Water Pollution Control Act, Law 92-500,
as amended.
Aliquot means a sample removed from the total sample which is truly representative of the
total sample.
Approval authority means the director of the approved state pretreatment program or the EPA regional
administrator.
Authorized or duly authorized representative of the industrial user means:
1.
A principal executive officer of at least the level of vice president, if the industrial
user is a corporation.
2.
The manager authorized to make management decisions which govern the operation of
the regulated facility, including: making major capital investment recommendations
or other comprehensive measures to ensure long-term environmental compliance with
environmental laws and regulations; ensuring that the necessary systems are established
or actions taken to gather complete and accurate information for control mechanism
requirements; or where authority to sign documents has been assigned or delegated
to the manager in accordance with corporate procedures.
3.
A general partner or proprietor if the industrial user is a partnership or proprietorship,
respectively.
4.
A duly authorized representative of the above-designated individual, if the authorization
is submitted in writing to the control authority by the above-designated individual
and the individual or position is responsible for the overall operation of the facilities
from which the wastewater discharge originates or has overall responsibility for environmental
matters for the company.
Best management practices (BMPs) means schedules of activities, recommended or prohibited practices, maintenance procedures,
and other management practices to comply with the prohibitions listed in sections 14.08.310 and 14.08.312 . BMPs also include treatment requirements, operating procedures, and practices to
control site runoff, spillage or leaks, sludge or waste disposal, or drainage from
raw materials storage. Best management practices (BMPs) may be developed to implement
the provisions of this chapter. Such BMPs shall be considered local limits and pretreatment
standards for the purposes of this chapter and section 307(d) of the Act.
Biosolids has the same meaning as that same term has under the state department of health and
environment water quality control commission biosolids regulation, 4.9.0 (5 CCR 1002-19),
as that regulation may be amended or restated, from time to time.
BOD (denoting biochemical oxygen demand ) means the quantity of oxygen utilized in the biochemical oxidation of organic matter
under standard laboratory procedure in five days at 20 degrees Celsius expressed in
milligrams per liter.
Building drain means that part of the lowest horizontal piping of a drainage system which receives
the discharge from soil, waste and other drainage pipes inside the walls of the building
and conveys it to the building sewer.
Building sewer (also house connection or lateral sewer) means the extension from the building drain, beginning two feet outside the foundation
wall to the public sewer or other place of disposal.
Bypass means the diversion of wastestreams from any portion of an industrial user's treatment
facility or regulated outfall.
Categorical industrial user means an industrial user subject to categorical pretreatment standards.
Categorical pretreatment standard (also categorical standard) means any regulation containing pollutant discharge limits promulgated by the United
States Environmental Protection Agency in accordance with sections 307(b) and (c)
of the Act (33 USC 1317) that apply to a specific category of users and that appear
in 40 CFR chapter I, subchapter N, parts 405-471.
COD (denoting chemical oxygen demand) means a measure of the oxygen-consuming capacity of organic or inorganic matter present
in water or wastewater, expressed in milligrams per liter, for a chemical oxidant
in a specific laboratory procedure.
Commercial or institutional users means all nonresidential users which introduce only sanitary sewage or primarily
segregated domestic wastes into a building sewer.
Compliance schedule means any schedule for meeting a compliance deadline required by the city, state
or United States Environmental Protection Agency.
Compliance monitoring sample means any sample collected to complete baseline monitoring reports, 90-day compliance
reports or self-monitoring reports.
Composite sample means a representative flow-proportioned or time-proportioned sample collected during
normal hours of process operation over a period representative of process discharge
to the POTW sewer. A composite sample shall be composed of a minimum of eight individual
samples or as modified by the control authority.
Compound tap means more than one property or building, separately owned or separately used, connected
to a single building sewer.
Control authority means the city.
Conventional pollutant means biochemical oxygen demand, suspended solids, pH, fecal coliform bacteria plus
any additional pollutants identified in the POTW NPDES permit, where the POTW is designed
to treat such pollutants, and in fact does treat such pollutants to the degree required
by the NPDES permit limitations.
Cooling water means the water discharged from any use such as air conditioning, cooling, refrigeration
or any thermal heat exchange process to which the only pollutant added is heat.
Daily maximum means the maximum allowable discharge of a pollutant during a calendar day. Where
limitations are expressed in units of mass, the daily discharge is the total mass
discharged over the course of the day. Where limitations are expressed as concentration,
the daily discharge may be the arithmetic average, flow-weighted mean, or instantaneous
maximum of the pollutant concentration derived from all measurements taken that day.
Design standards refers to the city public improvement design standards and construction specifications,
as amended.
Direct discharge means any discharge of pollutants into any stream, lake, river, ditch, reservoir,
groundwater or other water body. The term "direct discharge" does not include discharge
to the POTW.
Director means the director of water and waste services of the City of Longmont or the director's
designee.
Discharger means any person who discharges or causes the discharge of wastewater into the POTW
sewer system.
Domestic wastewater means waterborne liquid wastes from the sanitary conveniences of dwellings, commercial
buildings or institutions and industrial facilities. These wastes include human body
waste or excrement, household-type wastes including bath, toilet, laundry and kitchen
wastes from the noncommercial handling, preparation and cooking of food and other
similar wastes.
EPA means the United States Environmental Protection Agency.
Excessive maintenance means any maintenance required beyond normal sewer utilities maintenance schedules
for any portion of the sanitary sewer lines. Normal maintenance is considered to be
cleaning or jetting once a year.
Existing source means any source of discharge to the POTW that is not defined as a new source.
Food establishment means any facility involved in the preparation of food stuffs for sale to the general
public.
Garbage means the putrescible animal and vegetable waste resulting from the handling, preparation,
cooking and dispensing of food and from the handling, storage and sale of produce.
Grab sample means an instantaneous dip sample taken from a waste stream without consideration
for flow or time. A grab sample shall be representative of the discharge at the time
of sampling.
Grease interceptor means a passive interceptor having a rated flow exceeding 50 gallons per minute and
that is located outside the building.
Grease trap means a passive interceptor having a rated flow less than 50 gallons per minute and
that is located inside the building.
Holding tank sewage means any wastewater from holding tanks such as vessels, chemical toilets, campers,
trailers, septic tanks, sealed vaults and vacuum-pump tank trucks.
Indirect discharge means the introduction of pollutants into a POTW from any nondomestic source regulated
under sections 307(b), (c) or (d) of the Act.
Industrial user means a source of indirect discharge.
Industrial waste means the water-carried wastes from industrial manufacturing or industrial processing,
as distinct from sanitary sewage. The term "industrial waste" includes the trade wastes
produced by, but not limited to, food processing and bottling plants, food manufacturing
plants, slaughtering plants, tallow works, plating works, disposal services, industrial
cleaning plants, fertilizer plants, car and truck washing operations, laundries, cleaning
establishments, cooling plants, chemical plants and chemical treatment installations.
Instantaneous limit means the maximum concentration of a pollutant allowed to be discharged at any time,
determined from the analysis of any grab or composited sample collected, independent
of the industrial flow rate and the duration of the sampling event.
Interceptor means any grease interceptor, grease trap, oil separator or sand interceptor.
Interference means a discharge which, alone or in conjunction with a discharge from other sources,
both:
1.
Inhibits or disrupts the POTW, its treatment processes or operations or its sludge
processes, use or disposal; and
2.
Therefor is a cause of a violation of any requirement of the POTW's NPDES permit (including
an increase in the magnitude or duration of a violation) or of the prevention of sewage
sludge use or disposal in compliance with the following statutory provisions and regulations
or permits issued thereunder (or more stringent state or local regulations): Section
405 of the Act, the Solid Waste Disposal Act (SWDA) (including title II, more commonly
referred to as the Resource Conservation and Recovery Act (RCRA), and including state
regulations contained in any state sludge management plan prepared pursuant to subtitle
D of the SWDA), the Clean Air Act, the Toxic Substances Control Act, and the Marine
Protection, Research and Sanctuaries Act.
International Plumbing Code means the currently adopted plumbing standards as set forth in chapter 16.16 .
Local limits means the limitations on pollutants set forth under section 14.08.312 .
Mass limit or mass loading means the total number of pounds of a pollutant allowed to be discharged during a
specified time period.
May is permissive.
Monthly average means the arithmetic mean of the concentration values of a pollutant in the effluent
during a calendar month or as otherwise defined by the control authority.
Municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4) means publicly-owned facilities by which stormwater is collected and conveyed, including,
but not limited to, any roads with drainage systems, municipal streets, gutters, curbs,
catch basins, inlets, piped storm drains, pumping facilities, retention and detention
basins, and natural and human-made or altered drainage ditches, channels, lakes, reservoirs
or other drainage structures.
National Pretreatment Standard (also pretreatment standard or standard) means any regulation containing pollutant discharge limits promulgated by the United
States Environmental Protection Agency in accordance with section 307(b) and (c) of
the Act which applies to industrial users. The term "National Pretreatment Standard"
includes prohibitive discharge limits established pursuant to 40 CFR 403.5.
National Prohibitive Discharge Standard (also prohibitive discharge standard) means any regulation developed under the authority of section 307(b) of the Act and
the general pretreatment regulations (40 CFR 403.5).
Natural outlet means any outlet into a watercourse, pond, ditch, lake or other body of surface water
or groundwater.
New source means any building, structure, facility or installation from which there is or may
be a discharge of pollutants, the construction of which commenced after the publication
of proposed pretreatment standards under section 307(c) of the Act which will be applicable
to such source if such standards are thereafter promulgated in accordance with that
section, provided that:
1.
The building, structure, facility or installation is constructed at a site at which
no other source is located;
2.
The building, structure, facility or installation totally replaces the process or
production equipment that causes the discharge of pollutants at an existing source;
or
3.
The production or wastewater generating processes of the building, structure, facility
or installation are substantially independent of an existing source at the same site.
In determining whether these are substantially independent, factors such as the extent
to which the new facility is integrated with the existing plant, and the extent to
which the new facility is engaged in the same general type of activity as the existing
source should be considered.
NPDES permit (denoting National Pollution Discharge Elimination System permit) means a permit issued pursuant to section 402 of the Clean Water Act, including permits
issued by the state in compliance with the Act.
Oil separator means a device that separates and retains oil, grease, and flammable wastes while
permitting wastewater to discharge to the sewer.
Pass through means a discharge which exits the POTW into waters of the United States in quantities
or concentrations which, alone or in conjunction with a discharge or discharges from
other sources, is a cause of a violation of any requirement of the POTW's NPDES permit
(including an increase in the magnitude or duration of a violation).
Person means any individual, partnership, proprietorship, firm, company, corporation, association,
trust, estate, governmental entity or any other legal entity, or their legal representatives,
agents or assigns. For the purposes of this chapter, the masculine gender shall be
construed as including the feminine and references to the singular shall include the
plural where indicated by the context.
pH is a measure of the acidic or basic properties of a substance and is defined as the
logarithm, to the base 10, of the reciprocal of the concentration of hydrogen ions
in grams per liter of solution.
Pollutant means any sewage, sewage biosolids, garbage, chemical waste, biological material,
medical wastes, solid waste, dredged soil, incinerator residue, ash, munitions, radioactive
material, heat, rock, sand, cellar dirt and industrial, municipal and agricultural
wastes discharged into the water.
POTW(denoting publicly owned treatment works) means a treatment works as defined by section 212 of the Act, which is owned by the
state a municipality (as defined by section 502(4) of the Act). The term "POTW" includes
any devices and systems used in the storage, treatment, recycling and reclamation
of municipal sewage or industrial wastes of a liquid nature. The term "POTW" also
includes any sewers, pipes and other conveyances only if they convey wastewater to
the POTW treatment plant. The term "POTW" also means the municipality, as defined
in section 502(4) of the Act, which has jurisdiction over the indirect discharges
to and the discharges from such a treatment works.
Pretreatment means the reduction of the amount of pollutants, the elimination of pollutants or
the alteration of the nature of pollutant properties in wastewater to a less harmful
state prior to discharging such pollutants into the POTW sewer system. The reduction
or alteration of pollutants can be obtained by physical, chemical or biological processes,
except as prohibited by 40 CFR 403.6(d).
Pretreatment requirements means any substantive or procedural requirement related to pretreatment imposed on
an industrial user by the control authority, other than a National Pretreatment Standard.
Pretreatment standards (also standards) means prohibited discharge standards, categorical pretreatment standards, and local
limits.
Primarily segregated domestic wastes means that sewage which is introduced into a building sewer and which contains no
more than 50 percent industrial waste, prior to any intentional dilution.
Private sewage disposal system means any system or facility for treating, neutralizing, stabilizing or disposing
of sewage which is not a part of or connected to the POTW.
Prohibited discharge standards (also prohibited discharges) means absolute prohibitions against the discharge of certain substances.
Properly shredded garbage means the wastes from the preparation, cooking and dispensing of food, that have
been shredded to such a degree that all particles will be carried freely under the
flow conditions normally prevailing in public sewers, with no particle greater than
one-half inch (1.27 centimeters) in any dimension.
Public sewer system (also POTW sewer system) means the sewer pipes, chambers, structures and appurtenances in publicly owned land
or easements in which all owners of abutting property have equal rights and which
are controlled by the city.
Regulated outfall means the location defined in the permit where compliance monitoring samples are
collected.
Reserve capacity means the unused portion of the city's POTW capacity that is set aside for future
users resulting from future growth and development within the POTW service area.
Sand interceptor means a device that separates and retains heavy solids while permitting wastewater
to discharge to the sewer.
Sanitary sewage means the water-carried wastes from residences, hotels, restaurants, eating houses
or from business establishments or premises engaged solely in the sale, storage or
repair of goods, wares or merchandise, and which contains properly shredded garbage,
human wastes or animal wastes.
Sanitary sewer means the pipe or conduit system used for the collection and transportation of sewage
and into which stormwater, surface water and groundwater are not intentionally admitted.
Septage means the solid and liquid wastes from septic tanks, vault toilets, portable toilets
or similar facilities.
Sewage. See Wastewater.
Shall is mandatory.
Significant industrial user means any industrial user that:
1.
Is subject to the national categorical pretreatment standards under 40 CFR 403.6 and
40 CFR chapter I, subchapter N;
2.
Discharges an average of 25,000 gallons per day or more of process wastewater to the
city's wastewater treatment system (excluding sanitary, noncontact cooling water and
boiler blowdown wastewater);
3.
Contributes a process waste stream which makes up five percent or more of the average
dry weather hydraulic or organic capacity of the city's wastewater treatment plant;
or
4.
Is designated as such by the city on the basis that the industrial user has a reasonable
potential for adversely affecting the wastewater treatment plant's operation or for
violating any pretreatment standard or requirement.
Significant noncompliance. An industrial user is in significant noncompliance if its violation meets one or
more of the following criteria:
1.
Chronic violations of wastewater discharge limits, defined in this section as those
in which 66 percent or more of all the measurements taken for the same pollutant during
a six-month period exceed (by any magnitude) a numeric pretreatment standard or requirement,
including instantaneous limits;
2.
Technical review criteria (TRC) violations defined in this section as those in which
33 percent or more of all the measurements taken for the same pollutant parameter
during a six-month period equal or exceed the product of the numeric pretreatment
standard or requirement including instantaneous limits multiplied by the applicable
TRC (TRC = 1.4 for BOD, TSS, fats, oil and grease, and 1.2 for all other pollutants except pH);
3.
Any other violation of a pretreatment standard or requirement (daily maximum, long-term
average, instantaneous limit or narrative standard) that the director determines has
caused, alone or in combination with other discharges, interference or pass-through
(including endangering the health of POTW personnel or the general public);
4.
Any discharge of a pollutant that has caused imminent endangerment to human health,
welfare or to the environment or has resulted in the POTW's exercise of its emergency
authority to halt or prevent such a discharge;
5.
Failure to meet, within 90 days after the schedule date, a compliance schedule milestone
contained in a local control mechanism or enforcement order for starting construction,
completing construction or attaining final compliance;
6.
Failure to provide, within 30 days after the due date, required reports such as baseline
monitoring reports, 90-day compliance reports, periodic self-monitoring reports and
reports on compliance with compliance schedules;
7.
Failure to accurately report noncompliance;
8.
Any other violation or group of violations, including violations of best management
practices, which the director determines will adversely affect the operation or implementation
of the local pretreatment program;
9.
Failure to report an accidental, prohibited, or nonpermitted discharge.
Slug, slug load or slug discharge means any nonroutine, episodic nature discharge, including, but not limited to, accidental
spill or noncustomary batch discharge.
Standard industrial classification (SIC) means a classification pursuant to the most recent edition of the Standard Industrial
Classification Manual, issued by the executive office of the president, office of
management and budget.
Standard specifications. See Design standards.
Storm drainage system means and includes the municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4).
Stormwater means any surface flow, runoff and drainage consisting entirely of water from any
form of natural precipitation, and resulting from such precipitation.
TKN (denoting total Kjeldahl nitrogen) means the quantity of nitrogen in the forms of organic nitrogen and ammonia measured
by a standard test procedure using the Kjeldahl method.
Toxic pollutant means any pollutant or combination of pollutants regulated under provisions of section
307(a) of the Clean Water Act of 1977.
TSS (denoting total suspended solids) means total suspended matter that either floats on the surface of, or is in suspension
in, water, wastewater or other liquids, and that is removable by laboratory filtration
as prescribed by standardized methods and referred to as nonfilterable residue.
Upset means an exceptional incident in which there is unintentional and temporary noncompliance
with permit effluent limitations because of factors beyond the reasonable control
of the permittee. The term "upset" does not include noncompliance to the extent caused
by operational error, improperly designed treatment facilities, inadequate treatment
facilities, lack of preventive maintenance, or careless or improper operation.
User means any person who contributes, causes or permits the discharge of sewage or industrial
wastewater into the POTW sewer system.
Violation means any pollution concentration or mass loading which exceeds effluent limitations
defined by sections 14.08.312 or 14.08.313 or by a discharge permit issued under this chapter; any failure to provide adequate
and timely reports required by a permit; any failure to abide by any management conditions
required by a permit or this chapter; or any failure to abide by the terms, conditions
and restrictions of this chapter, a permit issued hereunder or applicable federal
or state regulations.
Wastewater (also sewage) means the water-carried and liquid wastes from dwellings, commercial buildings, institutions
and industrial facilities discharged to the POTW sewer system.
Wastewater classification survey means the questionnaire that each individual industrial user must complete and have
on file with the control authority before a permit can be issued to allow a discharge
of sewage or industrial wastes to the POTW sewer system.
Wastewater discharge permit or permit means the document issued to an industrial user in accordance with the terms and
provisions of this chapter stipulating requirements and limitations on wastewater
discharges to the POTW sewer system.
Wastewater strength means the quality of sewage as measured by its constituents and physical, chemical
and biological characteristics.
Watercourse means a natural or artificial channel for the passage of water either continuously
or intermittently.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.100; Code 2009, § 14.08.100; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-94-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-96-43, § 11; Ord. No. O-96-73, § 1; Ord. No.
O-98-15, § 2; Ord. No. O-99-10, § 6; Ord. No. O-2003-19, § 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78,
§ 2(14.08.100); Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 2, 3-19-2013; Ord. No. O-2024-03 , § 2, 1-9-2024)
It is unlawful for any person to place, deposit or permit to be deposited in any unsanitary
manner, on public or private property within the city or in any area under the jurisdiction
of the city, any human or animal excrement, garbage, contaminated water or material,
wastewater or other waste which constitutes a hazard to human health or a public nuisance.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.205; Code 2009, § 14.08.205; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
Except as provided for in section 14.08.240 , it is unlawful to construct or maintain any privy, privy vault, septic tank, cesspool
or other facility intended or used for the disposal of sewage.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.210; Code 2009, § 14.08.210; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
The owner of any house, building or property used for human occupancy, employment,
commercial enterprise, recreation or other purpose situated within the POTW service
area and abutting on or near any street, alley or public right-of-way in which there
is now located a public sanitary sewer is required, at the owner's expense, to install
suitable toilet facilities therein, and to connect such facilities directly with the
proper public sewer in accordance with the provisions of this chapter within 30 days
after the date of official notice to do so, provided that the public sewer system
is within 400 feet of the property line of the property or if the director or a county
or state public health agency determines that the existence of a private sewage disposal
system would constitute a public health hazard or contaminate public or private water
supplies or surface waters.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.215; Code 2009, § 14.08.215; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 3(14.08.215))
All wastewater installations shall be made in accordance with the design standards,
as adopted by the city council and currently in effect.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.220; Code 2009, § 14.08.220; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 4(14.08.220))
All costs and expenses incident to the installation and connection of sanitary facilities
and the building sewer shall be borne by the owner, or lessee if at the Vance Brand
Municipal Airport. The owner, or lessee if at the Vance Brand Municipal Airport, shall
indemnify the city from any loss or damage that may, directly or indirectly, be occasioned
by the installation of such facilities, except those damages directly attributed to
the negligence of the city. All such expenses shall constitute a lien against the
property. The city shall not be responsible for payment of any fees or rates for lessee
users at the Vance Brand Municipal Airport. The lessee at the Vance Brand Municipal
Airport shall be solely responsible for all costs and expenses incident to the installation
and connection of sanitary facilities.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.225; Code 2009, § 14.08.225; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-96-43, § 12)
Compound sewer taps shall not be permitted. A separate and independent building sewer
shall be provided for every building or complex of buildings under single ownership,
except as provided for in section 14.08.235 , for residential condominium or residential townhome ownership, or as provided for
master metering at Vance Brand Municipal Airport in section 14.04.290.C.5. The term
"residential townhome" means, for purposes of this chapter, three or more separately
owned dwelling units within one building structure sharing common walls, with each
unit of the building structure on a separate legal lot, and all common areas in homeowners'
association ownership. The term "residential condominium" means, for the purposes
of this chapter, three or more separately owned dwelling units, with the units often
stacked one above the other in a vertical configuration, sharing common vertical walls
and/or horizontal floors and ceilings with the entire structure, or combination of
multiple structures, situated on one legal lot. Each separate building structure shall
have a separate service line. Services for a residential townhome unit on a compound
tap must extend out from the building structure to a common service line which is
located at least five feet horizontally from the outside foundation of the building
structure. Service lines shall not be connected underneath or through one building
structure to the next.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.230; Code 2009, § 14.08.230; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-94-40, § 3; Ord. No. O-2000-53, § 2; Ord. No. O-2015-48, § 5, 10-13-2015)
A.
Compound building sewers may be permitted for residential condominiums or residential
townhomes in the city if the owner of the realty projected for residential condominium
or residential townhome development submits to the city, prior to site plan approval,
a declaration containing a covenant running with the land and binding upon the owners,
heirs, successors and assigns, that a homeowners' association, consisting of the owners
of each residential condominium or residential townhome unit, shall be jointly and
severally responsible for the sewer user charges attributable to the development and
any subsequent repairs or maintenance of the lines for the entire subdivision plat
or site plan. All such charges for residential condominium or residential townhome
units shall be based upon such rates and development fees applicable to multifamily
residential as otherwise provided by ordinance. Discontinuance of service for nonpayment
of such rates and development fees by the association shall be imposed upon the entirety
of the residential condominium or residential townhome development sharing the compound
tap.
B.
If the declaration containing the covenants described heretofore is not submitted
to the city by the owner of the realty to be developed as residential condominium
or residential townhome property, then each residential condominium or residential
townhome unit shall have a separate building sewer and no compound sewer shall be
permitted for such development.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.235; Code 2009, § 14.08.235; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-94-40, § 4)
Where a POTW sanitary sewer is not available under the provisions of section 14.08.215 , the building sewer shall be connected to a private sewage disposal system complying
with the provisions given below:
A.
Permit for a private system. Before commencement of construction of a private sewage disposal system, the owner
shall first obtain a written permit signed by the director. The application for such
permit shall be made on a form furnished by the city, which the applicant shall supplement
by any plans, specifications, and other information as is deemed necessary by the
director. A permit and inspection fee as given in section 14.08.680 shall be paid to the city at the time application is filed. A permit for a private
sewage disposal system shall not become effective until the installation is completed
to the satisfaction of the director. The city shall be allowed access to inspect the
work at any stage of construction and, in any event, the applicant for the permit
shall notify the director when the work is ready for final inspection, and before
any underground portions are covered. The city inspection shall be made within 72
hours of the receipt of notice by the director, weekends and city holidays excluded.
B.
