Construction of a private system. The type, capacities, location and layout of a private sewage disposal system shall
comply with all recommendations of the Department of Public Health of Boulder or Weld
County and all applicable requirements of the state and the United States Environmental
Protection Agency. No permit shall be issued for any private sewage disposal system
employing subsurface soil absorption where the area of the lot is less than 43,560
square feet. No private sewage disposal system shall be permitted to discharge to
any natural outlet.
C.
Maintenance. The owner shall operate and maintain the private sewage disposal facilities in a
sanitary manner at all times, in compliance with all applicable state, county and
local regulations and requirements pertaining to operation and maintenance, at no
expense to the city.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.240; Code 2009, § 14.08.240; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 3; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 6(14.08.240))
Unless appropriately authorized, no person shall uncover, make any connections with,
alter or disturb any POTW sewer or appurtenance thereof without first obtaining a
written permit from the director. A permit issued by the city wastewater utility will
also be necessary if the connection is within the street right-of-way. No person shall
maliciously, willfully or negligently damage, destroy or tamper with any property,
equipment or appurtenances which are a part of the POTW sewage system. No person shall
discharge wastewater into the POTW sewerage from any building other than the building
for which the permit was obtained.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.245; Code 2009, § 14.08.245; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2024-03 , § 3, 1-9-2024)
There shall be two classes of building sewer permits as follows:
A.
Residential;
B.
Commercial-industrial.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.250; Code 2009, § 14.08.250; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
The owner of either class of building sewer, or the owner's agent, shall make application
for a building sewer permit on a form provided by the city. The permit application
shall be supplemented by any plans, specifications or other information considered
to be pertinent in the judgment of the director. Permit and inspection fees for residential,
commercial and industrial building sewers are given in section 14.08.685 and shall be paid to the city at the time the application is filed.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.251; Code 2009, § 14.08.251; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
Old building sewers may be used in connection with new buildings or buildings undergoing
substantial renovation only when they are found, on examination and testing by the
director to meet all requirements of this chapter; otherwise, old building sewers
will be abandoned and plugged at the owner's expense. The plug in the old building
sewer must be inspected and approved by the director or a designated representative.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.252; Code 2009, § 14.08.252; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
Whenever possible, the building sewer shall be brought to the building at an elevation
below the basement floor. In all buildings in which any building drain is too low
to permit gravity flow to the POTW sewer, sanitary sewage carried by such building
drain shall be lifted by an approved means and discharged to the building sewer at
the owner's expense.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.253; Code 2009, § 14.08.253; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
No person shall make any connection of roof downspouts, foundation drains (underdrains),
areaway drains, storm drainage, or any other sources of surface runoff or groundwater
to a building sewer or building drain which is connected directly or indirectly to
the POTW sanitary sewer.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.254; Code 2009, § 14.08.254; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
The applicant for the building sewer permit shall notify the director when the building
sewer is ready for inspection and connection to the POTW sewer. The building sewer
shall be maintained in an unobstructed condition, to allow complete inspection, until
the inspection is completed. The connection shall be made under the supervision of
the director.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.255; Code 2009, § 14.08.255; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
No person shall discharge or cause to be discharged to a POTW sanitary sewer any stormwater,
surface water, or groundwater or water from roof drains, area drains, underdrains,
sump pump discharge lines, natural springs or seeps with the exception that stormwater,
surface water, or groundwater contaminated with pollutants which, if discharged without
treatment, may pollute surface waters or groundwater or constitute a public nuisance
or danger to public health, may be discharged to the POTW sewer system subject to
terms, conditions and requirements set forth by the director. Under no circumstances
will polluted surface waters, groundwater or industrial process water be allowed in
the POTW sewer system if the director determines that accepting the discharge would
result in a substantial reduction in treatment capacity, inhibition of the treatment
plant operation, dangers to public health or safety or violations of any state or
federal laws or regulations.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.270; Code 2009, § 14.08.270; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 4; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 6)
Except for those discharges allowed by section 14.08.270 , stormwater and all other surface drainage not contaminated by industrial or domestic
waste shall be discharged to the municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4) pursuant
to chapters 14.24 and 14.26 of this Code, or to surface waters, subject to the requirements and approval of the
State of Colorado, and such drainage shall not be channeled or introduced into the
POTW sewer system.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.280; Code 2009, § 14.08.280; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2024-03 , § 4, 1-9-2024)
Any industrial user which discharges into the POTW a substance which, if otherwise
disposed of, would be a hazardous waste as set forth in 40 CFR 261, shall comply with
the requirements in 40 CFR 403.12(p).
(Code 1993, § 14.08.281; Code 2009, § 14.08.281; Ord. No. O-2000-51, § 1)
No discharger shall contribute or cause to be discharged, directly or indirectly,
any of the following described substances to the POTW. Any discharge in violation
of these prohibitions shall be reported to the control authority by the discharger
in accordance with the procedures in section 14.08.316 unless other procedures are specified in a discharge permit assigned to the discharger.
A.
Any liquids, solids or gases which by reason of their nature or quantity are, or may
be, sufficient either alone or by interaction to cause fire or explosion or be injurious
in any other way to the operation of the POTW or the integrity of the sewer system
or cause a danger to the public health or safety. This prohibition includes, but is
not limited to, wastestreams with a closed cup flash point of less than 140 degrees
Fahrenheit or 60 degrees Centigrade using the test methods specified in federal regulations,
40 CFR 261.21;
B.
Any solid or viscous substances which will or may cause obstruction to the flow in
a sewer or other interference with the operation of the wastewater system;
C.
Any wastewater having a pH less than 5.0 or having any other corrosive property capable
of causing damage or hazard to structures, equipment, or personnel of the system,
or interference with the operation of the wastewater treatment plant;
D.
Any wastewater containing hazardous substances, toxic, conventional, or nonconventional
pollutants in sufficient quantity, either singly or by interaction, which could injure
or interfere with any wastewater treatment process, constitute a health or safety
hazard to humans or animals, or exceed the limitations set forth in sections 14.08.312 or 14.08.313 or the categorical pretreatment standards appropriate for the specific industrial
user. Hazardous substances, toxic, conventional or nonconventional pollutants shall
include, but not be limited to, any pollutant identified in 40 CFR 122 appendix D
(NPDES permit application testing requirements) tables II, III, IV, and V, or substances
alone or in combination with other constituents that are determined to be toxic by
the toxicity test as defined in 40 CFR 136 for wastewater or the toxicity characteristic
leaching procedure (TCLP) test as defined in 40 CFR 261 for biosolids;
E.
Any pollutants which result in the presence of toxic gases, vapors, or fumes within
the POTW in a quantity that may cause a public nuisance or acute worker health and
safety problems;
F.
Any substance which may cause the POTW's effluent or treatment residues, biosolids
or scums to be unsuitable for reclamation and reuse or which may interfere with such
reclamation and reuse process. In no case shall a substance discharged to the POTW
cause the POTW to be in noncompliance with biosolids use or disposal criteria, guidelines
or regulations developed under section 405 of the Clean Water Act, any criteria, guidelines
or regulations affecting biosolids use or disposal developed pursuant to the Solid
Waste Disposal Act, the Clean Air Act, the Toxic Substances Control Act or state or
local standards applicable to the biosolids management method adopted by the city.
A user may not introduce into the POTW any pollutants which cause pass through or
interference;
G.
Any substances which will inhibit the operation or performance of the POTW or pass
through the system and cause the POTW to violate any requirements of any discharge
permit issued by the state or federal governments;
H.
Any substance with objectionable color not removed in the treatment process, such
as, but not limited to, dye wastes and vegetable tanning solutions;
I.
Any wastewater having a temperature which will inhibit biological activity in the
POTW treatment plant resulting in interference; but in no case, wastewater with a
temperature which exceeds 40 degrees Celsius (104 degrees Fahrenheit) at the point
of introduction into the POTW treatment plant;
J.
Any slug load, which shall mean any pollutant, including oxygen demanding pollutants
(BOD, etc.), released in a single extraordinary discharge episode of such volume or
strength as to cause interference to the POTW;
K.
Any clean water as described in section 14.08.270 ;
L.
Any wastewater containing any radioactive wastes or isotopes of such halflife or concentration
as to exceed limits established by state and federal regulations;
M.
Any wastewater which causes the POTW effluent to exhibit toxicity to test organisms
in a standard biological toxicity test as defined by city, state or federal requirements,
or which the control authority determines would be toxic to or impede the treatment
capabilities of the biological processes in the wastewater treatment plant;
N.
Any petroleum oil, nonbiodegradable cutting oil or products of mineral oil origin
that will cause interference or pass through the wastewater treatment plant;
O.
Any wastewaters, wastes or pollutants that are delivered to the POTW by truck or other
means of surface transport, except at discharge points as are designated by the control
authority. Any discharge at designated points shall be subject to the requirements
of this chapter, including, but not limited to, the provisions in sections 14.08.312 , 14.08.314 , 14.08.319 and 14.08.410 ;
P.
Any emulsifying agents, solvents or enzymes that causes fats, oils or grease to pass
through an interceptor causing blockages of or the requirement for excessive maintenance
of the sanitary sewer.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.310; Code 2009, § 14.08.310; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 5; Ord. No. O-2000-51, § 2; Ord. No. O-2001-71, § 1; Ord.
No. O-2007, § 7(14.08.310); Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 3, 3-19-2013)
A.
The following pollutant limits are established to protect against pass through and
interference and will be applied to discharges from significant industrial users through
industrial wastewater permits or control mechanisms, based on the reasonable potential
to exceed the following maximum allowable discharge limits:

Pollutants | Daily Maximums (mg/L)
Ammonia | 30.0
Arsenic | 0.02
Cadmium | 0.10
Chromium (total) | 2.55
Chromium VI | 2.25
Copper | 3.31
Cyanide (total) | 1.14
Lead | 0.69
Mercury | 0.010
Molybdenum | 1.67
Nickel | 3.49
Selenium | 0.33
Silver | 0.43
Zinc | 2.47

B.
No user of the POTW shall discharge wastewater containing greater than 100 mg/L concentration
of oil and grease.
C.
No user of the POTW discharging from groundwater cleanup or underground storage tank
cleanup shall discharge wastewater containing greater than 0.05 mg/L concentration
of benzene or 0.75 mg/L concentration of total BTEX (benzene, tolulene, ethylbenzene,
and xylenes).
(Code 1993, § 14.08.312; Code 2009, § 14.08.312; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-94-52, § 2; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 6; Ord. No. O-2001-71, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2004-17, § 1; Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 4, 3-19-2013; Ord. No. O-2019-50 , § 1, 9-10-2019)
A.
No user may discharge wastes or wastewater containing pollutants in concentrations
or mass higher than those allowed by a discharge permit issued to the discharger pursuant
to section 14.08.330 . Concentrations higher than those listed in section 14.08.312 may be discharged only if the discharger has a permit specifying mass limitations
for each pollutant. Mass limitations may be assigned for the pollutants listed in section 14.08.312 or other pollutants if such limitations are approved by the director. Such limitations
shall only be allowed if the director finds them to be appropriate, consistent and
in furtherance of the purposes of this chapter. Mass limitations shall not be allowed
if the director determines that such limitations may:
1.
Adversely affect the operation of the sewer system or wastewater treatment plant;
2.
Prevent the wastewater treatment plant from meeting the requirements of its federal
or state discharge permit or any other federal or state requirements, including the
ability to dispose of biosolids and residues;
3.
Endanger the health, safety and welfare of city employees or the general public; or
4.
Violate the requirements of federal or state regulations.
B.
The sum of all mass limitations and the mass equivalents of all numerical limitations
assigned for certain pollutants shall be determined by the treatment capabilities
of the wastewater treatment facility. All dischargers who are subject to limitations
for such pollutants shall be assigned a proportional amount of the total allowable
mass available. The total allowable mass and the proportional amounts shall be determined
by the director based on factors which are reasonably related to the contribution
into the POTW as well as the type and quality of the wastewater constituents.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.313; Code 2009, § 14.08.313; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 7)
Certain pollutants which are compatible with the POTW treatment processes may be accepted
for treatment subject to discharge limitations to ensure proper operation of the POTW.
These pollutants may also be subject to a surcharge to cover the costs of such treatment.
Regulations governing conventional pollutant surcharges are found in section 14.08.650 .
(Code 1993, § 14.08.314; Code 2009, § 14.08.314; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 8; Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 5, 3-19-2013)
Each industrial user shall provide protection from accidental discharges of prohibited
or regulated materials. Facilities to prevent accidental discharges of these materials
into the city POTW shall be provided for and maintained by the user at the owner's
cost and expense. Detailed plans and specifications of such facilities shall be submitted
as a part of the waste management plan required by the discharge permit. Review and
approval of plans and operation procedures do not relieve the industrial user from
the responsibility to comply with discharge limitations or to modify the user's treatment
facilities in the future to meet subsequent standards, regulations or requirements.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.315; Code 2009, § 14.08.315; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 9)
In the event of an accidental discharge, it is the responsibility of the discharger
to notify the POTW at the time of the discharge. The notifications shall include the
location, time, volume, substance, concentrations, corrective actions and the persons
to be contacted concerning the accident. Within five days, the discharger must submit
a written report to the director explaining all the details of the spill, what procedures
were taken to mitigate the effects of the spill, and how the waste was disposed of,
if not to the wastewater system. This report shall also include a description of the
measures taken to prevent future occurrences. Notification does not relieve the discharger
of any expense, loss, damage or other liability which may have been incurred as a
result of damage to the POTW, to person or personal property, nor does such notification
relieve the discharger of any fines or penalties which may be assessed by the control
authority or other regulatory authority.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.316; Code 2009, § 14.08.316; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007, § 8(14.08.316))
A.
Each discharger shall be subject to the following provisions in the event of an upset
condition. An upset can be used as an affirmative defense to an action brought for
noncompliance with categorical pretreatment standards, provided the discharger demonstrates
through properly signed, contemporaneous operating logs or other relevant evidence
that:
1.
The upset occurred and the cause can be identified;
2.
At the time of the upset, the facility was being operated in a prudent and skillful
manner according to all appropriate operation and maintenance procedures;
3.
The following information was submitted, either verbally or in writing, to the control
authority within 24 hours of discovering the upset:
a.
A description of the indirect discharge and the cause of noncompliance;
b.
The period of noncompliance, including exact dates and times or, if the noncompliance
has not been corrected, the anticipated time the noncompliance is expected to continue;
c.
The steps being taken to reduce, eliminate and prevent recurrence of the noncompliance.
B.
If the above information is provided orally, a written submission must be sent within
five days of the occurrence of the noncompliance.
C.
The discharger has the burden of proof to establish the occurrence of an upset in
any enforcement proceeding.
D.
The discharger has the responsibility to control production or cease discharges as
necessary to maintain compliance with pretreatment standards and the requirements
of this chapter upon reduction, loss or failure of the discharger's facilities until
the facilities are restored or an alternative method of treatment is provided. This
requirement also applies where the primary source of power to the facilities is reduced,
lost or fails.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.317; Code 2009, § 14.08.317; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007, § 9(14.08.317))
Dilution of a waste discharge introduced into the wastewater system by an increase
in the use of process water in partial or complete substitution for adequate treatment
is not acceptable as a method for achieving the criteria set forth in this chapter
or the limitations contained in the federal pretreatment standards.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.318; Code 2009, § 14.08.318; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
Discharge permits issued under this chapter may be modified by special agreements
between the control authority and a discharger whereby an industrial wastewater of
unusual strength, concentration or character may be accepted by the city for treatment,
subject to payment by the discharger of agreed additional charges. Such charges shall
be determined by the control authority based on administrative, monitoring, testing
or other costs incurred in developing, negotiating and implementing the special agreements.
Special agreements shall only be possible if the director determines that the conditions
of the agreement shall not violate the purpose or requirements of this chapter or
endanger the public in any way. Special agreements shall not contain provisions which
supersede or take the place of any limits or conditions required by National Categorical
Pretreatment Standards.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.319; Code 2009, § 14.08.319; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 10(14.08.319))
A.
Users shall provide wastewater treatment as necessary to comply with this chapter
and their permits, if any, and shall achieve compliance with all categorical pretreatment
standards, local limits, and the prohibitions set out in this chapter or permit within
the time limitations specified by EPA, the state, or the control authority, whichever
is more stringent. Any facilities necessary for compliance shall be provided, operated
and maintained at the user's expense. Detailed plans describing such facilities and
operating procedures shall be submitted to the control authority for review, and shall
be acceptable to the director before such facilities are constructed or modified.
The review of such plans and operating procedures shall in no way relieve the user
from the responsibility of modifying such facilities as necessary to produce a discharge
acceptable to the control authority under the provisions of this chapter.
B.
Any facilities which are installed and operated by a discharger for the purpose of
meeting the requirements of this chapter shall be properly maintained and operated
in an efficient and effective manner. The treatment facilities shall be maintained
in good working order and operated and managed so as to minimize or prevent discharge
of any substance which could adversely affect human health or safety or the operation
or performance of the city's wastewater treatment plant, pass through the treatment
plant to receiving waters or cause other pollutants to be discharged. All users who
operate pretreatment facilities shall at all times follow good management practices,
which include, but are not limited to, the following:
1.
Adequate preventive maintenance procedures to prevent equipment and machinery from
breaking down or operating below optimum performance;
2.
Maintenance of backup or alternate treatment equipment to mitigate the effect of equipment
failures;
3.
Adequate staffing and staff training for proper operation;
4.
An adequate budget for operation and maintenance, staffing and capital improvements;
5.
Provisions for prompt response to emergency situations, including emergency response
procedures;
6.
Provisions for meeting long-term compliance deadlines.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.320; Code 2009, § 14.08.320; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 11(14.08.320))
A.
Except as provided for in subsection B of this section, bypass is prohibited and the
control authority may take an enforcement action against a user for an unanticipated
or anticipated bypass, unless:
1.
Bypass was unavoidable to prevent loss of life, personal injury, or severe property
damage;
2.
There were no feasible alternatives to the bypass, such as the use of auxiliary treatment
facilities, retention of untreated wastes, or maintenance during normal periods of
equipment downtime. This condition is not satisfied if adequate back-up equipment
should have been installed in the exercise of reasonable engineering judgment to prevent
a bypass which occurred during normal periods of equipment downtime or preventative
maintenance; and
3.
The user submitted the notices required in subsection C of this section.
B.
An industrial user may allow a bypass to occur which does not cause pretreatment standards
or requirements or any of the provisions or prohibitions of this Code to be violated,
but only if the bypass is necessary for essential maintenance to ensure efficient
operation. Such a bypass is subject to the notification requirements in subsection
C of this section.
C.
Notification requirements.
1.
If an industrial user knows in advance of the need for a bypass, it shall submit prior
notice to the control authority, at least ten days before the date of the bypass.
2.
An industrial user shall submit oral notice of an unanticipated bypass that exceeds
applicable pretreatment standards to the control authority within 24 hours from the
time the industrial user becomes aware of the bypass. A written submission shall also
be provided within five days of the time the industrial user becomes aware of the
bypass. The written submission shall contain a description of the bypass and its cause;
the duration of the bypass, including exact dates and times, and, if the bypass has
not been corrected, the anticipated time it is expected to continue; and steps taken
or planned to reduce, eliminate and prevent reoccurrence of the bypass. The control
authority may waive the written report on a case-by-case basis if the oral report
has been received within 24 hours.
The control authority may approve an anticipated bypass, after considering its adverse
effects, if the control authority determines that it will meet the three conditions
listed in subsection A of this section.
(Code 2009, § 14.08.321; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 12(14.08.321))
A.
All industrial dischargers to the sewer system must complete and submit an industrial
wastewater classification survey to the director. An industrial wastewater classification
survey must be submitted with the application for a discharge permit in accordance
with section 14.08.331 . In addition, a completed survey must be received by the director within 30 days
of the occurrence of any of the following:
1.
Receipt by the user of a survey questionnaire with a request for completion and submittal
of same from the director;
2.
Modification of any of the processes regulated by an existing permit which results
in any changes in characteristics, volume or point of discharge from those specified
in the existing permit;
3.
Addition of a process which is regulated by national categorical pretreatment standards
or addition of a discharge which is subject to local discharge regulations;
4.
Application for a building permit for initial construction, expansion or remodeling.
B.
The survey questionnaire must be submitted for review before the building permit is
issued.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.325; Code 2009, § 14.08.325; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
All industrial users shall update their industrial wastewater classification survey
on file with the director whenever significant changes are made in an industrial operation
or process. Significant changes include, but are not limited to, an increase or decrease
in wastewater volume of at least 20 percent, changes in the concentration or mass
loadings of waste materials or substances, or changes in types of waste discharged
to the POTW sewer. The director may also require the submittal of a new industrial
wastewater classification survey form as deemed necessary.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.326; Code 2009, § 14.08.326; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 13(14.08.326))
A.
No significant industrial user shall discharge wastewater to the POTW sewer without
having a valid wastewater discharge permit issued by the director. Applicants for
and holders of wastewater discharge permits shall be subject to the fee requirements
of section 14.08.675 .
B.
A wastewater discharge permit shall include such conditions as are deemed reasonably
necessary by the director to prevent pass through or interference, protect the quality
of the waterbody receiving the treatment plant's effluent, protect worker health and
safety, facilitate sludge management and disposal, and protect against damage to the
POTW.
C.
The director may deny any applicant a wastewater discharge permit for any past or
present failure or inability of the applicant to comply with the requirements of this
chapter or the inability of the wastewater treatment plant to treat the discharge,
if the director determines that the failure or a similar failure by the applicant
is likely to recur or continue. The director shall provide a written notice to the
applicant of permit denial by certified mail of personal service within 90 days of
the receipt of the application.
D.
The applicant may appeal the denial of a permit or the terms of a permit by filing
a notice of appeal with the director within ten days of the date of mailing or personal
service of the notice. No provision of the permit shall be stayed pending the appeal.
E.
Obtaining a wastewater discharge permit does not relieve a permittee of its obligation
to comply with all federal and state pretreatment standards or requirements or with
any other requirements of federal, state and local law.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.330; Code 2009, § 14.08.330; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 14(14.08.330); Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 6, 3-19-2013; Ord. No. O-2024-03 , § 5, 1-9-2024)
All dischargers of industrial wastewater to the sewer system must submit an application
for a discharge permit to the director. Applications shall be in the form prescribed
by the director. The applicant shall submit, in units and terms suitable for evaluation,
all information requested in the application, and any relevant supplemental information
requested by the director. New industrial users subject to federal categorical standards
or local limitations as determined by the director shall apply 90 days prior to connecting
to the city sanitary sewer. The application shall include a report containing, but
not limited to, information identifying the complete name and address of the facility
including the name of the operator and owner, a description of all business activities,
any environmental permits held by the facility, a description of operations, including
flow and pollution measurements, and any other information specified by the Act and
40 CFR 403.12(b)(1) through (7). All permittees shall apply for renewal of their wastewater
permit no later than 30 days prior to the expiration of their present permit.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.331; Code 2009, § 14.08.331; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-96-73, § 2; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 15(14.08.331))
A.
Prohibitions on the discharge of certain substances pursuant to section 14.08.310 .
B.
Restrictions and limitations on the discharge of certain pollutants pursuant to sections 14.08.312 , 14.08.313 and 14.08.445 .
C.
Notice of applicable national categorical pretreatment standards and compliance schedules
effective under section 14.08.445 .
D.
Requirements for installation of treatment technology necessary to achieve compliance
with the requirements of this chapter, including, but not limited to, the requirements
of sections 14.08.310 , 14.08.312 and 14.08.445 .
E.
Requirements for monitoring, sampling, recordkeeping and reporting, as specified in sections 14.08.336 through 14.08.342 .
F.
Provisions of special agreements regarding unusual wastewater characteristics pursuant
to section 14.08.319 .
G.
Requirements for additional payments to cover the costs of treating the wastes, if
such payments are not covered under any other provision of this chapter.
H.
Requirements for management plans to control slug discharges pursuant to section 14.08.350 .
I.
Requirements for notification of accidental discharges and upsets pursuant to sections 14.08.315 , 14.08.316 and 14.08.317 .
J.
Reporting requirements for the completion of actions specified in a compliance schedule,
if such a schedule is included in the permit.
K.
Requirements for a report, due 90 days after the issuance of a new permit, declaring
whether the permittee is in violation of its permit conditions and, if the permittee
is in violation, containing a schedule of actions necessary to come into compliance
no later than 180 days from the date of issuance of the permit. These requirements
apply only to new permits but may not conflict with any federal categorical standards
which may specify a different timeframe or require compliance upon discharge (as in
the case of new sources).
L.
Any other terms and conditions the control authority deems necessary to carry out
the requirements of this chapter and other applicable city, state and federal laws
and regulations.
M.
Requirements for payment of fees pursuant to section 14.08.675 .
N.
The wastewater discharge permit issuance date, expiration date and effective date
and a statement of duration of the permit, which is not to exceed three years, pursuant
to section 14.08.333 .
O.
A statement of nontransferability pursuant to section 14.08.334 .
P.
A statement of applicable civil and criminal penalties pursuant to section 14.08.500 for violation of pretreatment standards, requirements or any applicable compliance
schedule.
Q.
Any best management practices, including, but not limited to, schedules of activities,
recommended or prohibited practices, or maintenance procedures required to comply
with the prohibitions listed in sections 14.08.310 and 14.08.312 .
(Code 1993, § 14.08.332; Code 2009, § 14.08.332; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2004-17, § 2; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 16(14.08.332))
The director shall issue wastewater discharge permits for a specified time period
not to exceed three years. A wastewater discharge permit may be extended for a maximum
of one year from its original expiration date if the director decides that pending
changes in federal, state or local regulations would affect the terms and conditions
of the permit or other reasons, consistent with the purposes of this chapter, justify
the extension. Extensions may be granted only if the delay in issuing the new permit
is not due to any fault by the permittee and the permittee submitted a complete renewal
application as required in section 14.08.331 . The existing permit shall remain in force until the new permit is issued or the
extension is granted and the discharger shall meet all of the permit requirements.
The director shall notify the permittee of any permit extension in writing.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.333; Code 2009, § 14.08.333; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 10; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 17(14.08.333))
A wastewater discharge permit shall not be sold, traded, transferred, assigned or
sublet. A sale of controlling interest in a business shall constitute a sale and the
industrial user shall apply for a new permit.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.334; Code 2009, § 14.08.334; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 18(14.08.334))
A.
Promulgation of new applicable National Categorical Pretreatment Standards. If new
standards are promulgated, the director shall so notify the industrial discharger
in writing. The industrial discharger must submit a proposed schedule for complying
with the new standards within 30 days of notification. The modified permit shall contain
the new limits and a compliance schedule.
B.
Changes in the requirements of this chapter.
C.
Changes in state, federal or local regulations pertaining to the POTW biosolids or
discharge.
D.
Changes in processes used by the permittee, changes to production rates or changes
in discharge volumes or characteristics.
E.
Changes in available POTW capacity or modifications to the POTW treatment processes
which necessitate changes to industrial discharges to the wastewater system.
F.
Changes required as a result of any new information which was discovered or was not
available or provided at the time of permit issuance.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.335; Code 2009, § 14.08.335; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 11; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 19(14.08.335))
A.
All permittees must submit wastewater discharge monitoring reports and any supporting
documentation as requested by the director to the control authority at the frequencies
and times designated in the discharge permit, but in no event less frequently than
semiannually. The report forms shall be furnished by the control authority. Submitted
forms must contain all the results of analyses of the wastewater discharge performed
by the permittee as required in the wastewater permit, even if the analyses are not
required by the permit, and any other information required by the permit or requested
on the form itself. The reports must be complete, accurate and signed by an authorized
representative of the industrial user. All written reports will be deemed submitted
on the date postmarked by the U.S. Postal Service. If not postmarked, the date of
receipt shall govern.
B.
In cases where the pretreatment standard requires compliance with a best management
practice (BMP), the permittee must submit documentation required by the control authority
or the pretreatment standard necessary to determine the compliance status.
C.
All permittees must notify the control authority immediately of all discharges that
cause problems or could potentially cause problems to the POTW, including slug loadings.
D.
If sampling performed by an industrial user indicates a violation, the user shall
notify the control authority within 24 hours of becoming aware of the violation. The
user shall also repeat the sampling and analysis and submit the results of the repeat
analysis to the control authority within 30 days after becoming aware of the violation.
E.
Within 14 days of the due date of a compliance schedule milestones contained in a
permit or enforcement order, the industrial user shall submit a progress report to
the control authority, including, at a minimum, whether or not it complied with the
increment of progress to be met on such date and, if not, the date on which it expects
to comply with the increment of progress, the reason for the delay, and the steps
being taken to return to the schedule established.
F.
Within 90 days following the date for final compliance of the applicable categorical
pretreatment standards or in the case of a new source following commencement of the
introduction of wastewater into the POTW, any industrial user subject to pretreatment
standards and requirements shall submit a report containing flow measurements, pollutant
concentration and certification statements. Permittees subject to equivalent mass
or concentration limits established in accordance to 40 CFR 403.6(c) must include
a reasonable measure of long-term production rates. When the limits are expressed
in terms of allowable pollutant discharge per unit of production or other measure
of operation, the report shall include the permittee's actual production during the
sampling period.
G.
It is a strict liability offense to submit false, inaccurate or incomplete information
in any survey, application, letter or report and subjects the permittee to enforcement
actions set out in this chapter.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.336; Code 2009, § 14.08.336; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 20(14.08.336); Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 7, 3-19-2013)
Users subject to the reporting requirements of this chapter shall retain, and make
available for inspection and copying, all records of information obtained pursuant
to any monitoring activities required, any additional records of information obtained
pursuant to monitoring activities undertaken by the user independent of such requirements,
and documentation associated with best management practices. Records shall include
the date; exact place; method; time of sampling; the name of the person taking the
samples; the dates analyses were performed; who performed the analyses; the analytical
techniques or methods used, including BMPs; and the results of such analyses. These
records shall remain available for a period of at least three years. This period shall
be automatically extended for the duration of any litigation concerning the user or
the city, or where the user has been specifically notified of a longer retention period
by the control authority.
(Code 2009, § 14.08.337; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 21(14.08.337))
A.
Whenever required by the director, an industrial user shall provide, maintain and
operate, at its sole expense, monitoring equipment and facilities sufficient to allow
the safe inspection, sampling and flow measurements of the user's private sewer or
internal drainage systems based on the finding that compliance with standards of this
chapter reasonably requires such equipment or facilities. All devices used to measure
wastewater flow and quality shall be calibrated as required by the manufacturer to
ensure accuracy. Plans and specifications for the monitoring facilities shall be approved
by the director prior to construction of such facilities. The monitoring facilities
will normally be a monitoring manhole situated on the industrial user's premises,
but the control authority may, when such a location would be impractical or cause
undue hardship on the industrial user, allow the facility to be constructed in the
public street or sidewalk area, located so that it will not be obstructed by landscaping
or parked vehicles.
B.
Whenever required by the director, a monitoring manhole shall be installed for each
separate regulated discharge to the public sewer. The decision to require installation
of monitoring facilities will consider factors such as sampling frequency, sampling
parameters, economics, physical limitations of the plant site, and the requirements
of state and federal regulations. There shall be ample room in or near the monitoring
manhole or facilities to allow accurate sampling and preparation of samples for analysis,
and the manhole or facilities shall be safely, easily and independently accessible
to authorized representatives of the city at any time. The director may require that
the monitoring equipment be installed in an enclosed facility outside the manhole.
Whether constructed on public or private property, the sampling and monitoring equipment
and facilities shall be provided in accordance with the control authority's requirements
and all applicable local construction standards and specifications. Construction shall
be completed within 60 days following written notification by the director of the
requirement for installation. Federal regulations applicable to a specific industry
may require the installation of monitoring facilities at points within the industrial
treatment facilities.
C.
Whenever required by the director the monitoring facilities shall contain a Palmer-Bowlus
flume or similar flow-measuring device with a flow recorder and totalizer for measuring
and recording the discharge flow. The flow-measuring device and the measuring and
recording equipment and methods shall be approved by the director. The flow-monitoring
devices and associated equipment shall be accurate to within ten percent of the actual
flow being discharged. At the request of the director, the discharger must be able
to show proof, to the satisfaction of the director, of the accuracy of any flow-measuring
device and the accuracy and applicability of the measurement and recording methods
being used.
D.
Whenever the director finds it necessary for identification of discharges which may
not be compatible with the operation of the POTW, the monitoring facilities shall
contain a pH meter with a recorder for measuring and recording the discharge pH. The
pH meter, equipment, recorder and methods must be approved by the director as accurately
measuring and documenting pH. The discharger shall maintain and calibrate the pH meter
and equipment per the manufacturer's recommendation and at the expense of the discharger.
At the request of the director, the discharger must show proof, to the satisfaction
of the director, of the accuracy of the pH meter and the accuracy and applicability
of the measurement and the recording methods being used.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.340; Code 2009, § 14.08.340; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 12; Ord. No. O-2000-51, § 3; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 22(14.08.340);
Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 8, 3-19-2013)
A.
Sample types, measurements and analyses required for each industrial user shall be
determined by the director and included in the industrial user's discharge permit.
All measurement, tests and analyses of characteristics of water and wastes to which
reference is made in this chapter or in a discharge permit issued under this chapter
shall be in accordance with procedures established by the EPA pursuant to section
304(h) of the Act and contained in 40 CFR 136, as amended. If 40 CFR 136 does not
contain sampling or analytical techniques for the pollutant in question, or where
the EPA determines that the part 136 sampling and analytical techniques are inappropriate
for the pollutant in question, sampling and analyses shall be performed by using validated
analytical methods or any other applicable sampling and analytical procedures, including
procedures suggested by the control authority or other parties approved by EPA. All
sampling, measurements and analyses done for satisfaction of the requirements of the
permit shall be the responsibility of the industrial user or a designated representative
of the user. All samples taken of permitted discharges must be reported to the control
authority whether or not they are required by a permit. The wastewater discharge amount
shall be measured using either the flow measurement device installed per section 14.08.340 , or, at the discretion of the director, the metered water supply to the industrial
user's facility (or measurement adjustment thereof).
B.
All samples must be representative of the industrial user's discharge and shall be
collected during the monitoring period. Wastewater monitoring and flow measurement
facilities shall be properly operated, kept clean and maintained in good working order
at all times. The failure of a user to keep its monitoring facilities in good working
order shall not be grounds for the user to claim that sample results are unrepresentative
of its discharge.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.341; Code 2009, § 14.08.341; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 23(14.08.341))
A.
The control authority may conduct compliance monitoring at such times and frequencies
as are deemed necessary to carry out the purposes and intentions of this chapter.
The control authority shall collect compliance monitoring samples at least annually
for pollutants in any permitted industrial users' effluent. This monitoring shall
consist of any pollutants limited by applicable categorical pretreatment standards,
the wastewater discharge permit and all pollutants listed in section 14.08.312 . The control authority reserves the right to verify methods of sampling, flow and
pH measurements and analyses and to inspect records pertaining to all requirements
of the industrial user's permit. At such time that the control authority collects
a sample, an aliquot of that sample may be offered to the industrial user for analysis.
All costs incurred in the performance of measurements, tests and analyses done by
the control authority as a verification of methods and analyses of the industrial
user shall be charged to the industrial user in accordance with section 14.08.675 .
B.
The control authority also reserves the right to collect and analyze samples of the
industrial user's final discharge at any time in order to assess the user's complete
compliance status. An aliquot of the sample, if available, may be provided to the
industrial user upon written request. All sample aliquots are destroyed upon expiration
of the regulatory maximum holding time for the designated parameter as listed in 40
CFR 136.3, table II.
C.
It is a strict liability offense to tamper with, disable, impede or move any monitoring
or sampling device installed by the control authority.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.342; Code 2009, § 14.08.342; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2000-51, § 4; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 24(14.08.342); Ord. No. O-2013-07,
§ 9, 3-19-2013)
A.
All significant industrial users served by the POTW must develop and submit an appropriate
waste management plan consisting of general management practices, slug control practices,
and solvent or toxic organics management practices. The discharge permit shall incorporate
the plan by reference. The plan shall include, but not be limited to:
1.
An ongoing and complete inventory and description of the types and quantities of pollutants
and chemicals used or stored by the industrial user;
2.
A diagram of the facility including the process and storage locations, and the locations
of floor drains to sanitary or storm sewers;
3.
A description of the discharge practices, including nonroutine batch discharges;
4.
A description of the measures used to prevent discharge to sanitary or storm sewers;
5.
An outline of the spill-prevention procedures followed by the industrial user;
6.
A list or outline of the emergency response and notification procedures to be followed
in case of accidental spills. Notification procedures must include immediate notification
of any slug discharge with a follow-up written notification within five days of the
incident;
7.
A description of spill containment, treatment and disposal methods of pollutants and
chemicals used or stored by the industrial user;
8.
A description of the training procedures and training frequencies for personnel involved
in handling, storing and disposing of toxic or hazardous pollutants;
9.
A determination of identities and approximate quantities of toxic organic pollutants
used as well as discharged from any regulated manufacturing process;
10.
A description of management practices used to operate and maintain the facility, including,
but not limited to, preventative maintenance procedures, maintenance of backup equipment
to mitigate equipment failures, maintaining adequate staff levels for proper operation,
and prompt management and operational response to emergency situations as well as
long-term compliance deadlines.
B.
The director shall require a waste management plan to include particular contents
as needed to ensure that the POTW is protected from potential pass through, interference
or slug loading. The existence of a management plan does not relieve the discharger
from fines, civil penalties or other liabilities which may be imposed in the event
of violations of this Code or other applicable ordinances or laws.
C.
A user's failure to complete any practice outlined in its waste management plan is
an offense under this chapter.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.350; Code 2009, § 14.08.350; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 13; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 25(14.08.350); Ord. No. O-2013-07,
§ 10, 3-19-2013)
A.
In the event an industrial user intends to cease to discharge from a regulated process
or plans to disconnect from the wastewater system, the director must be notified no
later than 30 days prior to any action by the industrial user. The notification shall
provide a closure management plan that includes the following information, as a minimum:
1.
Date of planned disconnect;
2.
Methods of disposal of regulated process tanks, chemicals, sludges, plating wastes,
cleaning solutions;
3.
Methods of cleaning tanks, barrels or other vessels containing regulated pollutants;
4.
Names of carriers and ultimate disposal sites of the regulated pollutants and the
EPA permit numbers for transportation of the wastes, if a permit is required by EPA.
B.
The industrial user shall be responsible for all discharges to the wastewater system
and shall not be disconnected until the director has determined the industrial user
has disposed of the regulated wastes in a proper and safe manner and has requested
termination of the discharge permit in writing.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.360; Code 2009, § 14.08.360; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
Septage from vacuum-pump trucks or other sources shall not be discharged into the
POTW sewer system without written permission of the director. If such discharges are
allowed, the discharger shall be subject to any applicable provisions of this chapter,
including the requirements of sections 14.08.310 and 14.08.312 as well as any charges for administrative, monitoring, testing or other costs incurred
by the city in allowing the discharge. These costs shall be determined by the director.
Commercial and industrial users shall not discharge holding tank sewage to a POTW
sewer without having a valid wastewater discharge permit or letter of permission issued
by the director.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.410; Code 2009, § 14.08.410; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2024-03 , § 6, 1-9-2024)
Grease interceptors, oil separators and sand interceptors shall be installed on the
building drain of the following users of the sewer system:
A.
All commercial establishments engaged in the preparation and sale of foodstuffs to
the general public such as restaurants, cafes, lunch counters, cafeterias, bars, kitchens
in hotels, hospitals, factories or other profit or nonprofit organizations providing
food services;
B.
Establishments engaged in automatic and coin-operated laundries, car washes, filling
stations, commercial garages or similar businesses having wash or grease racks;
C.
All establishments operating a commercial garbage grinder;
D.
Dairies, slaughterhouses, commercial processing plants and other similar sources of
grease and fat byproducts, unless such establishments are otherwise regulated by a
discharge permit issued under this chapter;
E.
Any other users producing quantities of grit, sand, grease, fat or other materials
which have the potential of causing partial or complete obstruction of the building
sewer or other areas in the sewer system, require excessive maintenance of sewer lines
or impede the transmission or treatment of sewage.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.415; Code 2009, § 14.08.415; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2003-19, § 2)
All interceptors shall be designed and installed in accordance with the requirements
of chapter 16.16 and the design standards, such that excessive amounts of grease, oil, sand, and other
inert solids are effectively prevented from entering the POTW sewer. All other requirements
of chapter 16.16 , including safety considerations, access provisions for maintenance and cleaning,
design of vents and covers, and types of construction materials shall be applicable.
All interceptors shall be installed, maintained, repaired and operated by the discharger
at his or her own expense. The installation shall be kept in continuous operation
at all times, and shall be inspected and cleaned on a regular basis as required for
efficient operation, by a service contractor qualified to perform such cleaning and
inspection. Bioremediation may be used in addition to regular maintenance, provided
that the grease does not pass through the interceptor and cause blockages or excessive
maintenance requirements. Certificates of inspection and maintenance shall be kept
on the premises of the interceptor installation and shall be made readily available
to city authorized personnel for review and inspection. Wastes removed from the interceptor
must not be reintroduced into an interceptor or sewer. Dischargers shall be subject
to the remedies, including penalties, set forth in sections 14.08.500 through 14.08.560 for failure to inspect and maintain interceptors.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.416; Code 2009, § 14.08.416; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 14; Ord. No. O-2000-51, § 5; Ord. No. O-2003-19, § 3; Ord.
No. O-2007-78, § 26(14.08.416); Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 11, 3-19-2013)
The director or a designated representative of the director shall be permitted to
enter all private properties through which the city holds a duly negotiated easement
for the purpose of, but not limited to, inspection, sampling, measurement, observation,
photographing, monitoring, repair and maintenance of any portion of the POTW sewer
system lying within the easement. All entry and subsequent work, if any, on the easement
shall be done in full accordance with the terms of the duly negotiated easement pertaining
to the private property involved.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.420; Code 2009, § 14.08.420; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 12, 3-19-2013)
The director may inspect the facilities and equipment of any discharger at any time
to ascertain whether the applicable city ordinances, rules and regulations, industrial
discharge permit requirements and applicable National Categorical Pretreatment Standards
are being complied with.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.425; Code 2009, § 14.08.425; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 27(14.08.425))
A.
The director or a designated representative of the director shall have the right to
enter the premises of any user to determine whether the user is complying with all
requirements of this chapter and any wastewater discharge permit or order issued hereunder.
Ready access to all parts of the premises for the purposes of inspection, sampling,
photographing, records examination and copying, and the performance of any additional
duties will be made to the director, the state department of health, and the United
States Environmental Protection Agency without delay.
B.
Where a user has security measures in force which require proper identification and
clearance before entry into its premises, the user shall make necessary arrangements
with its security guards so that, upon presentation of suitable identification, the
director will be permitted to enter without delay for the purposes of performing specific
responsibilities.
C.
The director shall have the right to set up on the user's property, or require installation
of, such devices as are necessary to conduct any sampling and/or metering of the user's
operations.
D.
Any temporary or permanent obstruction to safe and easy access to the facility to
be inspected and/or sampled shall be promptly removed by the user at the written or
verbal request of the director and shall not be replaced. The costs of clearing such
access shall be borne by the user.
E.
In the event that the director or a designated representative of the director is refused
admission to the discharger's premises, the director will cause sewer service to the
premises in question to be discontinued until the POTW agents have been afforded reasonable
access to the premises to accomplish any required inspection, sampling or flow monitoring.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.430; Code 2009, § 14.08.430; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 28(14.08.430); Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 13, 3-19-2013)
Information and data obtained from reports, surveys, wastewater discharge permit applications,
wastewater discharge permits, and monitoring programs, and from the director's inspection
and sampling activities, shall be available to the public without restriction, unless
the user specifically requests otherwise, and is able to demonstrate to the satisfaction
of the director, that the release of such information would divulge information, processes,
or methods of production entitled to protection as trade secrets under applicable
state law. Any such request must be asserted at the time of submission of the information
or data. When requested and demonstrated by the user furnishing a report that such
information should be held confidential, the portions of a report which might disclose
trade secrets or secret processes shall not be made available for inspection by the
public, but shall be made available immediately upon request to governmental agencies
for uses related to the NPDES program or pretreatment program, and in enforcement
proceedings. Wastewater constituents and characteristics and other effluent data,
as defined by 40 CFR 2.302, will not be recognized as confidential information and
will be available to the public without restriction.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.435; Code 2009, § 14.08.435; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 29(14.08.435))
Limitations, standards or requirements that differ from or are more stringent than
those in this chapter may be imposed on an industrial user under the following conditions:
A.
The federal government promulgates federal categorical pretreatment standards for
an industrial category that are more stringent than the standards prescribed in this
chapter. Such federal standards shall immediately supersede the standards prescribed
by this article. The director shall notify all affected users of applicable testing
and reporting requirements.
B.
State requirements and limitations on discharges are more stringent than federal requirements
and limitations or those in this article.
C.
The director establishes limitations or requirements which are more stringent than
federal and state requirements or the limitations in this chapter in order to comply
with the purposes and objectives of this chapter.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.445; Code 2009, § 14.08.445; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
The director shall annually publish in a newspaper of general circulation in the city
a list of all dischargers that are found to be in significant noncompliance with any
provisions of this chapter during the previous 12-month period and a summary of any
enforcement actions taken against violators during that period.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.450; Code 2009, § 14.08.450; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
A.
It is unlawful and a strict liability offense for any person, partnership or corporation
to violate or permit or cause violation of this chapter or of the provisions of any
discharge permit issued under this chapter. Each day or part of a day any violation
occurs or continues is a separate offense. Each offense is punishable by a fine up
to $3,000.00, or imprisonment up to 180 days, or by both such fine and imprisonment.
B.
Nothing in this section prohibits the director from using any or all of the enforcement
alternatives outlined in this part. The director may use any available enforcement
alternatives the director believes will be effective. No enforcement alternative is
a condition precedent to any other alternative. Violation of this chapter or the provisions
of any discharge permit issued under it is a public nuisance. Any court of competent
jurisdiction shall enjoin violations.
C.
The director may adopt an enforcement response plan as guidelines for carrying out
the provisions of this chapter, provided that such policies and procedures are not
in conflict with this chapter or any applicable state or federal law or regulation.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.500; Code 2009, § 14.08.500; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-94-61, § 9; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 15; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 30(14.08.500);
Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 14, 3-19-2013)
If any discharger is found to be in violation of any of the requirements of this chapter
or in violation of any discharge permit issued under this chapter or discharge wastes
that present or may present an endangerment to the environment or which threatens
to interfere with the operation of the POTW, the director may immediately halt the
discharge or discontinue sewer service to the discharger by refusing to accept the
user's discharge for transmission or treatment or set reasonable orders or conditions
with which the discharger shall comply to protect the public health and safety. Each
breach of any such condition or order is a separate offense.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.530; Code 2009, § 14.08.530; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 15, 3-19-2013)
The director may suspend or revoke a wastewater discharge permit and terminate the
POTW sewer service if a discharger:
A.
Violates any condition stated in a wastewater discharge permit, or any provision of
this chapter, or any applicable state or federal regulation;
B.
Fails to report a discharge, accidental or otherwise, of a toxic or prohibited substance;
C.
Increases the use of process water or attempts to dilute the discharge for the primary
purpose of achieving compliance with any discharge limitation;
D.
Fails to accurately report wastewater constituents and characteristics;
E.
Tampers with, disrupts, damages or renders inaccurate any wastewater monitoring device
required by this chapter;
F.
Refuses reasonable access to the industrial user's premises for the purpose of inspection
or monitoring;
G.
Fails to pay fees or charges assessed by the city for POTW services;
H.
Makes any false statement, representation or certification in any application, record,
plan, data or document filed or required to be maintained pursuant to this chapter.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.540; Code 2009, § 14.08.540; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 31(14.08.540))
If the director finds cause for suspension or revocation of a wastewater discharge
permit, either under the terms of section 14.08.540 or any other section of this chapter, the director shall determine whether to revoke
the permit for the remainder of its term or to suspend it for any shorter period.
Such determination shall be based on the severity of the violation, the effects on
public health, safety and welfare and the time during which the violation can be remedied,
if at all.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.541; Code 2009, § 14.08.541; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 16)
Upon determination by the director of just cause, under the terms of this chapter,
to suspend or revoke a wastewater discharge permit, the discharger shall be sent,
by certified mail or personal delivery service, written notice of termination of POTW
service. The discharger may elect to appeal such determination, in which event such
discharger shall, within 20 days of the date of mailing or service, notify, in writing,
the director of intent to appeal, specifying the particular section of the determination
contested and the basis thereof.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.542; Code 2009, § 14.08.542; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 32(14.08.542))
Before the hearing of an appeal the director may suspend an industrial wastewater
discharge permit for a period of up to 20 days, if the director determines that the
suspension is necessary to prevent an imminent danger to the public health, safety
and welfare or interference with the operation or treatment abilities of the POTW.
The director may include in the temporary suspension reasonable orders or conditions
with which the permittee shall comply to protect the public health and safety. Any
breach of such conditions or orders is an independent ground for revocation of the
permit, assessment of a penalty, or both.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.543; Code 2009, § 14.08.543; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 33(14.08.543))
Whenever the director finds that any industrial user has violated or is violating
any of the provisions of this chapter or of any permit issued under this chapter,
the director shall serve upon such industrial user a written notice of violation and
may serve an administrative order. The notice of violation shall state the nature
of the violation and the administrative order shall require that the cause of the
violations be corrected and compliance with permit requirements be obtained within
a reasonable time, not to exceed 45 days, except as otherwise provided in this chapter.
The notice and any administrative order shall be sent to the discharger via certified
mail or personal service. The user may request a meeting with the director to discuss
the violation and correction schedule within ten days following the date of mailing
or personal service of the administrative order. The order may be modified following
the written response or meeting to include a revised schedule to correct the cause
of the violation, if the discharger demonstrates to the director that just cause exists
for such modification. The final date of compliance in any modified schedule shall
not extend beyond 180 days from the date of issuance of the original administrative
order. Nothing in this section shall prohibit the director from seeking appropriate
judicial relief where the actions or conduct of a POTW user constitute an immediate
threat to the public health, safety or welfare. Nor shall anything in this section
prohibit the director from immediately seeking criminal sanctions against a suspected
violator.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.550; Code 2009, § 14.08.550; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 17; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 34(14.08.550))
When the director finds that a discharger has violated, or continues to violate, any
provision of this chapter, permit or order issued hereunder, or any other pretreatment
standard or requirement, the director may fine the discharger a civil penalty of up
to $1,000.00 for each violation. Each day during which a violation occurs is a separate
violation. Nothing in this section shall prevent the control authority from acting
or seeking relief under any other sections of this chapter for any violations of this
chapter or any permit issued under it. Any costs incurred by the city, including reasonable
attorneys' fees, due to violations subject to civil penalty, shall be added to the
total amount of the civil penalty assessment, under section 14.08.580 .
(Code 1993, § 14.08.560; Code 2009, § 14.08.560; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-94-61, § 9; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 18; Ord. No. O-2000-51, § 6; Ord.
No. O-2003-19, § 4; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 35(14.08.560); Ord. No. O-2013-07, § 16,
3-19-2013)
Should the violations stated in a notice of violation and administrative order issued
pursuant to section 14.08.550 be subject to civil penalty, the director shall send notice of the corresponding
civil penalty to the responsible party via certified mail or personal delivery service.
The notice shall describe the nature of the violation and clearly state the amount
of the civil penalty along with the due date and possible consequences of nonpayment
in accordance with sections 14.08.566 through 14.08.568 .
(Code 1993, § 14.08.561; Code 2009, § 14.08.561; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
Any party who is the recipient of a notice of civil penalty assessment pursuant to section 14.08.561 may appeal the notice by filing a notice of appeal with the director on or before
the expiration of ten days following the date of mailing or personal delivery of the
notice.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.562; Code 2009, § 14.08.562; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 36(14.08.562))
Civil penalties not paid and received by the city within 30 days of issuance shall
be deemed delinquent and subject to the delinquency assessments contained in section 14.08.566 .
(Code 1993, § 14.08.565; Code 2009, § 14.08.565; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1)
On each civil penalty assessment not paid before the due date, interest at 1½ percent
per month shall accrue.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.566; Code 2009, § 14.08.566; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-94-61, § 9)
Before the filing of a civil action to collect one or more delinquent penalty assessments,
the director shall send a notice to the responsible party, via certified mail or personal
service, which shall advise the party of the nature of the violation which resulted
in a civil penalty, the dates on which violations occurred, the original due date
of the penalty assessment and the amount of the penalty, including any delinquency
charges. The notice shall further advise the responsible party that unless payment
of all assessments is made to the city within ten days of the date of the notice,
civil action to collect the delinquent amounts may be filed in a court of competent
jurisdiction for collection of such assessments.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.567; Code 2009, § 14.08.567; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 37(14.08.567))
If the city does not receive full payment of all assessments following the notice
provided for in section 14.08.567 , the city attorney shall file civil action for collection. Any inaccuracy or omission
in the notice under section 14.08.567 shall not bar or serve as a defense to the civil action. Any such defect may result
in the disallowance of interest until the city perfects notice.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.568; Code 2009, § 14.08.568; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-94-61, § 9)
Any discharger violating this chapter shall be liable for any expense, loss or damage
caused the POTW by reason of such violation, including increased costs for sewage
treatment, biosolids treatment and disposal and POTW operation and maintenance expenses
resulting from the user's discharge. If a user discharges pollutants that cause the
United States Environmental Protection Agency or the state to fine the city for violating
any condition of its NPDES permit, the discharger shall indemnify the city for the
total cost of the fine, including, without limitation, all legal, sampling, analytical,
and other associated costs and expenses.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.580; Code 2009, § 14.08.580; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-94-61, § 9; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 19)
A.
Within ten business days of timely receipt of a notice of appeal, the director shall
appoint a hearing officer having expertise in the field of wastewater to hear and
determine the matters subject to appeal and determination by this chapter. The hearing
office shall not be an employee of the department of utilities and public works.
B.
All hearings shall be informal, the object being to resolve the issues promptly and
economically. Rules of evidence shall not be strictly applied.
C.
The hearing shall be recorded by tape recording, stenographer, or other verbatim reproduction.
A copy of the proceedings shall be available to the appellant upon payment of the
reasonable costs thereof.
D.
The appellant has the burden of proof to provide that the hearing officer should act
as the applicant requests or that the determination appealed from was erroneous. Every
party to the proceeding has the right to present such party's case or defense by oral
and documentary evidence, to submit rebuttal evidence, and to conduct such cross examination
as may be required for a full and true disclosure of the facts. The hearing officer
may receive all or part of the evidence in form if the interests of the parties will
not be prejudiced substantially and if the hearing will be expedited thereby.
E.
The hearing officer may issue a decision at the hearing and shall, within 30 days
after completion of the hearing, issue a written decision with findings of fact and
conclusions of law, setting forth the grounds of the decision, based on the evidence
presented at the hearing. The hearing officer shall serve the decision on each party
to the hearing by personal service or by mailing by regular mail, to the last address
furnished to the hearing officer by the party. The decision shall be effective on
the date mailed or on such other date as in stated in the decision.
F.
The decision of the hearing officer is final subject only to judicial review pursuant
to Colorado Rules of Civil Procedure, rule 106(a)(4). No defense of objection may
be presented for judicial review unless it is first presented to the hearing officer,
prior to the decision thereof.
G.
No ex parte material or representation of any kind or any other communication outside
the hearing shall be considered by the hearing officer conducting the hearing unless
it is fully disclosed on the hearing record and an opportunity is given for comment
thereon at the hearing.
(Code 2009, § 14.08.585; Ord. No. O-2007-78, § 38(14.08.585); Ord. No. O-2024-03 , § 7, 1-9-2024)
The sewer system development fee is a one-time original connection charge and is credited
to the property as long as the building use and size of the water connection servicing
the facility remains unchanged. If the building use is changed impacting the POTW
or if the size of the water connection is enlarged, the building connection shall
be considered as an original connection and the system development fee shall be determined
as in section 14.08.610 . If a structure has multiple water taps, a system development fee shall be charged
for each water tap. Credit is allowed against the system development fee for the size
of the previous water connection. That portion of the system development fee required
for debt service attributable to expansion of the wastewater system and new regulatory
requirements shall be deposited to the sewer fund. All remaining portions of system
development fees, and interest earned, shall be deposited in the sewer construction
fund, and restricted solely to expenditures related to improvements and expansion
of the wastewater system.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.605; Code 2009, § 14.08.605; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 10; Ord. No.
O-2000-54, § 6; Ord. No. O-2015-10, § 1, 1-27-2015)
A.
The following charges shall be made for services by the city relative to providing
new or larger size wastewater service or changing customer user classification:
1.
Residential.

Water Meter Size (inches) | 2024
⅝ × ¾ | $5,690.00
¾ | $8,540.00
1 | $14,230.00

2.
Small commercial, mixed use and Vance Brand Municipal Airport users.

Water Meter Size (inches) | 2024
⅝ × ¾ | $8,190.00
¾ | $12,285.00
1 | $20,475.00
1½ | $40,950.00
2 | $65,520.00
3 | $131,040.00
4 and larger | Negotiated based on individual water usage characteristics

3.
Large commercial. Large commercial customers are defined by meter size and water usage characteristics.
They must have water demands that require a four-inch or larger water meter, continuous
flow greater than 160 gallons per minute, and nominal peaking. The system development
fee for large commercial customers is negotiated based on individual wastewater usage
characteristics.
4.
Multifamily. All multifamily services shall pay a per unit fee, which is cumulative, based on
the following schedule:

Fee Block | 2024
First 4 units | $4,310.00
Next 8 units | $2,820.00
Next 22 units | $1,800.00
Next 29 units | $1,650.00
Beginning with 64th unit | $900.00

5.
Vance Brand Municipal Airport master meter. The Vance Brand Municipal Airport master meter system development fee shall be the
same as for a five-eighths-inch by three-fourths-inch small commercial meter. The
rated capacity of a five-eighths-inch by three-fourths-inch meter will be set according
to the most recent version of the meter standards published by the American Water
Works Association and the corresponding fixture units as determined in the latest
edition of the International Plumbing Code. Each customer on the Vance Brand Municipal
Airport shall pay a proportionate share of the system development fee according to
the customer's number of fixture units. However, charges for monthly service and volume
use under section 14.08.640 shall be assessed based on separate meters for each and every sewer service at the
Vance Brand Municipal Airport.
6.
Effective date of fees. The fees set forth above for 2024 shall become effective as of the effective date
of the ordinance from which this chapter is derived. The city manager or designee
shall adjust the fees set forth in 2024 annually in the same proportion as changes
in construction costs, according to the "Engineering News Record 20-City Construction
Cost Index."
