(Code 1993, § 14.08.610; Code 2009, § 14.08.610; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 11; Ord. No.
O-96-43, § 13; Ord. No. O-97-73, § 1; Ord. No. O-99-10, § 6; Ord. No. O-2000-54, §
7; Ord. No. O-2006-76, § 6; Ord. No. O-2008-78, § 9; Ord. No. O-2015-10, § 2, 1-27-2015;
Ord. No. O-2015-48, § 6, 10-13-2015; Ord. No. O-2023-66B , § 1, 11-28-2023)
Each user of the POTW shall be charged a monthly user charge as set forth in this
section:
A.
Unmetered residential water users will be charged as follows:

2023 | 2024 | 2025
$43.89 | $47.40 | $51.67

B.
Metered water users will be charged a monthly service charge plus a volume charge
based on metered water usage as measured in 100-gallon increments as follows:
1.
Monthly service charge.

2023 | 2024 | 2025
$11.50 | $11.50 | $11.50

2.
Volume charge. Each user of the POTW shall be charged a volume charge based on the metered water
usage or, if the POTW user has wastewater flow measurement facilities, on the volume
of wastewater discharged to the POTW as follows:

2023 (per 1,000 gallons) | 2024 (per 1,000 gallons) | 2025 (per 1,000 gallons)
$6.29 | $7.05 | $7.82

C.
The rates set forth in this section shall become effective January 1 of each year
listed, except the rates listed for the year 2025 shall continue in effect until changed.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.640; Code 2009, § 14.08.640; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 17; Ord. No.
O-2008-78, § 10; Ord. No. O-2009-34, § 5, 7-14-2009; Ord. No. O-2012-67, § 1, 10-23-2012;
Ord. No. O-2017-67, § 1, 10-24-2017; Ord. No. O-2022-49 , § 2, 12-20-2022)
For all metered residential users who irrigate lawn or other landscaping during the
months of March, April, May, June, July, August, September, October and November,
the sewer use charge on bills rendered during those months may be adjusted to the
average monthly charges of the preceding January, February and March sewer utility
billings; however, POTW users obtaining the rate benefits of this section may be required
to submit to a semi-annual review of their utility application or at such times as
may be designated by the director. New residents, or residents moving to new premises,
will be charged based upon either the city-wide, single-family residential winter
quarter average or their monthly usage, whichever is lower, until they establish their
own winter quarter history.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.645; Code 2009, § 14.08.645; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 18; Ord. No.
O-2008-78, § 11; Ord. No. O-2009-34, § 6, 7-14-2009; Ord. No. O-2017-67, § 2, 10-24-2017)
For all metered commercial, mixed-use and industrial users of water, as defined in section 14.08.100 , who irrigate lawn or other landscaping during the months of March, April, May, June,
July, August, September, October and November, the sewer use charge on bills rendered
during those months may be adjusted to the average monthly charges of the preceding
January, February and March sewer utility billings. However, POTW users must apply
for the rate benefits of this section, must demonstrate that they have lawn or landscape
irrigation, and may be required to submit to a semi-annual review of their sewer utility
application or at such time as may be designated by the director. No such adjustment
will be given until the user has accumulated actual use data for January, February
and March sewer utility billings in the location being billed.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.646; Code 2009, § 14.08.646; Ord. No. O-92-17, § 1; Ord. No. O-2008-78,
§ 12; Ord. No. O-2009-34, § 7, 7-14-2009; Ord. No. O-2015-10, § 3, 1-27-2015; Ord.
No. O-2017-67, § 3, 10-24-2017)
For all metered commercial, mixed use and industrial users of water, as defined in section 14.08.100 , who use evaporative methods of building cooling, the sewer use charge may be adjusted
by use of a dedicated submeter. POTW users will be responsible for the cost of submeter
installation and any infrastructure, as well as the monthly service charge for the
associated submeter. Any submeter installed for the purpose herein remains subject
to the requirements stated under section 14.04.290 .
( Ord. No. O-2020-67 , § 1(14.08.647), 12-15-2020)
A.
Any significant industrial user as defined by this chapter discharging wastewater
having an average daily concentration of BOD that is greater than 340 milligrams per
liter, or concentration of TSS greater than 250 milligrams per liter, or TKN greater
than 50 milligrams per liter, shall be subject to a surcharge rate.
B.
The surcharge shall be paid in addition to the normal sewer user charge computed according
to the provisions of section 14.08.640 .
(Code 1993, § 14.08.650; Code 2009, § 14.08.650; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 19; Ord. No.
O-2008-78, § 13; Ord. No. O-2009-34, § 8, 7-14-2009; Ord. No. O-2012-67, § 2, 10-23-2012)
The surcharge rates for BOD, TSS and TKN applied to total strength concentrations
exceeding the base levels for significant industrial users as set forth in section 14.08.650 shall be as follows:

| 2013 | 2014 | 2015 | 2016 | 2017
BOD, per lb. | $0.47 | $0.56 | $0.63 | $0.65 | $0.67
TSS, per lb. | $0.37 | $0.41 | $0.45 | $0.46 | $0.46
TKN, per lb. | $0.33 | $0.51 | $0.66 | $0.70 | $0.74

The rates set forth in this section shall become effective January 1 of each year
listed, except the rates listed for the year 2017 shall continue in effect until changed.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.655; Code 2009, § 14.08.655; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 20; Ord. No.
O-98-15, § 20; Ord. No. O-2000-67, § 4; Ord. No. O-2003-19, § 5; Ord. No. O-2004-17,
§ 3; Ord. No. O-2008-78, § 14; Ord. No. O-2009-34, § 9, 7-14-2009; Ord. No. O-2012-67,
§ 3, 10-23-2012)
Calculation of the surcharge for POTW treatment of excess concentrations of wastewater
constituents discharged to the POTW sewer by significant industrial users, in accordance
with the provisions of section 14.08.650 , shall be subject to, but not limited to, the following conditions: Surcharges for
significant industrial users whose wastewater discharge is regulated by a duly issued
permit, as provided for in section 14.08.330 , shall be calculated using the concentration of BOD, TSS and TKN reported in their
wastewater discharge monitoring reports required under provisions of section 14.08.336 .
(Code 1993, § 14.08.660; Code 2009, § 14.08.660; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 21; Ord. No.
O-98-15, § 21; Ord. No. O-2012-67, § 4, 10-23-2012)
A.
Significant industrial users. The surcharge for excessive pollutant discharge to the
POTW, as provided for in section 14.08.650 , shall be calculated in accordance with the following formula:
Cs = {(Bc)(B) + (Sc)(S) + (Tc)(T) + (Pc)(P)} x (Vu) x 8.34
where:
"Cs" equals the monthly charge, in dollars, for POTW treatment of wastewaters with
excessive concentrations of regulated constituents.
"Bc" equals extra strength surcharge rate in dollars for treatment of one pound of
BOD.
"B" equals the concentration of BOD, expressed in milligrams per liter, discharged
by a POTW user in excess of an established base level.
"Sc" equals an extra-strength surcharge rate in dollars for treatment of one pound
of TSS.
"S" equals the concentration of TSS, expressed in milligrams per liter, discharged
by a POTW user in excess of an established base level.
"Tc" equals the extra-strength surcharge rate in dollars for treatment of one pound
of TKN.
"T" equals the concentration of TKN, expressed in milligrams per liter, discharged
by a POTW user in excess of an established base level.
"Pc" equals the extra-strength surcharge for treatment of any unit of pollutant, as
determined by the city council upon any future ordinance specifically applying to
any pollutant.
"P" means the concentration of any pollutant, expressed in milligrams per liter, discharged
by a POTW user in excess of an established base level.
"Vu" equals the total volume of wastewater, expressed in million gallons, discharged
monthly by a POTW user to the POTW.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.665; Code 2009, § 14.08.665; Ord. No. O-92-40, § 22; Ord. No.
O-98-15, § 22; Ord. No. O-2001-71, § 3; Ord. No. O-2008-78, § 15; Ord. No. O-2009-34,
§ 10, 7-14-2009; Ord. No. O-2012-67, § 5, 10-23-2012)
Editor's note— Ord. No. O-2012-67, § 6, adopted Oct. 23, 2012, repealed § 14.08.666, entitled "Excessive
pollutant surcharge adjustment for commercial and industrial use of irrigation water,"
which derived from: Ord. No. O-2008-78, § 16.
The director shall establish standard concentration values for wastewater constituents
subject to surcharge rates, as provided for in sections 14.08.650 through 14.08.665 . Standard concentration values will be based upon data obtained from the POTW wastewater
monitoring program or from nationally accepted industry standards. Any POTW user subject
to a surcharge for the discharge of excessive pollutants shall, at its own expense
and at no expense to the city, install suitable wastewater monitoring facilities in
accordance with plans and specifications approved by the director.
(Code 1993, § 14.08.670; Code 2009, § 14.08.670; Ord. No. O-87-42, § 1; Ord. No. O-92-17,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-98-15, § 23; Ord. No. O-2008-78, § 17; Ord. No. O-2009-34, § 11, 7-14-2009;
Ord. No. O-2012-67, § 7, 10-23-2012)
In addition to user charges provided for in section 14.08.640 and excess wastewater constituent surcharges provided for by sections 14.08.650 through 14.08.665 , there shall be added fees and charges for wastewater administration, including,
but not limited to, the following:
A.
Industrial wastewater discharge permit application or permit renewal fee, as follows:

Permitted Flow | Fee
One-time discharger approved by the director | $50.00
Zero discharger | $50.00
Industrial discharger | $500.00

The rates set forth in this section shall become effective January 1, 2009 and shall
continue in effect until changed.
B.
Industrial wastewater discharge permit administrative fee, based on permitted flow
and payable annually as follows:

Permitted Flow (gallons per day) | Annual Fee
< 500 | $300.00
500—25,000 | $700.00
> 25,000 | $1,000.00

The rates set forth in this section shall become effective January 1, 2009, and shall
continue in effect until changed.
C.
Laboratory analyses and support services, charged for performance of specific analytical
procedures, including charges for the cost of surveillance and surcharge monitoring
performed by the control authority, based upon the actual cost incurred by the POTW.
The charges shall be reviewed and adjusted annually by the director.
D.
Any other administrative costs incurred by the city and reasonably associated with
the user's discharge permit.
E.
An administrative fee equal to 25 percent of the cost for sewer maintenance. Sewer
maintenance includes cost of labor, equipment and materials required for removal of
sewer blockages or for excessive maintenance requirements.

The city council finds:
1.
The city council, a municipal corporation duly organized and existing as a home rule
city under article XX of the state constitution, acting on behalf of its water/wastewater
utility enterprise, is authorized pursuant to section 11.1 of the city's home rule
Charter and C.R.S. § 31-35-402, as amended, to provide water and sewer services within
or without the corporate limits of the city and to enter into water supply agreements
within or without the corporate limits of the city.
2.
Section 11.1 of the Charter provides that the city may provide municipal services outside of the
city limits if it clearly benefits the inhabitants of the city.
3.
It is the principal mission of the city's water/wastewater utility enterprise to provide
service within the city limits and to only provide service or raw water outside of
the city limits under circumstances which would clearly benefit the inhabitants of
the city, such as where providing service would protect the public health, safety
and welfare of the inhabitants of the city.
4.
From time to time, the city receives requests to provide water or sewer service or
raw water supply outside of the city limits, and it is desirable to set forth a uniform
policy for considering such applications. This chapter sets forth the city's policy
in considering such requests. The council's determination of such requests is legislative
and discretionary.
(Code 2009, § 14.09.010; Ord. No. O-2016-41, § 3, 6-14-2016)
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter shall have the meanings
ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates a different
meaning:
Raw water agreement means a raw water supply agreement, raw water rights lease agreement, raw water exchange
agreement, or any other formal or informal agreement or arrangement for the city to
provide raw water.
Subject political entity means any municipality with annexed territory within the water supply review zone,
and any water district (not including any water conservancy district or water conservation
district) whose territorial limits extend into any such municipality or into the water
supply review zone.
Urbanized area means any land within a Weld county designated regional urbanization area, or any
other area determined by the general manager of public works and natural resources
to contain an average residential density equal to or greater than one dwelling unit
per 2.5 acres or to have an average of 50,000 square feet or greater of gross floor
area devoted to nonresidential structures.
Water supply review zone means the geographical area bounded by Highway 66 on the north, Highway 52 on the
south, Interstate 25 on the east, and County Line Road on the west.
(Code 2009, § 14.09.015; Ord. No. O-2016-41, § 3, 6-14-2016)
A.
City staff may consider and process the following applications upon an initial determination
by staff that the agreement or contract arising from granting such service will protect
the public health, safety and welfare and thus clearly benefit the inhabitants of
the city. Upon receipt of such an application, staff shall process it as set forth
in subsection C of this section.
1.
Applications for water service for properties that are not within the water supply
review zone or the territory of a subject political entity, that will not be developed
beyond the existing use, and that have a failing or contaminated well.
2.
Applications for sewer service for properties that will not be developed beyond the
existing use and that have a failing septic system.
B.
The council presumes that other applications for water or sewer service outside of
the city limits will not clearly benefit the inhabitants of the city. Upon receipt
of such an application, staff shall reject the application and shall not process or
consider it further, unless directed otherwise by city council upon an initial finding
by council, by ordinance in the case of water service, that the agreement or contract
arising from granting such service would clearly benefit the inhabitants of the city.
C.
1.
All applications shall be on forms provided by the water/wastewater utility. The application
shall include an agreement setting forth the terms and conditions pursuant to which
service will be provided, including that the applicant agrees to annexation in the
future. If the property is eligible for annexation at the time the agreement is signed,
the agreement shall set forth the time in which the applicant must submit a petition
for annexation to the city. If the property is not eligible for annexation at the
time the agreement is signed, the agreement shall state that the applicant will submit
a petition for annexation when the property is eligible for annexation upon request
of the city. The applicant shall comply with all city requirements for annexations,
including, but not limited to, annexation agreements and the city's raw water requirement
policy. All applications and agreements are subject to review and decision by city
council.
2.
a.
All applications processed by staff for water service shall be forwarded to the city
water board for a recommendation. The board shall recommend approval if it determines
that the application will clearly benefit the inhabitants of the city. The application
will then be forwarded to city council for review and decision.
b.
All applications processed by staff for sewer service shall be forwarded to city council
for review and decision.
D.
The following shall not be subject to the provisions of this chapter:
1.
Sewer service outside city limits if required by the intergovernmental agreement between
the city and Boulder county concerning transferred development rights, included as
appendix E-1 of this Code, as it may be amended or extended.
2.
Water service pursuant to a tap trade between the city and a water district.
3.
Water or sewer service to city facilities, as the council finds that providing such
service clearly benefits the inhabitants of the city.
4.
If water or sewer service outside city limits is required as a result of a city construction
project.
(Code 2009, § 14.09.020; Ord. No. O-2016-41, § 3, 6-14-2016)
A.
Except as described below, the general manager of public works and natural resources,
or designee, may consider, process, and enter into raw water agreements one year or
less in duration, upon determining that the agreement or contract will protect the
public health, safety and welfare and thus clearly benefit the inhabitants of the
city. Rental rates for such agreements shall be based upon the current fully consumable
lease rate or the current surplus rental water market rates for agricultural or other
water uses.
B.
Except as described below, city staff may consider and process requests for raw water
agreements greater than one year in duration. All applications processed by staff
for water agreements shall be forwarded to the city water board for a recommendation.
The board shall recommend approval if it determines that the application will clearly
benefit the inhabitants of the city. The application will then be forwarded to city
council for review and decision.
C.
Policy on requests for raw water agreements to subject political entities and for
urbanized areas.
1.
The council presumes that requests for raw water agreements that would provide water
to a subject political entity or to an urbanized area will not clearly benefit the
inhabitants of the city. Upon receipt of such a request, staff shall reject the request
and shall not process or consider it further, unless directed otherwise by city council
upon an initial finding by council, by ordinance, that the agreement would clearly
benefit the inhabitants of the city. This subsection shall not apply to raw water
agreements for agricultural lands.
2.
Exceptions . This subsection C shall not apply to agreements for the exchange of water service
taps with water districts; nor to water the city supplies on a temporary, emergency
basis upon a declaration of disaster or emergency by the city manager or the state
governor, or upon such a declaration by a subject political entity if the city manager
determines actual disaster or emergency conditions exist; nor to agreements for infrastructure
installation for such disaster or emergency purposes. Nor shall this subsection affect
the terms of existing city contracts or intergovernmental agreements, but shall apply
to any extension thereof.
D.
Policy on requests for raw water agreements for oil and gas purposes.
1.
The city shall neither enter into nor process requests for raw water agreements that
would provide water for use in oil and gas operations.
2.
All raw water agreements processed by city staff shall contain provisions prohibiting
the use of water from the city in oil and gas operations, and provisions requiring
accounting of such water to prevent such use.
3.
This subsection D shall not apply to existing raw water agreements but shall apply
to any extension thereof.
(Code 2009, § 14.09.030; Ord. No. O-2016-41, § 3, 6-14-2016)
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter shall have the meanings
ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates a different
meaning:
Automated collection means that service which requires a city solid waste operations employee to operate
a collection vehicle which mechanically lifts and deposits discarded materials into
said vehicle from a city supplied container.
Commercial premises shall mean any premises that is not a residential unit where a commercial, industrial
or institutional enterprise is carried on, including, without limitation, retail establishments,
restaurants, hospitals, schools, day care centers, office buildings, nursing homes,
clubs, places of worship and public facilities.
Communal system for the collection of waste shall mean an arrangement for the collection of waste from multiple properties or
residences using collection containers shared by those properties or residences.
Containers means any solid waste, recycling, and organics collection receptacles including poly-carts,
dumpsters, or roll-off bins, supplied by the city or a licensed hauler to be used
for depositing solid waste, recyclable, and organic materials, respectively. Title
to city-supplied containers shall remain with the city.
Customer means any residential owner, occupant, or person having control of any residence,
home, condominium unit or apartment who subscribes to city solid waste, recycling,
or organics collection services.
Director means the city manager, or designated representative.
Economic hardship shall refer to a net loss of greater than ten percent of gross sales in the most
recently completed fiscal year.
Food scraps shall mean any animal- or vegetable-based foodstuffs, including food scraps resulting
from the preparation, cooking, and serving of food, unsaleable or outdated food, and
other compostable items such as organic waste from breweries and distilleries.
Hazardous waste , unless expressly provided otherwise, means the same as the term is defined by federal,
state, and local statutes, rules and regulations.
Household hazardous waste means discarded materials resulting from products purchased or used by the general
public for household purposes which, due to their quantity, concentration, or physical
or chemical characteristics, pose a substantial, or potential, hazard to human health
or the environment when improperly stored, treated, disposed of, or otherwise managed.
Infectious waste means any material incidental to, or resulting from, the operation of any hospital,
medical clinic, physician's office, veterinary clinic, or residential sickroom, as
defined by federal, state and local statutes, rules and regulations. The term "infectious
waste" also includes needles and syringes resulting from home use.
Innovation is considered an alternative to conventional waste services that diverts a minimum
of 50 percent of the total waste stream from the landfill through reuse or repurposing
of materials in an environmentally and socially responsible manner and that can be
documented for city staff to evaluate.
Landfill shall mean an area of land or excavation licensed by state and/or county authorities,
as applicable, to accept waste for permanent disposal.
Licensed hauler shall mean a company that has a current private refuse license per chapter 6.101 .
Organics collection services means any services provided by the city or a licensed hauler specifically to collect
organic materials.
Organic materials means organic matter such as grass, leaves, branches, food waste and any materials
accepted for composting by regional organics collection facilities.
Owner shall mean the owner of record, as shown by any records of the city, county or state
or any other record available to the city, whether an individual, individuals or entity,
and any agent or representative of the record owner. In any case where the owner,
by contract or otherwise, has assigned responsibility for waste management to tenants
or occupants, the owner shall remain a responsible party as that term is defined in chapter 2.97 .
Recyclable materials shall have the meaning as set forth in C.R.S. title 30, article 20, part 1 (C.R.S.
§ 30-20-101(4)), and shall generally include those aluminum, glass, plastic, cardboard
and paper products deemed acceptable for collection by regional recycling collection
centers.
Recycling shall mean the process of recovering useful materials from solid waste, including
items for reuse.
Residential solid waste or solid waste means all discarded putrescible and nonputrescible solid, semi-solid materials, including
recyclable materials, garbage, trash, and rubbish originating from residential units
and commercial premises. The term "residential solid waste" shall not include materials
infused with or containing poisons, explosives, dangerous or corrosive chemicals,
industrial materials, heavy metals or metal parts, lumber, rocks, bricks, concrete
blocks, motor vehicle parts, tires, sod, dirt, gravel, dead animals, materials from
construction or remodeling, discarded home and industrial appliances, hazardous waste,
household hazardous waste, or infectious wastes and materials, with the exception
of latex based paint containers containing solidified latex paint.
Residential unit means any single-family dwelling, multifamily dwelling or apartment house, but excluding
hotels.
Solid waste, recycling and organics collection services means and includes any manual, semi-automated, or automated collection provided by
the city or a licensed hauler for disposal, recycling, or organics collection services
of those discarded materials deemed acceptable by this chapter.
Special collection means fee-based services supplied by the city and available to customers on a first-come,
first-served basis when requested for disposal of qualifying solid waste materials.
Yard waste means yard clippings, grass, grass clippings, shrubs, clippings from bushes and shrubs,
wood, branches, leaves, weeds and twigs.
(Code 1993, § 14.12.010; Code 2009, § 14.12.010; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-2002-60,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2004-25, § 1; Ord. No. O-2011-36, § 1, 6-14-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-34,
§ 2, 5-24-2016; Ord. No. Ord. No. O-2023-26 , § 8, 7-11-2023)
A.
The city shall provide, and customers shall pay, for the routine curbside collection
and disposal of solid waste and recyclable materials, as defined in this chapter,
from all single-family and multifamily dwelling houses, including apartment houses,
but excluding hotels, within the city limits, except as otherwise provided in this
Code.
B.
The city manager, or designee, may promulgate rules and regulations associated with
the administration of this chapter and consistent with its purpose.
(Code 1993, § 14.12.020; Code 2009, § 14.12.020; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-2011-36,
§ 2, 6-14-2011)
A.
The city shall furnish containers and dumpsters to those residences receiving city-provided
solid waste and recycling collection services. The city shall assess a fee for each
container provided to a single-family dwelling, or to a living unit within any multifamily
dwelling having seven or fewer units. The fee shall be determined by the city's actual
cost to purchase, assemble and deliver the container. The city shall retain title
to such containers and dumpsters.
B.
The city shall issue no certificate of occupancy until the fee has been paid for each
living unit.
C.
Upon annexation of existing residential units, and the city's compliance with C.R.S.
§ 30-15-401(7.5)(b), the responsible customers shall pay the prescribed fee for such
containers or dumpsters.
D.
Each solid waste/recycling container shall be assigned to the residential unit and
not to the occupant of the residence.
(Code 1993, § 14.12.030; Code 2009, § 14.12.030; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-2019-18 , § 12, 3-19-2019; Ord. No. Ord. No. O-2023-26 , § 9, 7-11-2023; Ord. No. O-2025-62 , § 1, 10-28-2025)
A.
Customers shall place solid waste, recycling, and organics collection containers as
follows:
1.
Where the city collects at a public street, directly to the right or left of the driveway
at the curbline. Where the city collects at a public alley, adjacent to the premises
in the alley or across from the alley. The director may identify more specific locations.
2.
If the street or alley is not a public street or alley, it is the responsibility of
the customer to provide the city with the written consent of the owner of the street
or alley for city collection vehicles to use the street or alley for collection in
a location approved by the director.
3.
Containers shall be placed a minimum of two feet from each other and at least 3½ feet
on both sides of the container from any parked motor vehicle or stationary object.
B.
The combined weight of each container and its contents shall not exceed 335 pounds.
The contents shall securely fit into the container so the lid of the container can
close.
(Code 1993, § 14.12.040; Code 2009, § 14.12.040; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-2002-60,
§ 2; Ord. No. O-2011-36, § 3, 6-14-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-34, § 3, 5-24-2016; Ord. No. O-2025-62 , § 1, 10-28-2025)
The director shall prescribe and establish the routes and specified days of collection
and shall give timely notice to the public of changes.
(Code 1993, § 14.12.050; Code 2009, § 14.12.050; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1)
A.
Monthly subscription fees for service. Except as this chapter otherwise provides, the city assesses and each customer shall
pay the following monthly subscription fee for collection of residential solid waste
and recyclable materials, depending on the customer's level of service. To promote
efficient operations, the director may reasonably limit the number of times customers
may change their level of service each year.
1.
96-gallon service:

2026 | 2027 | 2028
$26.30 | $28.80 | $31.50

The rates set forth above shall become effective on January 1 of each year listed.
The rates set forth above shall remain in effect until changed.
2.
48-gallon service:

2026 | 2027 | 2028
$14.10 | $15.40 | $16.70

The rates set forth above shall become effective on January 1 of each year listed.
The rates set forth above shall remain in effect until changed.
3.
48-gallon, every-other-week service (24-gallon sized equivalent container):

2026 | 2027 | 2028
$7.10 | $7.70 | $8.30

The rates set forth above shall become effective on January 1 of each year listed.
The rates set forth above shall remain in effect until changed.
4.
Default multifamily service per unit:

2026 | 2027 | 2028
$14.10 | $15.40 | $16.70

The rates set forth above shall become effective on January 1 of each year listed.
The rates set forth above shall remain in effect until changed.
5.
Flexible multifamily service per unit: A multifamily customer may request a level of service comparable to that described
in subsection A.1 or A.3 of this section. The director may elect to provide such a
level of service in lieu of the default multifamily service, at a rate comparable
to the rates described in subsections A.1 and A.3 of this section, respectively, upon
finding that it is feasible and efficient to do so within the limitations of the city's
existing equipment and systems.
B.
The city also assesses a monthly waste management fee. The city shall use the fee
to operate and maintain city-wide waste management functions, including, but not limited
to: solid waste and recycling collection services for city departments, city facilities
and property (including, but not limited to, public buildings and parks) and city-wide
special events, Main Street collection, educational programs and all additional special
programs and services.
1.
Except as stated in subsection 2 of this section, each owner or utility billing customer
receiving service within the city shall be assessed and charged the following monthly
waste management fee. The owner or utility billing customer of any multi-family residential
building or multiple-unit nonresidential building shall be billed the monthly waste
management fee for each unit in the building if the units are not individually billed.

2026 | 2027 | 2028
$2.96 | $3.11 | $3.26

The rates set forth above shall become effective on January 1 of each year listed.
The rates set forth above shall remain in effect until changed.
2.
The following utility accounts are exempt from the monthly waste management fee: sprinkler
control boxes; utility boxes; telecommunications towers; and the electricity account
for a nonresident garage accessory to a single-family home when the home and garage
accounts are in the name of the same customer.
C.
Additional containers. Customers may procure an additional city-provided container for weekly collection
and disposal of additional solid waste. The city shall assess an additional monthly
charge as follows:
1.
96 gallon-container:

2026 | 2027 | 2028
$26.30 | $28.80 | $31.50

The rates set forth above shall become effective on January 1 of each year listed.
The rates set forth above shall remain in effect until changed.
2.
48-gallon container:

2026 | 2027 | 2028
$14.10 | $15.40 | $16.70

The rates set forth above shall become effective on January 1 of each year listed.
The rates set forth above shall remain in effect until changed.
D.
Special collections. The city establishes a fee for specific services, as set forth below, that will be
made available to customers on a first-come, first-served basis. These services may
be terminated indefinitely by the director based on equipment availability and other
city projects and priorities that may require the use of the city's sanitation equipment.
The city reserves the right to refuse to dispose of items that cannot be recycled,
landfilled, or handled by city staff and equipment. Only subscribing customers are
eligible for special collections. The fees for special collections are:
1.
Large item pick-up. $60.00 for two items, $15.00 for one additional item. A list of qualifying large
items will be available upon request and published on the city's web site. The customer
shall schedule all large item pick-ups by appointment with the solid waste operations
division. No person shall place or permit placement of such solid waste at curbside
or in the public right-of way or alley sooner than the Saturday before the appointed
day for such collection.
2.
Dumpster rentals. $165.00 per dumpster.
3.
Organics collection services. The city assesses and each customer shall pay the following monthly subscription
fee for a single-family dwelling for an every-other-week collection of a 96-gallon
or 48-gallon sized container provided by the city. Additional containers may be provided
for an additional monthly subscription fee of equivalent amount. The minimum subscription
term shall be 12 months. Customers wishing to terminate organics collection service
prior to the end of any term shall pay a $40.00 termination fee, except that no fee
shall be assessed for a customer terminating all utility services with the city. The
director may also extend organics collection services to a multifamily dwelling, upon
finding that it is feasible and efficient to do so within the limitations of the city's
existing equipment and systems, at a per-month fee equivalent or comparable to the
single-family rate considering any greater or lesser cost to the city of providing
the service, if the containers for the compostable containers meet the requirements
of section 14.12.040 .

2026 | 2027 | 2028
$9.90 | $12.40 | $14.90

The rates set forth above shall become effective on January 1 of each year listed.
The rates set forth above shall remain in effect until changed.
E.
Unscheduled special collection. City customers who pay for residential solid waste collection services as part of
their utility bills shall also pay the above special collection and disposal fee for
each unscheduled special collection and disposal of material placed contrary to section 14.12.110 , if the director deems the special collection necessary in the interest of public
health and safety. The director shall give reasonable advance notice to the resident
of this provision and of intent to collect and dispose before such collection. If
the director deems a special collection necessary to protect the public health and
safety from immediate hazard, the director may authorize such collection without the
requirement for advance notice. Every other person so placing or permitting such placement
shall pay the city said fee and costs and reasonable attorneys' fees incurred in collecting
it. Payment of or suit to collect these fees shall not bar criminal prosecution or
other remedies for violation of this Code.
(Code 1993, § 14.12.060; Code 2009, § 14.12.060; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-2002-60,
§ 3; Ord. No. O-2008-18, § 1; Ord. No. O-2011-36, § 4, 6-14-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-33,
§ 2, 5-24-2016; Ord. No. O-2016-34, § 4, 5-24-2016; Ord. No. O-2021-73 , § 4, 12-14-2021; Ord. No. O-2025-62 , § 1, 10-28-2025)
Editor's note— Sec. 5 of Ord. No. O-2011-36, adopted June 14, 2011, repealed § 14.12.065 which pertained
to private container collection, and derived from the 1993 Code; and Ord. No. O-2004-25.
A.
The city shall repair or replace containers that incur damage through routine use
at no cost to the customer.
B.
The city shall assess container repair or replacement costs to the customer if the
city finds that said replacements and/or repairs were caused by nonroutine use, including,
but not limited to, abuse, misplacement, or neglect of containers.
C.
It shall be the responsibility of the customer to notify the city when repair and/or
replacement of solid waste or recycling containers is necessary. The director shall
determine the criteria for repair or replacement of such containers.
(Code 1993, § 14.12.070; Code 2009, § 14.12.070; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-2002-60,
§ 4; Ord. No. O-2016-33, § 3, 5-24-2016)
A.
The city reserves the right to refuse solid waste, recycling and organics collection
of all material, or any portion thereof, placed for collection that fails to comply
with the requirements of this chapter. The solid waste operations supervisor, or designee,
shall label, or tag, those materials not collected to note the specific infraction,
and shall refuse collection of these materials until the customer has corrected the
cause for such refusal and paid the appropriate service fee/charge, if any is required.
B.
Any action taken by the city under this section shall not preclude action by the city
under section 14.12.110 .
(Code 1993, § 14.12.080; Code 2009, § 14.12.080; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-2002-60,
§ 5; Ord. No. O-2016-34, § 5, 5-24-2016)
A.
The owner or utility billing customer for every dwelling unit within any multifamily
residential building having eight or more units within the city shall subscribe to
and pay for at least weekly solid waste collection and disposal from such dwelling
unit, either through the services of the city or a licensed private hauler.
B.
The owner or utility billing customer for every dwelling unit within any multifamily
residential building within the city shall be billed the waste management fee for
all units in the building if the units are not individually billed by the city for
water or electric service.
C.
Upon presentation to the utility billing office of a contract for solid waste collection
by a single licensed private solid waste hauler, providing for at least weekly collection
for every unit within a multifamily dwelling building having eight or more units,
the city shall relieve the responsible party of the obligation to pay for, and shall
not provide the building with city-provided solid waste and recycling collection services.
The city shall remove any city-provided solid waste containers from the premises.
The city shall not return city containers unless, upon request of the owner of the
multifamily building, it again serves the building and bills responsible parties for
municipal solid waste and recycling collection services.
D.
Upon termination of service to any unit in a multifamily residential building having
eight or more units, a licensed hauler shall notify the utility billing office of
the names and addresses of the building and customers within the building, who shall
then become subject to the city-provided solid waste and recycling collection service
charges and shall receive the city-provided solid waste and recycling service.
(Code 1993, § 14.12.090; Code 2009, § 14.12.090; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-2021-73 , § 5, 12-14-2021; Ord. No. Ord. No. O-2023-26 , § 10, 7-11-2023)
A.
The owner of any multifamily dwelling that contracts with a licensed hauler for removal
of solid waste per 14.12.090 shall subscribe to and pay for recycling collection service at a level that accommodates
the total amount of recyclable materials generated by the dwelling units, with the
frequency of collection at least every other week. The owner shall provide waste diversion
signage in a conspicuous location near containers in at least English and Spanish
with clear symbology, and provide adequate education of tenants and facility managers
to ensure proper sorting and minimize contamination.
1.
Multifamily dwellings with 75 or more units shall contract for this service to begin
no later than January 1, 2024.
2.
Multifamily dwellings with up to 74 residential units shall contract for this service
to begin no later than June 1, 2024.
3.
Multifamily dwellings receiving solid waste services from the city will also receive
recycling collection services from the city.
B.
Exemptions. Any of the properties identified in the previous paragraph may apply for an exemption
if they meet of or more of the following criteria:
1.
De minimis volume: The applicant generates less than 96 gallons of solid waste per week total.
2.
Self-haul: The applicant self-hauls recyclable materials to a recyclables processing center
permitted by state and/or county authorities, as applicable, to accept such material
(but not to a landfill).
i.
The applicant must provide the destination facility name and location, as well as
sufficient evidence of self-hauling (e.g., a recent receipt with date and amount of
material).
ii.
The applicant must fully comply with all other requirements stated in this section.
3.
Space constraints: The location does not have space for recycling containers or the only available location
for recycling containers is not safely serviceable by hauler.
i.
The applicant must explore all possible options to fully comply with this section,
such as reducing the size of the container for non-recyclable waste and exploring
options to adjust enclosure size;
ii.
The applicant must provide a description and photograph of the area where the space
constraints exist and document efforts from item (i).
4.
Innovation: A minimum of 50 percent of the total solid waste generated is diverted from the landfill
though reuse or repurposing.
i.
The applicant has identified a method to reduce trash service through reuse or repurposing
of materials in an environmentally and socially responsible manner;
ii.
Applicant must provide a description of the innovation and proof of sufficient waste
diversion as a result of the innovation.
5.
Upon receipt of such a request for exemption, the director shall either approve the
exemption for good cause shown, request additional information from the applicant
that the director, in their sole discretion, requires to fully consider the application
for exemption or disapprove the exemption. For purposes of this section, "good cause
shown" shall mean evidence presented by the applicant that, to the reasonable satisfaction
of the director, demonstrates that the applicant meets one or more of the aforementioned
exemption criteria. The director shall issue a written decision on the application
within ten working days and, if disapproved, shall state the reasons in writing.
6.
Disapproval of an application for exemption may be appealed by filing a written appeal
with the city clerk within ten days of the date of service of the director's decision
setting forth the reasons why the appeal should be granted. Such appeal shall be conducted
pursuant to chapter 2.98 .
7.
If an exemption is approved in accordance with the foregoing provisions, the applicant
shall not be required to subscribe to recycling services for the two-year period following
approval of the exemption. If the constraints on which the exemption was based still
exist at the end of the exemption period, the applicant may submit a request for continued
exemption, providing sufficient details regarding current conditions to enable the
director to find good cause to grant the request.
(Ord. No. Ord. No. O-2023-26 , § 11, 7-11-2023)
A.
The owner of commercial premises shall subscribe to the services of a licensed private
hauler for recycling collection service at a level that accommodates the total amount
of recyclable materials generated by the commercial premises, providing for at least
every other week collection. The owner shall provide waste diversion signage in a
conspicuous location near containers in at least English and Spanish with clear symbology,
and provide adequate education of employees, tenants and facility managers to ensure
proper sorting and minimize contamination.
1.
Commercial premises of 10,000 or more square feet in total shall contract for this
service to begin no later than January 1, 2024.
2.
Commercial premises of less than 10,000 square feet in total shall contract for this
service to begin no later than June 1, 2024.
B.
Exemptions. Any of the aforementioned premises may apply for an exemption if they meet one or
more of the following criteria:
1.
De minimis volume: The applicant generates less than 96 gallons of solid waste per week total;
2.
Self-haul: The applicant self-hauls recyclable materials to a recyclables processing center
permitted by state and/or county authorities, as applicable, to accept such material
(but not to a landfill).
i.
The applicant must provide the destination facility name and location, as well as
sufficient evidence of self-hauling (e.g. a receipt with date and amount of material);
ii.
The applicant must meet all other requirements stated in this section.
3.
Sharing: The applicant shares a recycling container(s) through a communal system for the collection
of waste as defined in section 14.12.010 .
4.
Economic hardship: Losses from the prior fiscal year were greater than ten percent of gross sales. The
applicant must submit their most recent profit and loss statements from the prior
fiscal year.
5.
Space constraints: The location does not have space for recycling containers or the only available location
for recycling containers is not safely serviceable by hauler.
i.
The applicant must explore all possible options to fully comply with this section,
such as reducing the size of the container for non-recyclable waste and exploring
options to adjust enclosure size;
ii.
The applicant must provide a description and photograph of the area where the space
constraints exist and document efforts from item (i).
6.
Innovation: A minimum of 50 percent of the total solid waste generated is diverted from the landfill
though reuse or repurposing.
i.
The applicant has identified a method to reduce trash service through reuse or repurposing
of materials in an environmentally and socially responsible manner;
ii.
Applicants must provide a description of the innovation and proof of sufficient waste
diversion as a result of the innovation.
7.
Upon receipt of such a request for exemption, the director shall either approve the
exemption for good cause shown, request additional information from the applicant
that the director, in their sole discretion, requires to fully consider the application
for exemption or disapprove the exemption. For purposes of this section, "good cause
shown" shall mean evidence presented by the applicant that, to the reasonable satisfaction
of the director, demonstrates that the applicant meets one or more of the aforementioned
exemption criteria. The director shall issue a written decision on the application
within ten working days and, if disapproved, shall state the reasons in writing.
8.
Disapproval of an application for exemption may be appealed by filing a written appeal
with the city clerk within ten days of the date of service of the director's decision
setting forth the reasons why the appeal should be granted. Such appeal shall be conducted
pursuant to chapter 2.98 .
i.
If an exemption is approved in accordance with the foregoing provisions, the applicant
shall not be required to subscribe to recycling services for the two-year period following
approval of the exemption. If the constraints on which the exemption was based still
exist at the end of the exemption period, the applicant may submit a request for continued
exemption, providing sufficient details regarding current conditions to enable the
director to find good cause to grant the request.
(Ord. No. Ord. No. O-2023-26 , § 12, 7-11-2023)
A.
The owner of any food-related business, as more specifically described herein, shall
subscribe to the services of a licensed private hauler for organics collection service
at a level that accommodates the total amount of food scraps generated by the business,
providing for at least weekly collection. The owner shall provide waste diversion
signage in a conspicuous location near containers in at least English and Spanish
with clear symbology, and provide adequate education of employees, tenants and facility
managers to ensure proper sorting and minimize contamination.
1.
Food wholesalers, food manufacturers, food distributers and grocery stores shall contract
for this service to begin no later than January 1, 2025.
2.
The following business types shall contract for this service to begin no later than
June 1, 2025, required only for back of house volume as applicable:
i.
Restaurants, cafes, lunch counters, cafeterias, bars that prepare and serve food,
kitchens in hotels, hospitals, factories or other for-profit or nonprofit organizations
providing food services;
ii.
Food trucks;
iii.
Food pantries and food banks;
iv.
Nursing and residential care facilities;
v.
Public/rental commissaries or shared use commercial kitchens;
vi.
Caterers;
vii.
Breweries and distilleries;
viii.
Office building with dining services;
ix.
Colleges with dining services;
x.
Golf courses and country clubs at which food is prepared and served;
xi.
Event centers at which food is prepared and served;
xii.
Other businesses as deemed appropriate in the director's sole discretion.
B.
Yard waste—Service requirement.
1.
Any commercial entity that produces yard waste within the city shall divert yard waste
from the landfill through composting beginning no later than January 1, 2029.
C.
Exemptions. Any of the aforementioned premises may apply for an exemption if they meet one or
more of the following criteria:
1.
De minimis volume: the applicant generates less than 96 gallons of solid waste per week total or generates
no organic materials waste;
2.
Self-haul: the applicant self-hauls organic materials to a location or facility permitted by
state and/or county authorities, as applicable, to accept organic materials for composting
(but not to a landfill).
i.
The applicant must provide the destination facility name and location, as well as
sufficient evidence of self-hauling (e.g. a receipt with date and amount of material);
ii.
The applicant must still meet all other requirements stated in this section.
3.
Sharing: the applicant shares an organics container(s) through a communal system for the collection
of waste as defined in section 14.12.010 .
4.
Economic hardship: Losses from the prior fiscal year were greater than ten percent of gross sales. The
applicant must submit their most recent profit and loss statements from the prior
fiscal year.
5.
Space constraints: the location does not have space for organics containers, or the only available location
for organics containers is not safely serviceable by hauler.
i.
The applicant must explore all possible options to fully comply with this section,
such as reducing the size of the containers for other waste and exploring options
to adjust enclosure size.
ii.
The applicant must provide a description and photograph of the area where the space
constraints exist and document efforts from item (i).
6.
Innovation: a minimum of 50 percent of the total solid waste generated is diverted from the landfill
though reuse or repurposing.
i.
The applicant has identified a method to reduce trash service through reuse or repurposing
of materials in an environmentally and socially responsible manner;
ii.
Applicant must provide a description of the innovation and proof of sufficient waste
diversion after the innovation was implemented.
7.
Food donation: the applicant donates food scraps for human or feed animal consumption, in accordance
with applicable state and local health and safety regulations.
8.
On-site composting: the applicant composts all organic waste on-site in a manner that complies with all
applicable laws, codes and ordinances.
9.
Upon receipt of such a request for exemption, the director shall either approve the
exemption for good cause shown request additional information from the applicant that
the director, in their sole discretion, requires to fully consider the application
for exemption or disapprove the exemption. For purposes of this section, "good cause
shown" shall mean evidence presented by the applicant that, to the reasonable satisfaction
of the director, demonstrates that the applicant meets one or more of the aforementioned
exemption criteria. The director shall issue a written decision on the application
within ten working days and, if disapproved, shall state the reasons in writing.
10.
Disapproval of an application for exemption may be appealed by filing a written appeal
with the city clerk within ten days of the date of service of the director's decision
setting forth the reasons why the appeal should be granted. Such appeal shall be conducted
pursuant to chapter 2.98 .
11.
If an exemption is approved in accordance with the foregoing provisions, the applicant
shall not be required to subscribe to organics collection services for the two-year
period following approval of the exemption. If the constraints on which the exemption
was based still exist at the end of the exemption period, the applicant may submit
a request for continued exemption, providing sufficient details regarding current
conditions to enable the director to find good cause to grant the request.
(Ord. No. Ord. No. O-2023-26 , § 13, 7-11-2023)
A.
Authority to operate. The city may operate a waste diversion center for drop-off of recyclable materials
and disposal of tree limbs at a location determined by the director. The days and
hours of operation shall be determined by the director.
B.
Fees for tree limb disposal.
1.
Use by city residents for noncommercial use. Residents of the city shall have free use of the waste diversion center for their
noncommercial use.
2.
Use by noncustomers. Noncustomers, including, but not limited to, building contractors, shall pay a fee
as set forth below. The director may close use of the waste diversion center for tree
limb disposal to noncustomers if the director determines that the volume of such use
is interfering with the use by customers for their noncommercial use.
a.
Full-sized pick-up trucks. The fee shall be $35.00 for a level pick-up load and $40.00 for a cab-high pick-up
load.
b.
Mini pick-up trucks. The fee shall be $30.00 for a level pick-up load and $35.00 for a cab-high pick-up
load.
c.
Cars and minivans. The fee shall be $25.00 regardless of how full the vehicle is.
d.
Full-sized vans. The fee shall be $35.00 regardless of how full the vehicle is.
e.
Others. For any vehicle not listed above, the fee shall be $20.00 per cubic yard.
C.
Regulations. The director may promulgate and post at the waste diversion center rules and regulations
governing its use.
(Code 1993, § 14.12.095; Code 2009, § 14.12.095; Ord. No. O-2004-25, § 3; Ord. No.
O-2011-36, § 6, 6-14-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-33, § 4, 5-24-2016)
The city will not collect the following materials: Materials and debris produced by
contracted services, such as construction, remodeling or landscape services, in or
about the dwelling; concrete, rocks, dirt, sod; solid waste produced by home occupations
or any businesses; hazardous waste; infectious waste; dead animal carcasses (other
than small pest animals); bulk liquids; and automobile or truck bodies, chassis and
parts.
(Code 1993, § 14.12.100; Code 2009, § 14.12.100; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-2011-36,
§ 7, 6-14-2011; Ord. No. O-2012-80, § 2, 12-4-2012)
Violations listed below shall be deemed a public nuisance, and shall subject the responsible
party to the administrative civil penalties as defined in chapter 2.97 and further outlined in section 2.97.040.C. In addition to such penalties, the city
attorney is authorized to bring an action before any court of competent jurisdiction
to enjoin violations. The occupant and the owner of any premises wherein any solid
waste is produced or accumulated shall be jointly and severally responsible for compliance
with this chapter. It is a violation of this chapter:
A.
To refuse, fail to surrender, accommodate, or arrange for the authorized retrieval
by designated city employees, of any city-provided containers when so demanded by
the city, for reasons including, but not limited to, the termination of city collection
services within the time specified by the director.
B.
To place for recycling collection services anything other than accepted recyclable
materials, or otherwise to contaminate any recyclable materials by introducing non-accepted
recyclable materials into the recycling collection system.
C.
To place for organics collection services anything other than accepted compostable
materials, or otherwise to contaminate any compostable materials in the organics collection
services system.
D.
To fail to contract for waste collection, recycling collection, or organics collection
as required in this chapter, in the absence of an applicable exemption.
E.
To store, transport, or dispose of infectious waste in any city-provided container
in a manner that violates applicable federal, state and local statutes, rules and
regulations.
F.
To place containers for collection within the public right-of-way, before 4:00 p.m.
of the day before the scheduled collection, or to fail to remove containers from the
public right-of-way no later than 8:00 p.m. the day following collection.
G.
To place a container for collection on a sidewalk located within a public right-of-way
in a manner that impedes pedestrian access and/or use.
H.
To place any solid waste in any street, alley, or other public place within the city,
except in containers specified as acceptable for collection and in conformance with
the requirements for routine curbside, supplemental and special collection programs.
I.
To keep, place or maintain solid waste or solid waste, recycling, or organics collection
containers contrary to the requirements of this chapter, or to violate any other provision
of this chapter, including any implementing rules and regulations.
J.
To place tree limbs and leaves in any street, alley, or other public place within
the city, except during such time that the city designates a collection program for
such waste. The city manager or designee may promulgate rules and regulations governing
such programs, including placement of such waste and the size of the tree limbs that
will be collected. Tree limbs may also be disposed of at the waste diversion center
in conformance with section 14.12.095 .
(Code 1993, § 14.12.110; Code 2009, § 14.12.110; Ord. No. O-99-52, § 1; Ord. No. O-99-58,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2011-36, § 8, 6-14-2011; Ord. No. O-2012-80, § 3, 12-4-2012; Ord.
No. O-2016-34, § 6, 5-24-2016; Ord. No. Ord. No. O-2023-26 , § 14, 7-11-2023; Ord. No. O-2025-62 , § 1, 10-28-2025)
The city council establishes a uniform billing system for the city's utilities, which
may be referred to collectively as "utility billing rules and regulations." This chapter
does not apply to broadband services.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.010; Code 2009, § 14.20.010; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2014-67, § 1, 11-10-2014)
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter, shall have the
meanings ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates
a different meaning:
Customer means an adult who has requested utility service for a service address.
Mail or delivery means, when referring to a notice or other document being mailed or delivered, that
the notice or the document is deposited in the United States mail or physically delivered
to the address of the addressee, and does not mean actual physical receipt by the
addressee.
Owner means one or more owners of a service address according to the records of the Boulder
or Weld county clerk and recorder.
Service address means the address to which utility service is provided.
Third party means a party identified in writing by a customer to whom the city shall provide
written notification of a delinquency in payment of a utility bill.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.020; Code 2009, § 14.20.020; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
A.
Approval of an application for utility service by the city and acceptance of utility
service by the customer constitutes a contract between the city and the customer consisting
of the provisions of this chapter. No promise, agreement, or representation of any
employee of the city shall be binding on the city, unless the city and the customer
incorporate the promise, agreement, or representation into the customer's application
for utility service. By accepting utility service, each customer agrees to provide
the city utility billing division with all information requested by the city to determine
the appropriate rate schedule.
B.
The city may require nonresidential customers to sign a written contract containing
terms consistent with this chapter that the city determines are necessary or desirable
to protect the interests of both the city and the customer. The council may modify
the obligations of the city, the customer or both, as stated in this chapter in a
written contract.
C.
All adults residing at a service address that receives utility service from the city
are jointly and severally liable for payment of the utility service. No one shall
connect utilities in another's name to avoid payment of a prior balance due for utility
services. In such cases, the city may transfer the balance owed to the new account.
No one shall connect utilities in the name of a minor.
D.
Proof of residency may be required before service is connected. Proof of residency
includes, but is not limited to, leases, rental agreements and deeds.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.030; Code 2009, § 14.20.030; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
A.
The city will issue bills monthly for the cost of service provided which are due and
payable 20 days after the billing date. The city will allow at least 15 days between
mailing or delivery of a bill and the due date of a bill. Failure to receive a bill
does not relieve a customer from the obligation for timely payment. In the event of
a billing error, the city will refund or credit any overcharge against the next bill.
The city may collect or debit the amount of any undercharge for a period of two years
from the date of discovery of the undercharge.
B.
If the city connects service seven days or less before the initial reading date, the
city will not issue a separate bill for this period. The city will bill for this initial
period of service as part of the next regular billing.
C.
Whenever the city is unable to read the customer's meter, the city may issue an estimated
bill for the month, based on the best available information.
D.
Whenever a different reading exists between a remote reading device and a primary
water meter, the city will use the reading from the primary meter for billing purposes.
E.
Once the city transfers or discontinues service, the city will compute and mail the
final bill within the next regular billing cycle.
F.
The city may set forth charges for all services provided by the city to a particular
customer in one combined bill. Once an amount becomes past due, future bills will
not reflect a breakdown of such past due amounts for each separate utility service.
The city will apply any payment by a customer receiving a combined bill proportionately
between the amounts due for the applicable services provided by the city.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.040; Code 2009, § 14.20.040; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
A.
Subject to the provisions of this chapter, the city may require a deposit from any
customer to secure payment of bills. If, at any time, the city determines that a previous
deposit from a customer is insufficient, the city may require an additional deposit.
B.
The initial deposit of a commercial customer shall be in an amount not to exceed the
estimated charges of such user for the highest consecutive 90-day consumption period.
C.
The city shall retain a deposit for two years unless the city discontinues service.
At the end of the initial two-year period, the city will examine the customer's payment
record. If during the previous 12 months the customer was delinquent, the city will
retain the deposit for an additional 12 months. The city will repeat this process
each year. If the customer was not delinquent the previous 12 months, the city will
apply the deposit to the customer's bill. A customer shall be considered delinquent
when, during the previous 12 months, the city sent the customer a collection notice
or two or more bills listing an amount past due.
D.
The city will pay the customer accrued simple interest at the rate set by the state
public utilities commission for the period the city holds a deposit.
E.
Except as stated in subsection C of this section, the city will not apply a customer's
deposit to the customer's bill until the city discontinues or transfers the service.
When the city computes a final bill after discontinuance of service, the city will
apply the deposit to the bill. The city will refund any deposit in excess of the outstanding
balance to the customer, unless the customer has an outstanding balance for service
to another address, in which case the city may apply the credit balance to the outstanding
balance at the other address. To the extent the outstanding balance due is in excess
of the deposit, the city will bill the customer for the remaining amount due. In the
event the deposit is insufficient to satisfy the entire bill and the customer fails
to pay the remaining amount due, application of the deposit to amounts past due does
not constitute a settlement or satisfaction of the customer's bill or otherwise limit
the city's right to pursue all legal remedies in an effort to collect any amount due.
F.
In the event a customer files a petition in bankruptcy, section 14.20.120 shall apply.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.050; Code 2009, § 14.20.050; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
A.
No deposit shall be required to connect or transfer owner-occupied residential service
unless:
(1)
The city has ever discontinued service to the customer for nonpayment; or
(2)
Within the previous 12 months, the city has sent the customer a collection notice
or two bills listing an amount past due for a single service location. The city may
require a residential customer to provide evidence of home ownership when applying
for service.
B.
A $100.00 deposit shall be required to connect or transfer non-owner occupied residential
service. The city waives this deposit requirement for customers who have not been
delinquent as defined in this chapter within the preceding 12 months. A letter from
another electric utility indicating that the customer disconnected service within
the previous six months and was not delinquent, as defined in this chapter, for the
last 12 months of service at the previous utility will satisfy the requirement for
a deposit.
C.
In the event the city disconnects service to any active residential customer for nonpayment
or violation of any provisions of this Code, the customer is required to provide full
payment of all delinquent charges, and disconnect and reconnect fees, prior to reconnection
of service.
D.
The city shall charge any residential customer with a delinquent utility bill which
was not discharged in bankruptcy, a deposit of $200.00 before reconnecting service
if the city disconnected service to the customer. The city shall charge any residential
customer with a delinquent utility bill from more than one location that was not discharged
in bankruptcy, a deposit of $300.00. Payment of a deposit does not relieve a customer
of the obligation to pay a delinquent utility bill.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.060; Code 2009, § 14.20.060; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
All nonresidential service, except for service to federal, state, county, and municipal
government facilities, requires a deposit in one of the following forms and amounts
prior to connection or transfer:
1.
A deposit equal to the estimated charges for 90 days of service or $200.00, whichever
is greater:
a.
Cash (if the deposit requirement is in excess of $200.00, the customer may use a three-month
payment plan);
b.
Irrevocable letter of credit from a financial institution licensed to do business
in the state in a form acceptable to the city attorney;
c.
Guarantee from a financial institution licensed to do business in the state based
upon a cash deposit, in a form acceptable to the city attorney; and/or
d.
Any other form found acceptable to the city attorney, except performance bonds;
2.
A deposit equal to the estimated charge for 30 days of service and a written contract
with the city authorizing the city to contact the customer by letter or telephone
regarding potential utility shutoff if payment of any bill covering one month of service
is not received in the city's utility billing division four calendar days after the
due date appearing on the bill. If said delinquent amount is not paid in full six
calendar days after the due date appearing on the bill, or two calendar days after
a notice of discontinuance is served on the customer, the customer agrees that the
city is authorized to then discontinue service for nonpayment. In the event of discontinuance
of service, the city may apply the deposit to the amount due and require a new deposit
and payment of any remaining unpaid balance before restoring service to the customer.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.070; Code 2009, § 14.20.070; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
A.
The city will charge the following fee for initial connection of service:
1.
Residential customer: $22.00.
2.
Commercial customer: $38.00.
B.
It is the customer's responsibility to pay each utility bill when due. The city will
charge the customer a fee based on the then current city financial policy, for each
returned check, electronic fund transfer return, and each charged back credit card
transaction.
C.
The city will charge a customer a combined notification fee and penalty charge of
$10.00 when: (1) the customer's account is unpaid 27 days after the original due date;
or (2) the city visits the service address to hang a tag for disconnection. In addition,
the city will charge the following fees whenever the city disconnects service for
fraudulent use or noncompliance with this chapter:

1. | | Disconnection or reconnection
| | | Regular Hours | Non-Regular Hours
| a. | At the meter | $29.00 | $92.00
| b. | At the electric pole | $183.00 | $386.00
| c. | At the curb stop or meter pit | $30.00 | N/A

D.
Actual cost of installing a remote electric, water or combined electric and water
reading device for customers with a utility meter located in an enclosed area of the
service address who request that the city read their meters remotely without entering
the enclosed area of the service address.
E.
When a contract is signed for an interim connection under this chapter, the city will
charge the property owner or the owner's agent a fee of $3.00 plus $10.00 per connection.
F.
The city may add to the balance due all collection costs and fees, including attorneys'
fees, incurred by the city or its representative in collecting the balance owed by
the customer on disconnected utility accounts.
G.
Owners of every building, premises, lot or structure, and lessees at the Vance Brand
Municipal Airport, shall be liable for all water delivered to the premises and any
wastewater collected from the premises. All water and wastewater rents and rates shall
be a charge and lien upon the premises to which water is delivered and wastewater
collected, from the date the same becomes due until paid. The city may collect such
liens and liabilities by an action at law or equity to enforce the lien. In addition
to an action at law or equity to enforce the lien, all water rents and rates may be
certified by the clerk to the Boulder or Weld county treasurer to be collected and
paid over by the Boulder or Weld county treasurer in the same manner as taxes. In
case the tenant in possession of the premises or building contracts to pay the water
and wastewater rent, such contract does not relieve the property owner from such obligation
and lien, and the city shall not be required to look to any person whatsoever, other
than the owner, for the payment of water and wastewater rents and rates.
H.
All residential electric customers whose consumption is monitored by a manually read
meter will be permanently transitioned, at no charge to the customer, to a standard
wirelessly communicating, advanced metering infrastructure (AMI) meter, capable of
being read remotely, and all new residential meter installations will be AMI, unless
customer elects to opt out of AMI and meets the eligibility requirements to opt out.
Only residential, single-phase customers are eligible to opt out. Where the residential,
single-phase customer is not the property owner, signed permission of the property
owner is required in the form prescribed by LPC to establish eligibility to opt out.
Customers desiring to opt out of AMI must complete and return an enrollment form and
agreement provided by LPC. Once approved, all eligible customers opting out of AMI
will pay a recurring monthly charge of $24.65 for manual read of the non-AMI meter
in addition to a one-time service installation and administrative fee of $50.00 if
the opt out enrollment form and agreement is returned to LPC within 14 days of LPC's
provision of notice of the scheduled AMI meter installation; or $125.00 if returned
any time after such 14-day notice. The one-time fee shall be non-refundable and shall
apply once to that customer at that premises. If a customer has elected to opt out
and has a non-AMI meter installed, but then later elects to opt in to an AMI meter,
LPC will install and activate an AMI meter at that premises at no additional cost.
A customer shall become ineligible for continued participation as an opt out customer
in the event of customer's noncompliance with the regulations governing electric service
set forth in sections 14.32.190 through 14.32.240 or customer's disconnection of service for nonpayment two times
within any 12-month period.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.080; Code 2009, § 14.20.080; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2009-70, § 1, 12-8-2009; Ord. No. O-2014-67, § 2, 11-10-2014; Ord. No. O-2022-06 , § 2, 2-22-2022; Ord. No. O-2025-53 , § 2, 10-28-2025)
A returned check, electronic fund transfer return, or charged back credit card transaction
received by the city constitutes nonpayment of the amount of the returned check, electronic
fund transfer return or charged back credit card transaction. When a customer makes
a payment resulting in a returned check, electronic fund transfer return, or charged
back credit card transaction following a notice of discontinuance, the city may discontinue
service without additional notice. If the city receives three insufficient funds checks,
electronic fund transfer returns, or charged back credit card transactions or any
combination thereof, from any customer, the city may, following written notice to
the customer, require all future payment to be made in cash or certified funds.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.090; Code 2009, § 14.20.090; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
When a change of occupancy or of legal responsibility for an account occurs regarding
any premises served by the city, the outgoing customer shall give notice of such change
within two business days before such change. The city shall hold the outgoing customer
responsible for all service provided for two business days after the city receives
such notice.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.100; Code 2009, § 14.20.100; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
A.
The city will provide service to an owner following a request by a tenant to terminate
service if an owner enters into an interim service contract with the city. Interim
service agreements require owners to:
1.
Be responsible for all services following receipt of a request to terminate service
from a tenant until service is terminated upon request of the owner or transferred
to another tenant; and
2.
Pay the interim contract fee established by this chapter.
B.
Interim services includes the services and their related fees or billing rates provided
upon the interim connection, including, where applicable, electric, water, wastewater,
storm drainage and sanitation.
C.
In the event that an owner has one or more interim accounts and one or more of those
accounts becomes delinquent, as defined in the interim contract, the city may cancel
any interim account of the customer and disconnect service until the past due amount
of all interim accounts are paid in full.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.110; Code 2009, § 14.20.110; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
A.
In the event any customer files a bankruptcy petition, the city will charge a three-month
estimated usage deposit pursuant to the Federal Bankruptcy Code, title 11 USC 366
as security for services provided to the customer after the date the customer files
a bankruptcy petition. The deposit is due and payable within 20 days of the city's
receipt of notice of filing the bankruptcy petition. The city will return the deposit
as described in this chapter consistent with the bankruptcy code. Unless excused by
the bankruptcy court, failure to pay a deposit in the time specified may result in
disconnection of service until the city receives the deposit.
B.
Unpaid charges for services rendered before the filing of a bankruptcy petition are
pre-petition charges. Charges for services rendered after the customer files a bankruptcy
petition are post-petition charges if authorized by the bankruptcy trustee, if any,
or the debtor. Pre-petition charges are the responsibility of the bankruptcy debtor;
post-petition charges are the responsibility of the party authorizing them.
C.
When authorized, the city will file a claim with the bankruptcy court for all services
rendered to the bankruptcy debtor. The city shall credit any amount received for pre-petition
services to the city's bad debt expense.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.120; Code 2009, § 14.20.120; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
A.
Customers billed under a residential rate schedule may convert to a budget billing
plan, subject to this chapter, by submitting a written request to convert to a budget-billing
plan. If the customer receives a combined bill which includes charges for more than
one service described in this chapter the budget billing plan must be applied to the
entire bill and may not be applied to fewer than all of the services listed on the
combined bill. Upon request of a customer, the city will implement a budget-billing
plan during the billing cycle beginning more than 14 days after receipt by the city
of a written request for a budget-billing plan.
B.
Any customer electing a budget billing plan shall pay monthly an amount not less than
1/12
