of the total of the customer's 12 most recent monthly bills, adjusted to reflect rate
changes which became effective during the 12-month period, plus five percent. The
city will estimate any unavailable service usage data required. A customer may not
elect a budget-billing plan unless the city has billed the customer for six months
of service at one address and the customer has no past due balance for the previous
six months.
C.
Once implemented, a budget-billing plan will remain in effect for 12 months. In the
12th month, the city will compare the actual consumption of service against the amount
billed over the previous 12 months. If the customer has overpaid and has a credit,
the city will reduce the new budget-billing amount to reflect the credit over the
next year. If the customer has underpaid, the city will increase the budget-billing
amount over the next year to make up for the underpayment.
D.
The budget-billing plan will automatically continue from year to year unless terminated
by either party.
E.
To be eligible for a budget-billing plan, a customer shall be required to execute
an agreement for a budget billing plan at least 15 days prior to the beginning of
the budget-billing period. If a customer on a budget-billing plan fails to pay the
amount billed in any month, the city will commence collection procedures against the
unpaid amount. The city will remove the customer from the budget-billing plan and
may discontinue service pursuant to this chapter.
F.
The city may adjust the monthly budget-billing amount to reflect change in the city's
rates or the customer's consumption.
G.
If the city terminates service to a customer on a budget-billing plan, the city will
remove the customer from the plan and the entire outstanding amount of the account
shall be due and payable immediately.
H.
The customer may elect to terminate the budget-billing plan at any time by notifying
the city in writing and by paying the outstanding amount of the account. If the customer
has a credit balance, the city will, at the customer's option, issue a check for the
balance after adjustments or apply the balance to future billings.
I.
The customer's bill under the plan will indicate actual consumption for the month
billed and the amount that would be charged under the current rates for the actual
consumption. However, the customer's obligation will be to pay the amount denominated
budget billing amount.
J.
The city may require a deposit from a customer participating in the budget-billing
plan, pursuant to this chapter.
K.
The budget-billing plan will be adjusted each year by taking into consideration all
of the following:
1.
Rate changes during the previous 12 months;
2.
Credit or debit balances incurred during the previous 12 months;
3.
A change in consumption during the previous 12 months; and
4.
Contract agreements.
The city will notify a customer of a new budget-billing amount.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.130; Code 2009, § 14.20.130; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
A customer wishing to discontinue service should give at least two business days'
notice to the city unless otherwise specified in the applicable rate schedule or contract,
in order to allow sufficient time for final meter reading and disconnection of service.
When the city does not receive such notice, the customer will be liable for service
for two business days following receipt of notice to discontinue service. Notice of
discontinuance of service will not relieve the customer of responsibility for payment
of any minimum or guaranteed payment under contract or rate schedule.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.140; Code 2009, § 14.20.140; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
A.
The city will only discontinue utility service in accordance with this chapter.
B.
The city will mail or otherwise deliver written notice of any proposed discontinuance
of service by first class mail or as otherwise provided in this chapter at least seven
days in advance of the proposed date for discontinuance of service. The notice will
advise the customer which provisions of this Code have been violated, which service
will be discontinued, and, if applicable, the amount past due and the date by which
the past due amount must be paid in order to avoid discontinuance of service. For
the purposes of this chapter, amount past due means any sum overdue for, or charge
related to, services provided by the city, deposits or other charges. With regard
to a combined bill, as described in the regulation governing billing, the city may
issue one notice of discontinuance prior to discontinuance of all services provided
by the city. The city may install remote connect meters at city's discretion.
C.
The reminder notice and the collection notice will be in both English and Spanish.
D.
A notice of discontinuance will advise a customer:
1.
Of the opportunity to request a hearing prior to discontinuance of service, during
which the customer will have the opportunity to present to a hearing officer any facts
or disagreement with respect to the amount or date any payment is due or violation
of any other regulation;
2.
That a customer may participate in a hearing by telephone;
3.
That a residential customer may avoid discontinuance of service by paying on or before
the expiration date of the notice, the current month's bill in full, and entering
into an installment payment plan agreeable to the city to pay the remaining past due
balance in equal monthly installments, consistent with this chapter;
4.
That, in the event a residential customer is unable to pay for services as billed
by the city, and a medical certification is received by the city's utility billing
division indicating that discontinuance of service would be dangerous to the health
or safety of a residential customer or a permanent resident of the residential customer's
household, the city will not discontinue service for 30 days from the date of the
medical certification. Residential customers may request a 30-day extension upon delivery
of a second medical certification to the city's utility billing division;
5.
Of the names and telephone numbers of agencies that may assist with the payment of
utility bills. Providing a list of such agencies is for informational purposes only
and the city makes no representations regarding such listings or the accuracy of any
information listed.
E.
In the event a residential customer has signed a third party notification form identifying
a third party to whom a notice of discontinuance is to be sent, a notice of discontinuance
will also be mailed by first class mail or delivered at least seven days in advance
of the proposed discontinuance of service date to the designated third party. The
city will make available a third party notification form to each new residential customer.
The city shall annually inform residential customers about third party notification
forms and how to obtain copies of such forms. The customer may mail or deliver to
the city third party notification forms, which shall be signed by both the customer,
or the customer's legal representative, and the third party to be notified in the
event the city issues a notice of discontinuance of service.
F.
The city will not issue a discontinuance of service notice or offer the opportunity
for a hearing before discontinuance of service, as set forth in this chapter, when:
1.
In the opinion of the city, the immediate discontinuance of service is imperative
for safety reasons. Safety reasons include, but are not limited to, the existence
of a hazardous condition on the customer's premises or in the immediate vicinity and
use by the customer in a manner dangerous to the customer or others. The customer
is entitled to a hearing after the discontinuance of service;
2.
Any governmental authority orders discontinuance due to alleged violations by the
customer of the ordinances, statutes or regulations applicable to the service;
3.
A party other than the city restores service that the city discontinued.
G.
Before discontinuing service to the common areas of a multiple-dwelling unit or group
of dwelling units, the city will:
1.
Deliver or mail the notice of discontinuance of service to the last known address
of the owner or manager; and
2.
Make a reasonable effort to mail or deliver written notice to tenants residing in
the multidwelling unit or group of dwelling units.
H.
The city will not discontinue service:
1.
For nonpayment of any amount past due unless the amount has appeared on a regular
monthly bill with a due date at least 20 days after the billing date;
2.
For nonpayment of any amount not past due;
3.
For nonpayment of any amount past due, on which the customer has made payment arrangements;
4.
Except as provided in subsections F and J.2 of this section, for any reason unless
the city has made a reasonable effort to provide actual notice of the proposed discontinuance
of service to any customer either in person, by mail or by telephone. Reasonable effort
means make contact by telephone, mail a reminder notice of discontinuance, or attempt
to make personal contact at the location of service at least 24 hours before discontinuing
service to inform the customer of the notice of discontinuance of service and the
conditions under which discontinuance of service may be avoided. The city may affix
to a door of the service address or mail notice of the proposed discontinuance of
service;
5.
If a customer pays the current bill in full and enters into an installment plan agreeing
to pay all past due amounts, as provided in this chapter, on or before the expiration
date of the notice of discontinuance of service;
6.
For 30 days if the residential customer presents a medical certification in compliance
with the requirements of this chapter;
7.
Between November 1 and April 30, inclusive, without following the provisions of subsection
L of this section.
8.
If a customer has been approved for a city utility rebate program as provided in chapter 4.08 , but any rebate amount has not yet been applied to the customer's account.
I.
The city will restore service within 24 hours of a customer satisfying any of the
provisions of this section unless a physical impediment prevents restoration of service.
1.
Pays in full the amount shown on the notice of discontinuance of service plus any
deposit and connection and reconnection fees required by the city;
2.
Pays reconnection and collection fees required by the city, enters into an installment
payment plan described in this chapter with the city and makes the first installment
payment required under the installment plan. This provision shall not apply if the
cause for discontinuance was the customer's failure to make a payment under an installment
payment plan;
3.
Presents a medical certification in compliance with the provisions of this chapter;
4.
Pays all reconnection and collection fees plus any deposit required by the city, is
notified by the city that the cause for discontinuance of service, if other than nonpayment,
has been remedied, pays all additional costs incurred by the city, including, but
not limited to, the actual cost of line crew personnel being sent to the service address
to connect or disconnect service at the utility pole or water at the curb stop or
meter pit when connection or disconnection at the meter is impractical or impossible.
J.
Installment payment plans.
1.
Unless the city has disconnected service due to a customer breaching a current installment
payment plan, the city shall authorize an installment payment plan if a residential
customer meets one of the following conditions on or before the expiration date of
a notice of discontinuance of service:
a.
Pays the current bill in full and agrees to enter into an installment payment plan,
as provided in this chapter, for any amount past due;
b.
Pays at least one-third of all amounts due and agrees to unless a current installment
payment plan has been breached following disconnection of service, as provided in
this chapter, for the entire remaining amount due;
c.
Pays the collection fees, reconnection charges and makes a deposit, all as required
by the city, agrees to enter into an installment payment plan, and makes the first
installment payment under the plan.
2.
If a customer has breached a current installment payment plan, the city may disconnect
service without further notice. A customer must pay the required deposit, any collection
or reconnection fees and the arrears on the account before the city will restore service.
3.
Installment payment plans shall include:
a.
The unpaid remainder of the past due amount shown on the notice;
b.
Any collection or reconnection fees as required by the city.
4.
The city will explain the terms of an installment payment plan and offer an installment
payment plan to any customer who contacts the city in response to a notice of discontinuance
of service. The city may require the customer to sign an installment payment plan
agreement as a condition of entering into an installment payment plan.
5.
An installment payment plan shall consist of equal monthly installments over a period
agreed upon by the customer and the city not to exceed six months.
a.
The first payment shall be due, together with the new bill, on the due date of the
new bill.
b.
Each succeeding installment payment shall be due, together with each new bill, on
the due date of the new bill.
6.
If a customer fails to make an installment payment when due, the city may proceed
to discontinue service as outlined in this chapter. If service is discontinued for
failure to make an installment payment, the city may decline to restore service until
all amounts past due have been paid, together with such collection fees, reconnection
charges, interest, and any deposit, as required by the city.
7.
Nothing in this chapter shall prevent the city from offering any installment payment
plan terms to avoid discontinuance of service or to restore service, if the terms
are at least as favorable to the customer as the terms and conditions set forth in
this chapter.
8.
Only residential customers have a right under this chapter to an installment payment
plan. The city may offer installment payment plans to nonresidential customers on
a case-by-case basis. The terms and conditions of an installment payment plan for
a nonresidential customer shall be consistent with the provisions for installment
payment plans for residential customers under this chapter.
K.
The city will not discontinue service, or if the city has discontinued service, the
city will restore service during any period when discontinuance of service is demonstrated
by a residential customer to be dangerous to the health or safety of the residential
customer or a permanent resident of the customer's household as provided in this chapter.
1.
A customer demonstrates a danger to the health or safety of a residential customer
or a permanent resident of the customer's household when a physician licensed to practice
medicine in the state, or a health practitioner licensed to practice in the state
and acting under a licensed physician's authority, certifies in writing that such
a danger exists and the written certification is received by the city utility billing
division.
2.
Upon receipt of a medical certification, the city will refrain from discontinuing
service, and restore discontinued service, for 30 days from the date of the medical
certification. The city will refrain from discontinuing service for an additional
30 days upon receipt of a second separate medical certification prior to the expiration
of the initial 30-day period.
3.
A residential customer may invoke the provisions of subsection H of this section only
once during any 12 consecutive months. In addition, a customer must arrange for payment
of any past due amount at the time the medical certification is presented.
L.
The following procedures apply to discontinuation of service:
1.
On the city's first attempt to collect a delinquent bill, the city will mail the customer
a reminder notice to the service address or the mailing address in the city's billing
system, indicating that service is at risk of being discontinued;
2.
On the city's second attempt to collect a delinquent bill, the city will notify the
customer by mail indicating that service is scheduled to be discontinued;
3.
Not less than seven calendar days after the second attempt to collect or make satisfactory
payment arrangements with the customer, a city representative or designee may disconnect
service if the customer has failed to make satisfactory arrangements with the city
for payment. The city representative or designee will leave at the service address,
a service disconnect card detailing the reason for disconnection of service, the web
address of the customer payment portal and the contact telephone number for utility
billing.
The disconnect card will not be required if the city has, within the previous 24 hours,
contacted the customer by telephone, e-mail or text, giving the same information as
is contained on the disconnect card.
Electric service will not be discontinued if the U.S. Weather Bureau predicts the
high temperature will be below 20 degrees Fahrenheit. If the city disconnects the
electric service, the city will not reconnect the electric service based upon the
temperature dropping to 20 degrees Fahrenheit or below. Water service may be disconnected
regardless of the temperature.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.150; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2; Ord. No. O-2014-67, § 3, 11-10-2014; Ord. No. O-2024-89 , § 2, 12-17-2024; Ord. No. O-2026-35 , § 1, 6-9-2026)
A.
Unless specific provisions of a customer's written service contract provide otherwise,
a customer that disagrees with a decision of the city concerning billing, payment
arrangements, termination of service, or violations of this Code may request a hearing
before a hearing officer during which the customer will have the opportunity to challenge
the decision of the city and to present any relevant facts regarding matters in dispute.
B.
If the customer requests a hearing within 30 days of the billing date on which the
disputed charges first appeared the city will not, except as authorized by section
14.20.150.F, disconnect service for nonpayment of any amount under dispute until the
hearing officer decides the dispute.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.160; Code 2009, § 14.20.160; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
In accordance with provisions of the Colorado Open Records Act, customer and account
information is not public and the city will not release customer or account information
except as provided below:
1.
The city will provide a customer a copy of their own records, upon request of the
customer.
2.
The city will provide customer and account information to persons whose names do not
appear on the account when the customer provides to the city written authorization
to release information from the customer's account, specifying to whom and when the
information may be released.
3.
The city will provide customer and account information upon request of law enforcement
when made in compliance with state or federal law and to city employees in connection
with their official duties.
4.
The city will provide customer and account information in response to lawfully issued
subpoenas or other legal process.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.170; Code 2009, § 14.20.170; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
The city electric utility rates and regulations, dated September 1999 and amended
June 2003, are amended by deleting section 4 , billing process, and section 5 , budget billing and discontinuance of service in their entireties.
(Code 1993, § 14.20.180; Code 2009, § 14.20.180; Ord. No. O-2003-77, § 2)
Pursuant to article IV of the Charter, there is adopted as the "City of Longmont Storm
Drainage Criteria Manual," dated July 1984, of the city, by reference thereto, the
said manual published by W.R.C. Engineering, Inc., 2765 South Colorado Blvd., Denver,
Colorado, all to have the same force and effect as if set forth herein in every particular.
The subject matter of the adopted manual includes comprehensive criteria relating
to all aspects of urban storm drainage for the purpose of protecting the public health,
safety and general welfare.
(Code 1993, § 14.24.010; Code 2009, § 14.24.010; Ord. No. O-87-1, § 1)
Copies of the city storm drainage criteria manual, dated July, 1984, adopted by section 14.24.010 , all certified to be true copies, have been and are now on file in the office of
the public works director, and may be inspected by any interested person between the
hours of 8:00 a.m. and 5:00 p.m., Monday through Friday, holidays excepted. The manual,
as finally adopted, shall be available for sale to the public through the office of
the public works director at a moderate price reflecting cost to the city and expenses
incurred in securing the same.
(Code 1993, § 14.24.020; Code 2009, § 14.24.0200; Ord. No. O-87-1, § 1)
Any violation of any provision of the city storm drainage criteria manual, dated July,
1984, adopted at section 14.24.010 , is punishable according to chapter 1.12 .
(Code 1993, § 14.24.030; Code 2009, § 14.24.030; Ord. No. O-87-1, § 1; Ord. No. O-94-61,
§ 9)
A.
Except as stated in subsection B of this section, each owner or utility billing customer
receiving service within the city shall be assessed and charged the following monthly
user charge for operations and maintenance of the storm drainage system and for storm
drainage capital improvements:

| 2022 | 2023 | 2024
Residential customer | $14.90 | $16.70 | $18.85
Nonresidential customers per each 20,000 square feet of surface area or portion thereof | $14.90 | $16.70 | $18.85

The rates shall become effective January 1st of each year listed, except the rate
listed for the year 2024 shall continue in effect until changed.
Accordingly, the owner or utility billing customer for every unit within any multi-family
residential building or multiple-unit nonresidential building shall be billed the
storm drainage fee for each unit in the building if the units are not individually
billed.
B.
The following utility accounts are exempt from the monthly user charge: sprinkler
control boxes; utility boxes; telecommunications towers; and the electricity account
for a nonresident garage accessory to a single-family home when the home and garage
accounts are in the name of the same customer.
(Code 1993, § 14.24.040; Code 2009, § 14.24.040; Ord. No. O-84-83, § 1; Ord. No. O-87-73,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-97-22, § 1; Ord. No. O-2005-57, § 1; Ord. No. O-2011-76, § 1, 10-25-2011;
Ord. No. O-2013-65, § 1, 11-12-2013; Ord. No. O-2021-56 , § 1, 10-26-2021; Ord. No. O-2021-73 , § 6, 12-14-2021)
A.
Single-family dwellings. A plant investment fee for the municipal storm drainage system shall be assessed
to each single-family dwelling certificate of occupancy issued for new residential
construction. This plant investment fee shall not apply to permits issued for additions
to or remodeling of existing single-family dwellings. All funds collected by the charge
of the plant investment fee shall be utilized only for capital improvements.

Class | 2023 | 2024 | 2025
Single- Family | $1,202.77 | $1,403.03 | $1,637.63

B.
Non-single-family dwellings. A plant investment fee for the municipal storm drainage system per square foot of
impervious surface acre shall be assessed at the time set in chapter 4.78 for the construction or expansion of a building other than a one-family dwelling.
All funds collected by the charge of the plant investment fee shall be utilized only
for capital improvements.

Class | 2023 | 2024 | 2025
Non-Single-Family | $0.2743 | $0.4472 | $0.7289

1.
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter shall have the meanings
ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates a different
meaning:
Common impervious surface means the total impervious surface within the development not located within an individual
building site, including city and private streets and other impervious surfaces used
by or benefiting more than one building site.
Impervious surface means areas on or in real property where the passage of stormwater into the earth's
surface has been reduced by the works of man.
Works of man means and includes buildings, roads, driveways, patio areas, roofs, sidewalks, parking
lots or storage areas, and other manmade structures or hard-surfaced areas.
2.
In calculating the amount of impervious surface in regard to each certificate of occupancy
or building permit, the total area of all building sites within the development shall
be divided into the total area of the building site for which a certificate of occupancy
or building permit is being issued. That result shall be multiplied by the total common
impervious surface and that result shall be added to the total impervious surface
of the building site to reach the total impervious surface area upon which the fee
shall be determined for that certificate of occupancy or building permit.
C.
The implementation of this fee system shall be the responsibility of the public works
and natural resources department.
1.
After the timeframe specified in the tables above, the director, or the director's
designee, shall adjust the plant investment fees annually in the same proportion as
changes in construction costs, according to the Engineering News Record 20-City Construction
Cost Index.
(Code 1993, § 14.24.050; Code 2009, § 14.24.050; Ord. No. O-84-83, § 1; Ord. No. O-97-22,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2001-77, § 1; Ord. No. O-2005-57, § 2; Ord. No. O-2012-54, § 2, 8-28-2012;
Ord. No. O-2019-18 , § 11, 3-19-2019; Ord. No. Ord. No. O-2023-19 , § 2, 5-23-2023)
Editor's note— Ord. No. O-2011-58, adopted Aug. 23, 2011, repealed and reenacted Ch. 14.26 in its
entirety to read as herein set out. Former Ch. 14.26 pertained to the same subject
matter, consisted of §§ 14.26.010—14.26.290, and derived from Ord. No. O-2009-18,
adopted Apr. 28, 2009.
The purpose of this chapter is to protect the environment, the water quality of our
watercourses, waterbodies and wetlands, and the health, safety and welfare of the
public by preventing the discharge of pollutants to the city municipal separate storm
sewer system (MS4). The regulations in this chapter enable the city to comply with
the requirements of the Federal Clean Water Act, the State of Colorado Water Quality
Act and federal and state law. The objectives of this chapter are to:
A.
Prevent pollutants from being discharged to the MS4;
B.
Prohibit illegal connections and discharges to the MS4;
C.
Reduce pollutants in stormwater discharges from construction activity;
D.
Reduce pollutants in runoff from developed and redeveloped areas through the implementation
of permanent stormwater control measures;
E.
Provide for inspection and monitoring procedures necessary to ensure compliance with
this chapter; and
F.
Meet the requirements of the MS4 general permit issued by the state department of
public health and environment.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.010), 1-14-2020)
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter shall have the meanings
ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates a different
meaning:
Applicable development site means a development requiring a stormwater permit, as delineated in section 14.26.120 .
CDPHE means the state department of public health and environment.
CDPS means the state discharge permit system administered by the state department of public
health and environment.
Clean Water Act means the Federal Water Pollution Control Act (33 USC 1251 et seq.) and any subsequent
amendments thereto.
Common plan of development means a contiguous area where multiple separate and distinct construction activities
may be taking place at different times on different schedules, but remain related.
In this context, the term "contiguous" means construction activities located within
one-fourth mile of each other.
Construction activity means ground surface disturbing and associated activities (land disturbance), which
include, but are not limited to, clearing, grading, excavation, demolition, installation
of new or improved haul roads and access roads, staging areas, stockpiling of fill
materials, and borrow areas. The term "construction activity" does not include routine
maintenance to maintain the original line and grade, hydraulic capacity, or original
purpose of the facility. Activities to conduct repairs that are not part of routine
maintenance or for replacement are construction activities and are not routine maintenance.
Repaving activities where underlying and/or surrounding soil is exposed as part of
the repaving operation are considered construction activities. Construction activity
is from initial ground-breaking to final stabilization regardless of ownership of
the construction activities.
Control measures means techniques, processes, activities, structures, or treatment devices that, when
used singly or in combination, prevent or reduce the pollutant content of a stormwater
discharge to the MS4 or waters of the state.
Development means any manmade change to improved or unimproved real property, including, but
not limited to, the alteration, construction, reconstruction, conversion, or enlargement
of any structure; any change in use of a property, building, or structure; and any
mining, dredging, filling, grading, paving, excavation, or drilling operation. The
term "development" shall include the act of subdivision, unless otherwise expressly
excluded.
Director means deputy city manager of the city or designee.
Director of finance shall mean the director of the city finance department or designee.
Discharge , when used as a verb, means to allow pollutants to, directly or indirectly, enter
stormwater, or to allow stormwater or non-stormwater that contains pollutants to directly,
or indirectly enter the MS4 or receiving waters. When used as a noun, the term "discharge"
means the pollutants, non-stormwater, or stormwater containing pollutants that directly
or indirectly enter the MS4 or receiving waters.
Discharger means any person engaged in activities or operations or owning facilities that will
or could result in pollutants entering stormwater, MS4 or receiving waters, or the
owners of real property on which such activities, operations or facilities are located.
Disturbed area means that portion of the land's surface disturbed by any work or activity by any
means, including, but not limited to, grading; excavating; stockpiling soil, fill
or other materials; clearing; vegetation removal; removal or deposit of any rock,
soil or other materials; or other activities which expose soil.
Facility means any building, private home, structure, process or activity from which there
is or may be a discharge of a pollutant.
Final stabilization means when all ground surface disturbing activities at the site have been completed,
and uniform vegetative cover has been established with an individual plant density
of at least 70 percent of pre-disturbance levels, or equivalent permanent, physical
erosion reduction methods have been employed. For purposes of this Code, establishment
of a vegetative cover capable of providing erosion control equivalent to pre-existing
conditions at the site will be considered final stabilization.
Hazardous materials means any material, including any substance, waste, or combination thereof, which
because of its quantity, concentration or physical, chemical, or infectious characteristics
may cause, or significantly contribute to, a substantial present or potential hazard
to human health, safety, property or to the environment when improperly treated, stored,
transported, disposed of or otherwise managed.
Illegal connection means any permanent or temporary connection to the MS4 that has not been approved
by the city, regardless of whether said connection had been previously allowed.
Illegal discharge means any direct or indirect release of pollutants to the MS4.
Low impact development means systems and practices that use or mimic natural processes that result in the
infiltration, evapotranspiration or use of stormwater in order to protect water quality
and associated aquatic habitat. It is a comprehensive land use planning and engineering
design approach to managing stormwater runoff with the goal of mimicking the pre-development
hydrologic regime. It emphasizes conservation of natural features and use of engineered,
on-site, small-scale hydrologic controls that infiltrate, filter, store, evaporate,
and detain runoff close to its source.
Maximum extent practicable (MEP) means the standard for implementation of stormwater management programs to reduce
pollutants in stormwater, taking into account equitable considerations and competing
facts, including, but not limited to, the gravity of the problem, public health risk,
societal concern, environmental benefits, pollutant removal effectiveness, regulatory
compliance, public acceptance, ability to implement, cost and technical feasibility.
MEP refers to stormwater management programs taken as a whole. Section 402(p) of the
Clean Water Act requires that municipal permits "… shall require controls to reduce
the discharge of pollutants to the maximum extent practicable, including management
practices, control techniques and systems, design and engineering methods, and such
other provisions as the administrator of the Environmental Protection Agency or the
state determines appropriate for the control of such pollutants."
Mobile washing operation means a commercial activity involving power washing, steam cleaning, or any other
method of mobile cosmetic cleaning engaged in for commercial purposes or related to
a commercial activity, including, but not limited to, vehicles, fabric, exterior surfaces,
heating and cooling systems, and animals, including pets.
Municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4) means publicly owned facilities by which stormwater is collected and conveyed, including,
but not limited to, any roads with drainage systems, municipal streets, gutters, curbs,
catch basins, inlets, piped storm drains, pumping facilities, retention and detention
basins, and natural and human-made drainage ditches, channels, lakes, reservoirs and
other drainage structures.
MS4 general permit means the stormwater discharge permit, CDPS General Permit COR090000—Stormwater Discharges
Associated with Municipal Separate Storm Sewer Systems, issued to the city by the
state department of public health and environment.
National Pollutant Discharge Elimination System (NPDES) stormwater discharge permit
or NPDES permit means a permit issued pursuant to section 402 of the Clean Water Act, including permits
issued by the state in compliance with the Act.
Non-stormwater discharge means any discharge to the MS4 that is not composed entirely of stormwater or any
release of pollutants that potentially or actually discharges to the MS4.
Nonstructural stormwater control measures are preventative actions that involve pollutant management and pollutant source controls
that are not structural controls. Nonstructural controls include such measures as
minimization of impervious areas, minimizing directly connected impervious areas,
street sweeping, and conserving open space that will infiltrate stormwater runoff.
Operator means the individual who is responsible for day-to-day supervision and control of
activities occurring at or on a property.
Owner means any person who owns a facility, development, or land, or part of a facility,
development or land.
Permanent stormwater control measures means long-term techniques, processes, activities, structures, or treatment devices
that, when used singly or in combination, prevent or reduce the pollutant content
of a stormwater discharge.
Pollutant means any substance that is harmful to humans, animals, public health, the environment,
or that can degrade the quality of receiving waters, cause the receiving waters to
violate the stream standards established by the state, or affect beneficial uses of
water. The term "pollutant" includes, but is not limited to, sediment, dredged spoil,
rock, sand, silt, incinerator residue, ash; solid waste; sewage; wastes from industrial,
commercial, domestic, or agricultural sources; trash, litter, garbage or food waste;
landscaping materials, lawn clippings, leaves, branches or other landscaping and yard
debris; medical waste; wrecked or discarded equipment; radioactive materials; wastes
that contain bacteria, viruses and other pathogens that pose a threat to human health;
pet wastes; heat, surfactants, soaps, and cleaning products and wastes and residues
from washing operations, including those that are biodegradable; oil and grease, petroleum
hydrocarbons and antifreeze; metals; and toxic or hazardous wastes as defined by federal,
state, or local laws and regulations, including biocides and pesticides.
Pollution means the alteration of the physical, thermal, chemical or biological quality of,
or the contamination of, any water that renders the water harmful, detrimental or
injurious to humans, animal life, vegetation or property, or to public health, safety
or welfare, or impairs the use or public enjoyment of the water for any lawful or
reasonable purpose.
Potential illegal discharge means a condition creating a substantial probability of an illegal discharge.
Premises means any building, lot, parcel of land, or portion of land whether improved or unimproved,
and including adjacent sidewalks and parking strips.
Receiving water means any waters of the state that receives a stormwater discharge from the MS4,
including all watercourses, even if they are usually dry, and irrigation ditches that
receive municipal stormwater. The term "receiving water" also includes storm sewer
systems owned by other entities.
Responsible party means an owner or operator.
Spill means any intentional or unintentional release of solid or liquid material which
may cause a discharge of pollutants to the MS4 or to waters of the state.
Stormwater means any surface flow, runoff and drainage consisting entirely of water from any
form of natural precipitation, and resulting from such precipitation.
Structural stormwater control measure means any permanent or temporary stormwater control measure designed and constructed
to capture, attenuate, and remove pollutants from stormwater runoff.
Wastewater (sewage) means any water-carried or liquid wastes, other than uncontaminated stormwater, originating
from domestic or nondomestic sources, such as dwellings, commercial buildings, institutions
and industrial facilities.
Watercourse means a natural or artificial channel for the continuous or intermittent passage
or storage of water.
Waters of the state (state waters) means any and all surface and subsurface waters that are contained in or flow in
or through the state, but does not include waters in sewage systems, waters in treatment
works of disposal systems, waters in potable water distribution systems and all waters
withdrawn for use until use and treatment have been completed.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.020), 1-14-2020)
This chapter applies to all direct or indirect discharges to the MS4 or receiving
water and any activities that generate, or could generate such discharges, unless
explicitly exempted by this chapter.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.030), 1-14-2020)
Except as otherwise provided in this Code, the director shall administer, implement
and enforce the provisions of this chapter, and, notwithstanding any other provision
of this Code, any and all provisions regarding stormwater quality included in any
permit issued under any provision of this Code.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.040), 1-14-2020)
A.
Non-stormwater discharges to the city's MS4 are prohibited. Illegal non-stormwater
discharges include, but are not limited to, discharges that:
1.
Cause or contribute to violations of the city's MS4 general permit;
2.
Cause or contribute to any blockages of or damage to the MS4;
3.
Constitute a fire or explosion hazard;
4.
Contain any toxic or hazardous material, including, but not limited to, pathogens,
chemicals and automotive fluids and hazardous waste as defined in 40 CFR 261.3;
5.
Cause or contribute to a public nuisance as defined in chapter 9.04 ;
6.
Contain radioactive material;
7.
Result from any construction activities, including, but not limited to, earthwork,
paving, concrete placement, painting, saw-cutting and material storage;
8.
Consist of trucked or hauled wastes, including, but not limited to, septage, recreational
vehicle waste, and water from mobile washing operations;
9.
Contain or consist of any pollutant or any material that is, or has properties, constituents
or characteristics that could be, harmful to humans, animals, aquatic life or the
environment;
10.
Constitute a potential illegal discharge.
B.
The following discharges to the MS4 are exempt from the discharge prohibitions in
subsection A of this section, unless the discharge, either by itself or in combination
with other actual or potential discharges, unduly restricts the capacity of the MS4
to carry design storm flows, or causes a public nuisance or safety concern:
1.
Line flushing, tank draining, or other potable water sources when managed in accordance
with applicable stormwater control measures as required by CDPHE guidance, regulations,
or other requirements;
2.
Irrigation return flows, diverted stream flows, natural riparian habitat or wetland
flows, rising groundwater and natural springs;
3.
Uncontaminated groundwater infiltration to storm drains;
4.
Uncontaminated pumped groundwater from foundation or footing drains or sump pumps,
provided such water does not flow onto sidewalks, cause nuisance or public safety
conditions such as algae growth, ice formation, or sediment buildup in gutters or
on sidewalks, streets, or other surfaces, or unduly restrict the volumetric or treatment
capacity of any permanent stormwater control measure or stormwater detention facility;
5.
Air conditioning condensation;
6.
Water from individual residential car washing;
7.
Discharges from swimming pools, spas and hot tubs if the discharges contain no detectable
residual chlorine or other chemicals that could endanger aquatic life or cause pollution
of surface waters;
8.
Discharges associated with emergency firefighting activities;
9.
Discharges authorized by a CDPS or NPDES permit;
10.
Agricultural stormwater runoff;
11.
Dye testing, provided that the testing and materials to be used have been approved
by the applicable federal, state or local regulatory agencies and that the person
doing the testing provide verbal notification to said agencies a minimum of 24 hours
prior to the time of the test, and the testing is conducted in a manner consistent
with manufacturer recommendations;
12.
Runoff of roadway anti-icing and deicing agents and wash water from cleaning streets
and surfaces, provided that best management practices to reduce or prevent pollution
are followed;
13.
Discharges from landscape irrigation or lawn watering, provided such discharges comply
with chapters 9.04 and 14.04 and do not cause public nuisance or public safety condition such as algae growth,
ice formation, vegetation deposition, or sediment deposition in gutters or on sidewalks,
streets, or other surfaces, or unduly restrict the volumetric or treatment capacity
of any permanent stormwater control measure or stormwater detention facility;
14.
Discharges exempted from the MS4 general permit because they are in accordance with
CDPHE's low risk policy guidance documents.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.050), 1-14-2020)
A.
It is unlawful to cause materials to be deposited or remain in such a manner or location
as to constitute an illegal discharge of pollutants into the MS4 or waters of the
state. Notwithstanding other requirements of law, any person responsible for any known
or suspected release of materials which are resulting or may result in illegal discharges
or pollutants discharging into stormwater, the MS4 or waters of the state, shall take
all necessary steps to ensure the discovery, containment, and cleanup of such release.
B.
In the event of a release of hazardous materials, the person shall immediately notify
emergency response agencies of the occurrence of such discharge via emergency dispatch
services. In the event of a discharge of nonhazardous materials, said person shall
notify the director in person or by phone as soon as practicable, but no later than
24 hours after becoming aware of the discharge. Notifications shall include, at a
minimum, the location, time, contents, and volumes of the illegal discharge, and a
contact name and telephone number. Notification does not relieve the user of any enforcement
actions.
C.
The construction, use, maintenance, or continued existence of illegal connections
to the MS4 is prohibited. This prohibition expressly includes, without limitation,
illegal connections made in the past, regardless of whether the connection was permissible
under law or practices applicable or prevailing at the time of connection.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.060), 1-14-2020)
A.
In the event of an immediate danger to public health or safety, the city is authorized
to enter upon the private property, without giving prior notice, to take any and all
measures necessary to abate the violation and/or restore the property.
B.
The director may enter and inspect any building, structure, real property, or premises
at reasonable times to ensure compliance with this chapter.
C.
Inspections may include, but not be limited to, monitoring and documentation of any
potential or actual discharges to the MS4, examination of operating practices and
records, and to ensure compliance with any permits issued under this chapter or other
permits issued by the city that include stormwater control requirements.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.090), 1-14-2020)
A.
Stormwater control measures must be used to prevent pollutants generated by any activity
from entering the MS4 or being transported to the MS4 via streets, sidewalks or other
surfaces, regardless of whether or not a permit is required or has been issued. Activities
that require the use of stormwater control measures include, but are not limited to,
construction, land development, operations and maintenance of facilities, material
storage, utility installation or maintenance, landscaping, and work in the right-of-way.
All costs associated with the installation, maintenance and removal of stormwater
control measures shall be the responsibility of the person responsible for or performing
the activity.
1.
All stormwater control measures must be appropriate for the pollution source, and
installed and maintained in a manner to ensure that they function properly for the
duration of their use.
2.
The city may require stormwater control measures to be installed, modified, maintained,
relocated or removed in order to prevent pollutants from discharging to the stormwater
control measures. In this case, the owner or operator shall make the necessary modifications
to the stormwater control measures immediately.
3.
Stormwater control measures that are installed for control of pollutants from temporary
activities such as construction, must be removed once the site or location is in a
condition where the stormwater control measures are no longer needed to prevent pollutants
from entering the MS4.
B.
All persons who own or operate a commercial or industrial establishment or property
and use or store materials in such a manner that may cause a potential discharge of
pollutants to the MS4 or performs any activities that result in a disturbed area shall
provide, at their own expense, protection against accidental discharge of prohibited
materials or other wastes into the MS4 or watercourses through the use of stormwater
control measures.
C.
Any person responsible for activities or premises that are the source of a potential
or illegal discharge may be required to implement, at said person's expense, additional
structural and nonstructural stormwater control measures to prevent the further discharge
of pollutants to the MS4.
D.
The owner or operator of any paved parking lot, paved areas of a gas station, paved
private street, sidewalk or driveway or any related areas or structures that allow
stormwater to be discharged to the MS4 shall clean those areas or structures as frequently
and thoroughly as necessary to prevent the discharge of pollutants to the MS4. The
methods used for cleaning shall not result in a discharge of pollutants to the MS4.
E.
Materials, including, but not limited to, stockpiles, chemicals, or equipment used
in construction, maintenance or landscaping activities shall be stored and used in
a manner to prevent the release of pollutants to the MS4.
(Code 2009, § 14.26.100; Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.100), 1-14-2020)
A.
It shall be unlawful for any person to conduct any construction activity resulting
in the following total disturbed area without first obtaining a stormwater construction
activity permit for such construction activity:
1.
One acre or more;
2.
Less than one acre if such activities are part of a larger common plan of development,
or sale, that would disturb one or more acres, even though multiple, separate, and
distinct land development activities may take place at different times on different
schedules;
3.
A disturbed area of any size if the city determines that the construction activity
could result in a discharge of pollutants that could have a detrimental effect on
an adjacent watercourse or wetlands, or could result in pollutants being discharged
to the MS4 that would adversely affect public health or the environment.
B.
A stormwater construction activity permit will be issued to the owner or operator
at the conclusion of the development review process described in this Code and after
the submission of a complete permit application pursuant to section 14.26.140 , and will include terms and conditions for implementation of stormwater control measures,
both structural and nonstructural, for all active construction sites meeting the conditions
of subsection A of this section. It is unlawful to fail to maintain the stormwater
control measures in accordance with the requirements of this chapter or a permit issued
hereunder.
C.
A permanent stormwater control permit shall be required for any disturbed areas described
in section 14.26.120.A.1, 2 and 3, except those meeting the criteria for excluded
sites under the city's MS4 general permit, and those meeting the requirements set
forth in section 14.26.120.C.4.
1.
A permanent stormwater control permit shall be issued to the owner of the permanent
stormwater control measure upon final inspection by the city to verify compliance
with all approved design documentation and standards, and after submission of a complete
permit application pursuant to section 14.26.140 . The permit will include terms and conditions for operation and maintenance of the
post-construction permanent stormwater control measures.
2.
Permanent stormwater control measures, both structural and nonstructural, shall be
owned, operated and maintained in perpetuity by the property owner in a manner that
allows them to function properly at their design capacity or documented intent, unless
or until the city relieves the property owner of that responsibility in writing. It
is unlawful for a property owner to fail to operate or maintain the permanent stormwater
control measures in accordance with the requirements of this chapter and the permit
issued hereunder.
3.
The owner of the permanent stormwater control measure shall provide for maintenance
access to all permitted permanent stormwater control facilities. Such access shall
be memorialized on the subdivision plat, annexation plat, development agreement or
other instrument in a form acceptable to the city and shall be recorded in the office
of the county clerk and recorder.
4.
A permanent stormwater control permit is not required for those areas that are treated
in an existing permanent stormwater control facility if the responsible party submits
documentation that, in the city's sole discretion, the requirements of section 14.26.150 have been met.
D.
The city may include stormwater control requirements related to construction activities
in any permits issued by the city pursuant to this Code in order to apply the requirements
of this chapter. Beyond the permits required by this section, these permits include,
but are not limited to, building permits required by title and permits for work in
the public right of way or use of public places required by chapter 13.04 or 13.37 .
E.
A permit may be required for other temporary or continuous discharges to the MS4 if
the city determines that a permit is needed to prevent pollutants from being discharged
to the MS4, or to further the purposes of this Code. The city may also require a permit
for discharges to the MS4 that are allowed by state or federal regulations.
F.
The city shall not issue a permit if the applicant fails to provide the application,
design or other information required in this chapter; fails to pay the fees required
by this chapter; or if the discharge, either by itself or in combination with other
discharges, unduly restricts the capacity of the MS4 to carry design storm flows;
or if requested by the city, fails to provide proof of issuance of any state or federal
permit.
G.
The city may modify permits to comply with changes in state, federal, or local requirements
or regulations, or to further the purposes of this chapter. If applicable, the permit
may include a reasonable time schedule for complying with the modified requirements.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.120), 1-14-2020)
Permits shall contain terms and conditions necessary to carry out the requirements
of this chapter, including, but not limited to, the following:
1.
Identification of responsible party (owner or operator);
2.
Effective date and duration of permit;
3.
Requirements for modification, transfer, reassignment, renewal, and termination of
the permit;
4.
Requirements for installing, inspecting, operating, maintaining, and replacing stormwater
control measures;
5.
Requirements for managing pollution sources to prevent transport of pollutants by
stormwater runoff;
6.
Monitoring requirements;
7.
Reporting requirements;
8.
Permit duration:
a.
The term of a stormwater construction activity permit is two years, at which point
it will terminate unless renewed.
b.
Every five years after the initial issuance of the permanent stormwater control permit,
the responsible party must submit a new application form.
9.
Other terms and conditions as determined by the city to ensure that all pollution
sources are controlled and stormwater control measures are being operated and maintained
in a manner that allows them to meet their design conditions.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.130), 1-14-2020)
The city will not issue any permit required under this chapter until a complete application,
site plan and other required information has been submitted, the city has completed
its review of all application materials and all fees required by this chapter have
been paid. A stormwater construction activity permit is required before any construction
activities can start.
A.
A completed permit application with all required information shall be submitted on
a form prescribed by the city. The permit application must be prepared and signed
by the owner or operator who is responsible for compliance with the permit throughout
the term of the permit.
B.
In support of the application for a stormwater construction activity permit, the applicant
shall submit a site plan with all information requested and required by the city to
allow a complete review of site conditions, drainage on the site and stormwater control
measures to prevent pollutants generated from, stored on or used on the site from
being discharged to the MS4 or state waters. Such information includes, but is not
limited to, plans and specifications, including erosion control plans showing grading
and contours of the site, locations of all private and public stormwater collection
and conveyance systems, locations and identification of all potential pollutant sources
on the site, descriptions and locations of the erosion and sediment control and pollution
prevention stormwater control measures, structural and nonstructural, that will be
used to keep pollutants from entering the MS4, and the results of any tests on the
soil or groundwater on the site. All stormwater control measures shall meet the technical
standards and specifications requirements of section 14.26.150 .
C.
In support of the application for a permanent stormwater control permit, the applicant
shall submit written certification that the permanent stormwater control measures
were inspected and confirmed to be constructed as shown on the construction plans
and specifications and the final grading and drainage plan approved by the city. The
certification shall be in a form prescribed by the city and include all information
and documentation required by the city. The certification shall be signed by a professional
engineer licensed by the state. The certification and accompanying documentation shall
clearly show that the stormwater control measures meet the design and operating conditions
associated with the site.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.140), 1-14-2020)
A.
In determining what stormwater control measures shall be required and what technical
standards shall apply to meet the requirements of this chapter, the director shall
consider what measures are practicable and effective to prevent or minimize stormwater
pollution, using the following standards, criteria, and requirements as a guide, in
order of precedence:
1.
The Urban Drainage and Flood Control District "Urban Storm Drainage Criteria Manual-Volume
3-BMP" or its successor;
2.
Stormwater design standards, as described in subsection B of this section;
3.
The current city design standards and construction specifications;
4.
The state department of transportation stormwater quality requirements, and supporting
documents;
B.
In support of the design for a permanent stormwater control permit, one of the following
stormwater treatment design standards must be met:
1.
Water quality capture volume (WQCV) standard. The control measure is designed to provide
treatment or infiltration of the WQCV and both the following conditions are satisfied:
a.
Runoff from 100 percent of the applicable development site is captured, except the
permittee may exclude up to 20 percent, not to exceed one acre, of the applicable
development site area when it is not practicable to capture runoff from portions of
the site that will not drain towards control measures. In addition, the permittee
must also determine that the implementation of a separate control measure for that
portion of the site is not practicable (e.g., driveway access that drains directly
to street).
b.
Evaluation of the minimum drain time shall be based on the pollutant removal mechanism
and functionality of the control measure implemented. Consideration of drain time
shall include maintaining vegetation necessary for operation of the control measure
(e.g., wetland vegetation).
2.
Runoff reduction standard. The control measure is designed to infiltrate into the
ground where site geology permits, evaporate, or evapotranspire a quantity of water
equal to 60 percent of what the calculated WQCV would be if all impervious area for
the applicable development site discharged without infiltration. This base design
standard can be met through practices such as green infrastructure. The term "green
infrastructure" generally refers to control measures that use vegetation, soils, and
natural processes or mimic natural processes to manage stormwater. Green infrastructure
can be used in place of, or in addition to, low impact development principles.
3.
Alternate treatment methodologies approved by the city to be equivalent to one of
the other listed standards above.
C.
Low impact development. An evaluation of low impact development techniques to control
stormwater runoff from the site is required for all applicable development sites that
install permanent stormwater control measures. Implementation of low impact development
techniques is required to the maximum extent practicable.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.150), 1-14-2020)
A.
It is unlawful and a strict liability offense for any person to violate any provisions
of this chapter or to permit or cause a violation of this chapter, or of any permit
issued under this chapter, or of any permit issued pursuant to this Code that contains
stormwater pollution control provisions; an administrative order or stop-work order;
or to remove, tamper, disrupt, obstruct, or otherwise interfere with devices, equipment,
materials, or procedures installed or used by the city to detect, verify, monitor
or abate actual or potential discharges of pollutants to the MS4. Each day or part
of a day any violation occurs or continues is a separate offense. Each offense is
punishable by a fine of no more than $999.00, or imprisonment up to 180 days, or by
both such fine and imprisonment.
B.
Nothing in this section prohibits the director from using any or all of the enforcement
alternatives outlined in this chapter. The director may use any available enforcement
alternatives the director believes will be effective. No enforcement alternative is
a condition precedent to any other alternative. Violation of this chapter or the provisions
of any permit issued under it is a public nuisance. Any court of competent jurisdiction
shall enjoin violations.
C.
Nothing in this section shall prohibit the director from seeking appropriate judicial
relief where the actions or conduct of a user constitute an immediate threat to the
public health, safety or welfare. Nor shall anything in this section prohibit the
director from immediately seeking criminal sanctions against a suspected violator.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.160), 1-14-2020)
The director may suspend, modify, or revoke any permit issued under this chapter or
permits that are issued under any provision of this Code that include stormwater requirements,
and refuse to allow construction activities or a discharge to the MS4 to continue
if a permittee or discharger:
1.
Violates any condition stated in the permit, or any provision of this chapter, or
any applicable state or federal regulation;
2.
Fails to report a discharge, accidental or otherwise, of a toxic or prohibited substance;
3.
Fails to accurately report constituents and characteristics of any discharge, or changes
in site conditions;
4.
Fails to install appropriate stormwater control measures to control potential pollution
sources;
5.
Fails to maintain installed stormwater control measures;
6.
Fails to comply with an administrative order or stop-work order;
7.
Removes, tampers, disrupts, obstructs, or otherwise interferes with devices, equipment,
materials, or procedures installed or used by the city;
8.
Refuses reasonable access to the site or premises for the purpose of inspection or
monitoring;
9.
Fails to pay fees, charges or penalties assessed by the city; or
10.
Makes any false statement, representation or certification, knowing it to be false
with reckless indifference or disregard to the truth of the matter, in any application,
record, plan, data or document filed or required to be maintained pursuant to this
chapter
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.170), 1-14-2020)
In the event of a violation of this chapter which involves an actual or potential
discharge of pollutant to the MS4 or an immediate danger to public health, public
safety, or the environment, the director is authorized, with or without giving prior
notice, to take any and all measures necessary to abate the violation and/or restore
the property. Violators are liable for the costs incurred for abatement.
A.
If the city abates a violation, within 30 days after abatement of the violation, the
responsible party will be notified of the cost of abatement by personal delivery or
by regular mail to the last known address of the responsible party. The costs of abatement
shall include all direct and indirect costs of manpower, equipment, expenses and administrative
costs incurred by the city. The notice shall be effective upon the date of mailing
or personal delivery.
B.
The property owner may file a written appeal to the director, objecting to the cost
of the abatement within ten days of the effective date of the notice.
C.
If no appeal is filed, then the charges shall become due and payable on the date set
forth in the notice, which date shall be after the expiration of the time in which
to file an appeal.
D.
In the event an appeal is filed, a hearing on such appeal shall be held before a hearing
officer selected by the director. The hearing officer shall not be an employee of
the department of public works and natural resources. The hearing shall be conducted
in compliance with chapter 2.98 . The hearing officer's decision shall be limited to upholding or modifying the amount
of the assessment. If any charges are upheld upon completion of such hearing, then
such charges shall become due and payable within ten days after the decision of the
hearing officer.
E.
If the amount due is not paid within ten days of the decision of the hearing officer,
if appealed, or the expiration of the time in which to file an appeal under this section
if not appealed, the charges shall become a special assessment against the property
and shall constitute a lien on the property for the amount of the assessment. The
assessment shall be certified by the director of finance to the office of the county
treasurer for collection in the same manner as the collection of general property
taxes.
F.
Upon receipt of the assessment roll certified by the director of finance, the county
treasurer shall collect the amounts so assessed in the same manner as the collection
of general property taxes. The director of finance may accept payment of the amount
of assessment for costs and charges before certification to the county treasurer.
After certification, only the county treasurer may accept payment.
G.
The city may also assess a civil penalty against the responsible party for the amount
due, as set forth in the notice, or, if appealed, as set forth in the hearing officer's
decision. The director shall send notice of the civil penalty to the responsible party
via regular mail to the last known address of the responsible party or via personal
service. The notice shall clearly state the amount of the civil penalty along with
the due date and possible consequences of nonpayment in accordance with this chapter.
Collection of the civil penalty shall be in accordance with section 14.26.210.D and
E.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.180), 1-14-2020)
Whenever the director determines that any activity is occurring which is not in compliance
with the requirements of this chapter or a permit issued in accordance with this chapter,
or any federal or state regulations applicable to the requirements of this chapter,
the director can order the activity stopped upon service of written notice upon the
responsible party. The responsible party shall immediately stop all activity until
authorized in writing by the city to proceed. If the responsible party is not on the
site or cannot be located, the notice to stop work shall be posted in a conspicuous
place upon the area where the activity is occurring and shall state the nature of
the violation. It shall be unlawful for any responsible party to fail to comply with
a stop-work order. Failure to comply shall subject the responsible party to any and
all enforcement actions as outlined in this chapter.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.190), 1-14-2020)
A.
Any person who has violated or is violating any of the provisions of this chapter
or of any permit issued under this chapter, or any federal or state regulations applicable
to the requirements of this chapter, shall be subject to a written notice of violation
or an administrative order, or both. The notice of violation shall state the nature
of the violation and the administrative order shall require that the cause of the
violations be corrected and compliance with requirements of this chapter be obtained
within a reasonable time, as specified in the administrative order, and that the violation
may be subject to abatement as set forth in this chapter. The notice and administrative
order shall be sent via regular mail to the last known address of the responsible
party or by personal service. The person receiving the notice or order may appeal
the notice of violation and administrative order to the director within ten days following
issuance of the administrative order. Upon appeal, the director may modify the administrator
order to include a revised schedule to correct the cause of the violation, if the
director finds that just cause exists for such modification.
B.
Should the person not agree with the director's determination on a notice of violation
and/or administrative order that does not contain civil penalties, the party may appeal
the director's determination in writing within ten days of such determination. Any
notice of appeal shall be submitted to the director and a copy thereof to the office
of the city attorney. The director shall appoint a hearing officer to hear and determine
the matters subject to appeal and determination by this chapter. The hearing officer
shall not be an employee of the department of public works and natural resources.
The hearing shall be conducted in compliance with chapter 2.98 . An appeal of a notice of violation and/or administrative order that contains civil
penalties shall follow the procedures of section 14.26.210.B through E.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.200), 1-14-2020)
It is unlawful and an offense for any person to violate this chapter or any of the
provisions of a permit issued under this chapter, or to cause or allow such a violation.
Each day or part of a day any violation occurs or continues is a separate offense.
Violations of this chapter or any of the provisions of a permit issued under this
chapter are subject to a civil penalty of up to $500.00 per day, per violation, except
for civil penalties assessed for abatement of a violation, which shall be in the amount
of the cost of the abatement as set forth in this chapter. In addition, any costs
incurred by the city, including reasonable attorneys' fees, due to violations subject
to civil penalty, shall be added to the total amount of the civil penalty assessment.
Nothing in this section shall prevent the city from acting or seeking relief under
any other sections of this chapter for any violations of this chapter or any permit
issued under it.
A.
Should the violations stated in a notice of violation and administrative order issued
pursuant to this chapter be subject to civil penalty, the director shall send notice
of the corresponding civil penalty to the responsible person via regular mail to the
last known address of the owner or via personal service. The notice shall describe
the nature of the violation and clearly state the amount of the civil penalty along
with the due date and possible consequences of nonpayment in accordance with this
chapter.
B.
Any person who is the recipient of a notice of civil penalty assessment pursuant to
this chapter may appeal the notice in writing to the director on or before the expiration
of ten days following the date of the notice. Upon receipt of the appeal, the director
will meet with the person and allow the person to present evidence or reasons why
the person believes that the violations are not subject to civil penalty or present
reasons for reductions in the civil penalty amount. Based on the evidence and reasons,
the director may reduce the amount of civil penalty, require additional corrective
actions of the party, or take other administrative actions as deemed necessary.
C.
Should the person not agree with the director's determination, the party may appeal
in writing within ten days of such determination. Any notice of appeal shall be submitted
to the director and a copy thereof to the office of the city attorney. The director
shall appoint a hearing officer to hear and determine the matters subject to appeal
and determination by this chapter. The hearing officer shall not be an employee of
the department of public works and natural resources. The hearing shall be conducted
in compliance with chapter 2.98 .
D.
Civil penalties not paid and received by the city within 30 days of the latter of
the issuance of the notice of civil penalty, the director's determination pursuant
to subsection B of this section, or the hearing officer's determination pursuant to
subsection C of this section, shall be deemed delinquent, except that civil penalties
issued for the cost of abatement shall be delinquent if not paid within ten days of
the notice of civil penalty issued pursuant to section 14.36.180.G or the hearing
officer's decision, if appealed pursuant to section 14.26.180.D. Delinquent civil
penalties are subject to delinquency charges of 1½ percent per month, compounded quarterly.
E.
Before the filing of a civil action to collect one or more delinquent penalty assessments,
the director shall send a notice to the responsible person via regular mail or by
personal service, which shall advise the person of the nature of the violation which
resulted in a civil penalty, the dates on which violations occurred, the original
due date of the penalty assessment and the amount of the penalty, including any delinquency
charges. The notice shall further advise the person that unless payment of all assessments
is made to the city within ten days of the date of the notice, the city will take
whatever actions are necessary to collect the assessments.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.210), 1-14-2020)
Any person violating this chapter shall be liable for any expense, loss or damage
caused to the city or the MS4 by reason of such violation, including administrative,
operation and maintenance expenses incurred by the city. If a person discharges pollutants
that cause the United States Environmental Protection Agency or the state to fine
the city for violating any condition of its federal or state permit, the person shall
indemnify the city for the total cost of the fine, including, without limitation,
all legal, sampling, analytical, and other associated costs and expenses.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.220), 1-14-2020)
The fees and charges in this section shall apply to administration of the stormwater
permit requirements in this chapter.
A.
The following fees for application, site plan review, and inspection shall apply to
stormwater construction activity permits:
1.
An application fee of $100.00. This fee shall also apply to the renewal or re-issuance
of any permit that has expired, or been revoked or suspended, to any transfers or
reassignments of a permit, and to new application forms submitted pursuant to section 14.26.130 .
2.
A site plan review fee of $500.00. This fee shall also apply to the review of any
site plan modifications that are associated with new or revised development plans
that are required by title 15 .
3.
An inspection fee of $1,500.00. This fee shall also apply to the re-issuance of any
permit that has expired or been revoked or suspended.
4.
A reduced inspection fee of $500.00 for the renewal of a permit that no longer has
construction activity occurring on the site and is pending final stabilization.
B.
The following fees for application, site plan review, and inspection shall apply to
permanent stormwater control permits:
1.
An application fee of $100.00. This fee shall also apply to the renewal or re-issuance of any permit that has expired
or been revoked or suspended and to any transfers or reassignments of a permit.
2.
A site plan review fee of $500.00. This fee shall also apply to the review of any site plan modifications that are associated
with new or revised development plans that are required by this Code.
3.
An inspection fee of $400.00. This fee shall also apply to the re-issuance of any permit that has expired, or been
revoked or suspended.
( Ord. No. O-2020-01 , § 1(14.26.230), 1-14-2020)
The director of the Longmont Public Library shall set the schedule of fines and fees
for overdue material and services provided by the Longmont Public Library upon approval
of the city manager. The director shall assess those fines and fees for overdue material
and services provided by the Longmont Public Library, payable by the library card
holder or the person for whom the library renders services to the director or an appointed
representative. The director may designate up to seven days of amnesty per calendar
year, when patrons may return overdue books in suitable condition, without paying
accumulated fines. The director may assess fines and fees for all circulating materials,
including leased books, reference books, replacement cards, past due books, damaged
or lost books, microfilm prints, and such. The director may also assess service charges,
fees or rental payments as required by other agencies providing services to the Longmont
Public Library.
(Code 1993, § 14.28.010; Code 2009, § 14.28.010; Ord. No. O-88-13, § 1; Ord. No. O-94-61,
§ 9)
It is unlawful for any patron to hold circulating materials, including, but not limited
to, material checked out belonging or loaned to the Longmont Public Library, such
as children's and adult's books, reference material, magazines, pamphlets, films,
art prints, records and cassettes in excess of five weeks past due.
(Code 1993, § 14.28.020; Code 2009, § 14.28.020; Ord. No. O-88-13, § 1)
The library card holder is responsible for any fines and fees provided in this chapter.
The card holder shall also be the responsible party in any suit brought by the city
in the city municipal court. Nothing in this chapter shall bar any other civil or
criminal remedy for abuse of library privileges or conversion of materials.
(Code 1993, § 14.28.030; Code 2009, § 14.28.030; Ord. No. O-88-13, § 1; Ord. No. O-94-61,
§ 9)
In addition to city enforcement of fines or costs assessed a patron, until all fines,
replacement or repair costs have been paid, the checkout privileges of library patrons
may be suspended upon written notice being mailed to the last known address provided
the library by the patron. An aggrieved patron may appeal, in writing, such suspension
of privileges, within ten days of such notice, to the director of the Longmont Public
Library, and the director shall set and conduct a hearing in the matter within five
days of receipt of such appeal. The suspension shall, in any event, be removed upon
payment of the monies assessed.
(Code 1993, § 14.28.040; Code 2009, § 14.28.040; Ord. No. O-88-13, § 1)
Visitor's cards may be issued upon a cash deposit with the library in an amount determined
by the director of the library. The deposit will be refunded when the card is surrendered
clear of outstanding books or fines.
(Code 1993, § 14.28.050; Code 2009, § 14.28.050; Ord. No. O-88-13, § 1)
The violation of section 14.28.020 is an offense, punishable according to chapter 1.12 .
(Code 1993, § 14.28.060; Code 2009, § 14.28.060; Ord. No. O-94-61, § 9; Ord. No. O-88-13,
§ 1)
Editor's note— Ord. No. O-2024-83 , § 2, adopted Dec. 17, 2024, amended Ch. 14.32 in its entirety, by amending, deleting
and/or combining former sections to read as herein set out. Former Ch. 14.32, §§ 14.32.010—14.32.240,
pertained to similar subject matter. See the Code Comparative Table for full derivative
history.
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter shall have the meanings
