Recreation means activity done for enjoyment or health and is to be liberally construed.
Recreation building means a building or a part of a building designed for public recreation purposes
that has an estimated useful life of five years or longer and that is required by
policy of the council adopted by resolution or ordinance, such as the budget. Examples
could potentially include, but are not limited to, the St. Vrain Memorial Building,
the Longmont Recreation Center, the Longmont Senior Center, the Longmont Youth Center,
the Centennial Pool, an aquatic center, and expansions for recreation purposes of
existing buildings.
(Code 2009, § 14.46.010; Ord. No. O-2016-27, § 1, 4-26-2016; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018)
A.
The intent of this chapter is to charge an impact fee to applicants for residential
development in the city to fund capital improvements needed to address demand for
recreation buildings attributable to new residential development. This recreation
buildings impact fee's methodology is based largely on Impact Fee Study, City of Longmont,
Colorado (2016), prepared for the city by the TischlerBise Consultancy. The council
intends that the impact fees collected under this chapter will fund expenditures for
capital facilities attributable to new development.
B.
The council finds that the study and this chapter define classifications that are
generally applicable to broad classes of property, quantifies the reasonable impact
of proposed development on capital facilities, and establishes charges at a level
no greater than necessary to defray such impacts directly related to proposed development.
This fee will not be used to remedy any deficiency in capital facilities that exists
without regard to proposed development.
(Code 2009, § 14.46.020; Ord. No. O-2016-27, § 1, 4-26-2016)
The recreation buildings impact fee is imposed on new and expanded dwelling units
at the time a certificate of occupancy is issued for each unit. The director of planning
and development services, or designee, is authorized and directed to collect the fee
established by this chapter at the time a certificate of occupancy has been approved
for each dwelling unit.
(Code 2009, § 14.46.030; Ord. No. O-2016-27, § 1, 4-26-2016; Ord. No. O-2019-18 , § 6, 3-19-2019)
A.
Fee schedule. By square footage of each dwelling unit, the recreation buildings impact fee is as
follows:

Size of Dwelling Unit (rounded to the nearest square foot) | Fee
800 or less | $582.58
801 to 1,600 | $940.26
1,601 to 2,400 | $1,142.66
2,401 to 3,200 | $1,257.20
3,201 to 4,000 | $1,398.61
4,001 or more | $1,489.63

B.
Indexing. Starting in 2017, the fee shall be automatically adjusted annually based on the changes
in construction costs as determined by building valuation data as published in the
building standards magazine.
C.
Dwelling unit enlargements. The fee for the enlargement of a dwelling unit, or replacement of a dwelling unit
with a larger dwelling unit, shall be the difference between: (a) the fee for the
enlarged dwelling unit based on square footage; and (b) the fee that would apply to
the pre-existing dwelling unit based on square footage if that fee were to be charged
at the time of the enlargement.
(Code 2009, § 14.46.040; Ord. No. O-2016-27, § 1, 4-26-2016)
A.
Credits. In the event that any owner dedicates or conveys to the city a portion of any development
for recreation building purposes, other than as streets, alleys, drainage, utility
easements or such other infrastructure required to serve the development, or as required
by section 15.02.100 or a public improvement agreement thereunder, section 15.02.060.A.4 or an annexation
agreement thereunder, or another provision of the Code, the city council may by resolution
reduce the recreation buildings impact fee by the value of the land dedicated or conveyed.
Payments and dedications for recreation building purposes required by such other sections
of the Code address only site-specific impacts of development, whereas this recreation
buildings impact fee defrays only system-wide impacts.
B.
Affordable housing waiver. The planning and development services director may waive the applicable recreation
buildings impact fee on the development of low- or moderate-income housing, under
the process described in chapter 4.79 .
C.
Replacement. Fees shall not be assessed under this chapter for replacement of a building containing
one or more dwelling units with a comparable new building which would not be assessed
a higher fee under this chapter than the pre-existing dwelling unit would be assessed
at the time of the replacement.
D.
No refunds. Any request for a reduced or waived fee under this section must be made at or before
the time of certificate of occupancy. Fees shall not be refunded after they are paid.
(Code 2009, § 14.46.050; Ord. No. O-2016-27, § 1, 4-26-2016; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018; Ord. No. O-2019-18 , § 7, 3-19-2019)
The city parks and recreation advisory board shall review all aspects of the allocation
and sufficiency of the recreation buildings impact fee established in this chapter
and submit its findings and recommendations to city council at least every five years
after adoption of the fee. The council shall make any necessary adjustments by ordinance.
(Code 2009, § 14.46.060; Ord. No. O-2016-27, § 1, 4-26-2016)
All monies collected according to this chapter shall be placed in a separate fund
known as the recreation buildings fund, and shall be disbursed only to pay for the
purchase of land or the cost of engineering, design, permitting, or construction of
new recreation buildings or capital improvements to existing recreation buildings
necessitated by new residential development. The city shall not use the recreation
buildings fund for periodic or routine maintenance.
(Code 2009, § 14.46.070; Ord. No. O-2016-27, § 1, 4-26-2016)
These fees apply to the provision of broadband services to residential and commercial
customers using the city-owned fiber optic backbone and related infrastructure, including
any fiber laterals necessary to provide the service. The fees shall produce revenues
at least sufficient to pay the cost of service to provide the broadband services,
including, but not limited to, all of the costs of operation and maintenance of the
facilities.
(Code 2009, § 14.48.010; Ord. No. O-2013-15, § 2, 5-7-2013)
The director of the city power and communication (director) may offer discounted broadband
service fees under section 14.48.010 to the following:
1.
Low-income families with school age children. For the purposes of this chapter, the term "low-income families" means families with
school age children under the age of 19 years old and have household income not exceeding
80 percent area median income.
2.
Low-income seniors and disabled individuals. This discount shall be available to any residential customer qualifying for tax or
rental relief under provisions set forth in the state department of revenue form 104-PTC.
Applications for the discount shall be made annually on forms provided by the city
and shall include a copy of form 104-PTC, as filed with the state department of revenue.
(Code 2009, § 14.48.020; Ord. No. O-2013-15, § 2, 5-7-2013)
A.
Applicability. The director is authorized to enter into other contracts for the use of the city's
telecommunications system, including, but not limited to, dark fiber licenses, conduit
licenses, and collocation agreements.
B.
Fees. The fees shall produce revenues at least sufficient to pay the cost of service to
provide, operate and maintain the facility or service.
(Code 2009, § 14.48.030; Ord. No. O-2013-15, § 2, 5-7-2013)
The director may adopt such rules and regulations necessary to administer this chapter
and consistent with its purpose, including, but not limited to, setting fees in accordance
with this chapter.
(Code 2009, § 14.48.040; Ord. No. O-2013-15, § 2, 5-7-2013)
The city will provide by electronic means, first class U.S. mail or otherwise a written
notice of proposed or completed discontinuance of service for actual or apparent violation
of the broadband terms and conditions as then currently written. The notice will advise
the broadband customer the reason for discontinuance of broadband service, and, if
applicable, the amount past due and the date by which the past due amount must be
paid in order to avoid discontinuance of service. For the purposes of this chapter,
the term "amount past due" means any sum overdue for or charge related to broadband
service provided by the city.
(Code 2009, § 14.48.050; Ord. No. O-2014-67, § 4, 11-10-2014)
A.
Subject to appropriation of funds by city council, the city manager or designee shall
have the authority to offer rebates or incentives for products and services that reduce
the consumption of electricity or water by city utility customers. The city manager
or designee shall promulgate rules governing rebate or incentive programs, which shall
include, but not be limited to, the product or service for which the rebate or incentive
is offered, the method of rebate or incentive, amount of rebate or incentive, duration
of the rebate or incentive program and if it includes single-family residential, multifamily
residential, commercial or industrial customers.
B.
Such programs may include, but are not limited to, appliances, lighting, heating,
ventilation, air conditioning, building weatherization, "green" building, motors,
manufacturing equipment, controls, qualified renewable energy systems, plumbing fixtures,
irrigation systems, soil amendments, xeriscape planting materials, technologies and
audits that reduce electric or water consumption.
(Code 2009, § 14.49.010; Ord. No. O-2008-80, § 2)
The provisions of this chapter are intended to impose a special service fee for the
maintenance, operation, repair, and renewal of city-owned parks, greenways and pools
in an amount calculated as shown herein. The fee will be used to support maintenance,
operations, repair and replacement of playgrounds, sport courts, sport fields, pool
facilities, park and greenway buildings and facilities, including restrooms, shelters
and pump stations, and landscaping located in city parks and greenways. General maintenance
services include cleaning, inspections, snow removal, turf and vegetation management,
irrigation servicing and minor repairs, including minor repairs to buildings and facilities.
Larger repairs and replacements for parks, greenways and pools are identified in the
city's capital improvement plan. Additionally, the fee will be used to repair and
replace park, greenway and pool facilities and amenities damaged in the 2013 Longmont
flood. Said fee shall not be used to collect more than is necessary to fund such maintenance
and services. Ensuring that the city's parks, greenways and pools remain clean and
safe for all scheduled and unscheduled activities is critical to safeguard the health,
safety, and welfare of parks and greenways users.
(Code 2009, § 14.50.010; Ord. No. O-2009-68, § 1, 10-27-2009; Ord. No. O-2013-64,
§ 1, 11-12-2013)
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter shall have the meanings
ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates a different
meaning:
A.
Feepayer means each owner or utility billing customer who is obligated to pay a utility bill
pursuant to chapter 14.20 of the Longmont Municipal Code.
B.
Finance director means the chief financial officer, or his or her designee.
(Code 2009, § 14.50.020; Ord. No. O-2009-68, § 1, 10-27-2009; Ord. No. O-2013-64,
§ 1, 11-12-2013; Ord. No. O-2021-73 , § 7, 12-14-2021)
A.
There is hereby established a park and greenway maintenance fee which shall be assessed
and charged monthly, pursuant to the provisions of this chapter, to the feepayer for
the purpose of funding the maintenance, operation, repair, and renewal of parks, greenways
and pools.
B.
The amount of the fee shall be $2.00 per unit per month. Said amount represents the
portion of total park and greenway maintenance, repair, operation and renewal costs
to be borne by the fee, as determined by the city council, divided by the total number
of feepayer accounts within the city.
C.
The following feepayer utility accounts are exempt from the monthly park and greenway
maintenance fee: sprinkler control boxes; utility boxes; telecommunications towers;
and the electricity account for a nonresident garage accessory to a single-family
home when the home and garage accounts are in the name of the same customer.
(Code 2009, § 14.50.030; Ord. No. O-2009-68, § 1, 10-27-2009; Ord. No. O-2013-64,
§ 1, 11-12-2013; Ord. No. O-2021-73 , § 8, 12-14-2021)
A.
The fee shall be billed, collected, and enforced as part of the utility bill pursuant
to chapter 14.20 regarding utility accounts, billing, and collections.
B.
The revenue from the park and greenway maintenance fee shall be deposited in a separate
fund known as the park greenway maintenance fund, and shall be used only for the purposes
set forth in section 14.50.010 .
(Code 2009, § 14.50.040; Ord. No. O-2009-68, § 1, 10-27-2009; Ord. No. O-2013-64,
§ 1, 11-12-2013)
Please see chapter 4.08 for all rebate information.
(Code 2009, § 14.50.050; Ord. No. O-2009-68, § 1, 10-27-2009; Ord. No. O-2013-64,
§ 1, 11-12-2013; Ord. No. O-2019-23 , § 1, 3-19-2019)
A.
Unless specific provisions of a feepayer's written service contract provide otherwise,
a feepayer that disagrees with a decision of the city concerning billing or rebate
pursuant to this chapter may request a hearing before a hearing officer during which
the feepayer will have the opportunity to challenge the decision of the city and to
present any relevant facts regarding matters in dispute. The appeal shall be conducted
pursuant to chapter 2.98 of this Code.
B.
If the feepayer requests a hearing within 30 days of the billing date on which the
disputed charges first appeared the city will not, except as authorized by section
14.20.150.F, disconnect service for nonpayment of any amount under dispute until the
hearing officer decides the dispute.
(Code 2009, § 14.50.060; Ord. No. O-2009-68, § 1, 10-27-2009; Ord. No. O-2013-64,
§ 1, 11-12-2013; Ord. No. O-2021-73 , § 9, 12-14-2021)
The parks and maintenance fee established at section 14.050.030 shall continue as
stated in section 14.50.030 unless extended by a legislative act of the city council.
(Code 2009, § 14.50.070; Ord. No. O-2009-68, § 1, 10-27-2009; Ord. No. O-2012-66,
§ 1, 10-23-2012; Ord. No. O-2013-64, § 1, 11-12-2013)
The city council finds:
A.
Since the inception of the open space program, the city has acquired 2,006 acres of
land by purchase, lease, and the use of conservation easements.
B.
As set forth in section 4.04.130.I, open space shall meet one or more of the following
criteria or goals:
1.
Preservation of natural areas, wildlife habitat, wetlands, agriculture and visual
corridors;
2.
Link and trails, access to public lakes, streams, and other usable open space lands,
stream corridors and scenic corridors along existing highways;
3.
Conservation of natural resources, including, but not limited to, forest lands, range
lands, agricultural land, aquifer recharge areas, and surface water;
4.
District parks devoted to low-impact recreational uses;
5.
Implementing greenways and open space policies or strategies of the city area comprehensive
plan;
6.
Urban shaping buffers between or around municipalities or community service areas
and buffer zones between residential and nonresidential development (goals).
C.
There may be instances when these goals may be furthered through an exchange of interest
in city land for property that has a higher open space program value, or by sale of
a parcel having limited open space value to another landowner or a city fund, and
utilizing the proceeds to obtain land that more clearly meets the goal without expending
additional limited open space funds.
D.
It is in the interest of the city that the council retain the ability to take advantage
of opportunities to expand the open space property inventory and the quality of land
preserved.
(Code 2009, § 14.52.010; Ord. No. O-2011-10, § 1, 2-8-2011)
The purpose of this chapter is to give the city the flexibility to dispose of open
space purchased or otherwise obtained pursuant to the provisions of section 4.04.130 , where such action will result in the city obtaining greater acreage or land that
more directly meets the purposes of that section.
(Code 2009, § 14.52.020; Ord. No. O-2011-10, § 1, 2-8-2011)
A.
Disposal of open space. Disposal of open space should be for a public purpose as determined by city council.
1.
The term "public purpose" means and includes, but is not limited to, use of the property
for right-of-way and easements for city roads, utilities, and life-essential infrastructure,
and the sale or exchange of property interests that result in a net benefit to the
open space program.
2.
The disposal should be sensitive to the integrity of the property features, including
sensitive habitat and cultural and historical features, and generally should not adversely
affect the remainder of the property.
B.
Parks and recreation advisory board recommendation.
1.
Before taking final action on any request to dispose of open space land, the city
council shall refer the request to the parks and recreation advisory board (PRAB),
and other advisory boards the city council feels necessary, for their recommendations.
2.
The PRAB shall schedule a public hearing on the request no later than 60 days from
the date of the referral. Notice of the public hearing and request for comments on
the request shall be published on the city website and in a newspaper of general circulation
following city council referral.
3.
All comments received from the public shall be included in the material provided to
the PRAB prior to its public hearing.
4.
The PRAB shall forward its recommendations to the city council following the public
hearing.
C.
Compensation.
1.
Sale or transfer.
a.
The sales price of any open space land to another landowner or the transfer cost to
another city department shall be based on the fair market value of the property interest
conveyed as determined by the city council. Such sales price or transfer cost must
be for an amount equal to or greater than the original purchase price unless the city
council determines that a bona fide public purpose is served by conveying the property,
or a portion or interest thereof, for a sum less than the original purchase price,
based upon a finding that the sale or exchange of the property interest will promote
the environment, health, safety, or general welfare of the residents of the city and
will result in a net benefit to the open space program by conserving the original
open space goals intended by the initial acquisition of the property; and
b.
The proceeds of any sale or transfer of open space land shall be placed in the open
space fund.
2.
Land exchange. The city council may approve an exchange of existing open space land for other property
if it determines that the value of any land to be obtained in the exchange is of equivalent
value, taking into account the relative size of both parcels, the ecological value
of the parcels, and whether the obtained parcel would better meet the purposes of
the open space program than the current parcel.
D.
Majority required for transfer of open space. The transfer of open space land to another city department shall be by motion approved
by majority vote of the city council. Any conveyance of title to open space land must
be made by ordinance approved by a two-thirds majority vote of city council pursuant
to section 13.2 of the Charter.
(Code 2009, § 14.52.030; Ord. No. O-2011-10, § 1, 2-8-2011; Ord. No. O-2020-29 , § 2, 8-11-2020)
